# Outstanding work

Open items, grouped by area. [Limitations](./limitations.md) describes current behaviour for users. This page lists what is still to be done and what data each item needs.

Windows reference images come from `scripts/gdi-fixtures`. The **Windows fixtures** workflow (`.github/workflows/windows-fixtures.yml`) runs those scripts on a GitHub-hosted Windows runner and pushes the output to a `windows-fixtures/<run_id>` branch. See `scripts/gdi-fixtures/README.md`.

The [12 October 2026 native parity review](./native-parity-review.md) records the newer baseline, portable extent-scaling and bilinear/texture fixes, and the assessment of Rust/open-source alternatives. The round-by-round measurements below retain their original dates.

Independent fractional RoundRect translation controls cover 576 paths in both arc directions; every native translation and every replay path is exact.

## Current parity priorities (8 October 2026)

The full suite passes: 202 files, 6,777 tests (8 October 2026, after round 6 with its eight workstreams and the reinstated ClearType dropout control; 186 files and 6,328 tests after round 5). Passing the existing bounds does not mean pixel-exact playback. `bun scripts/gdi-fixtures/report.ts` uses available Windows fixture fonts, reports both zero-tolerance and >8-level RGB differences, includes overlap edges, and prints rendered/reference dimensions. On this machine (report regenerated on 8 October 2026 on the head that includes rounds 1 to 6; at the end of round 5 509 of 737 were exact and 503 of 737 had complete ink coverage, 420 of 625 and 417 of 625 at the end of round 4, 396 of 614 and 394 of 614 at the start of round 4; the comparison count grew by 112 with the round-5 sheets and by 132 with the mirrored-map sheets of round 6), 637 of 869 comparisons are RGB-exact over their common device area, with no render failures (round 6: the 132 mirrored-map sheets added 111 exact ones, and 15 `compat-playback-*` sheets whose arcs were wrong became exact); 634 of 869 also have complete RGB ink coverage (white margins ignored, alpha checked separately only where individual tests require it). No comparison omits reference ink since round 6 (the four `codec-jpeg-*-playback` comparisons, `12bit`, `arithmetic`, `arith-prog-420-dri2` and `cmyk-photo`, used to: their 280x180 render was smaller than the 300x200 reference, 9,600 pixels each; the 9,600 pixels were the probe's own magenta surface, see References), and 13 contain rendered ink beyond the captured reference (18 at the end of round 5) (the nine clipped references of References, the two regenerated raster-face captures `textx-fon-system.regen` and `textx-fon-fixedsys.regen`, whose output equals their own `.regen.wide` captures, `src/gdi-text-regeneration.fixture.test.ts`, the two mirrored sheets `compat-playback-mx-r34-geo5` and `compat-playback-mxy-r34-geo5`, which paint 4 pixels past their 420 x 300 capture (the same unverified-extent case: no native playback into a larger surface was captured for them, so the extra ink is unverified rather than known to be wrong), and, before round 6, seven `compat-playback-*` sheets, the `geosq9` sheets of the identity, 32x11, 34 and 43 maps and the `wide7` sheets of the identity, 32x11 and 34 maps, where our output is 1 to 90 pixels larger than the 420 x 300 capture and 16 to 260 inked pixels fell outside it; round 6 played the seven natively into larger surfaces: the ink beyond the capture is at negative device coordinates (the converter canvas origins are -1 to -8 in x and -5 to -53 in y), Windows paints exactly those pixels and no others, the original overlap is reproduced pixel for pixel, and the differences over the full area stay within the ceilings pinned in `playback-extents.json` (0, 0, 0, 2, 7, 55 and 6 pixels for the seven sheets; the arc fix of round 6 has since lowered the sheets' own residuals in `src/emf-compat-playback.fixture.test.ts` to 14 pixels at most), so the extra ink was right and the 420 x 300 captures were the clipped side). Expanded native playback covers 101 formerly uncaptured extents and reproduces every original overlap pixel (`src/gdi-playback-extents.fixture.test.ts`); 71 of the 101 full-area controls are exact and the others differ chiefly in text. The remaining 9 captures did not reproduce the original reference; their native playback is committed as `.wide.png` and shows the converter extent is right. This is a local baseline, not a claim of full-image or cross-environment parity: references are composited over white, alpha is not compared independently, dimensions can differ, and old font captures have incomplete provenance.

Work in this order, using native captures and tighter regression bounds for every confirmed fix:

Another 576 signed native outlines and 2,304 signed GDI+ images isolate projection, original-distance scaling and movement arithmetic; 436 outlines and 1,344 signed images are currently exact with individual bounds. The candidate closes all signed outlines but increases four formerly inexact signed image residuals and still loses fifteen prior exact real-font controls, so it remains excluded from production. The fifteen are one glyph (`src/ttf-diagonal-projection.fixture.test.ts`): Arial 16 `y` under TextRenderingHintAntiAlias, fifteen origins that the two contrasts double to 30 captures, and the same candidate gains 64 captures of Times New Roman Italic 22 `a` (both AntiAlias and AntiAliasGridFit); over the 3,328 + 2 x 1,536 grayscale captures nothing else changes. The freedom-vector half of the candidate (`fDotP = 16384` when the vectors are equal) changes no capture, so the whole effect is the per-product projection. The first divergence in Arial `y` is an MDRP[0xC4] at byte 565 projecting the difference (142, -475) on (-15513, -5270): combined rounding gives 18, per-product 19, and native keeps 18 (the GDI diagnostic font `opcode-mdrp`, built from exactly these vectors and operands, follows per-product arithmetic through GetGlyphOutline, so classic GDI and GDI+ AntiAlias disagree on identical operands). Enumerating every assignment of the four (Arial) and four (Times New Roman Italic) disagreeing projections of the two glyphs and keeping the assignments that reproduce the native image determines three of them: Arial's MDRP project (142, -475) must be combined-rounded; Times New Roman Italic's MIRP dualProject (95, -52) and MDRP project (-16, -113) must be per-product rounded. The condition is therefore not the opcode (two MDRPs), not project against dualProject, not equal projection and freedom vectors (the Italic MDRP has them equal, Arial's does not, but the Italic MIRP's dual vector also differs from its freedom vector), and not a different rounding of the sum: truncating the exact sum fits all three determined projections but loses 576 closed captures, flooring it 574 (and gains 32), and rounding the exact sum to nearest, which is the production arithmetic, changes nothing. The two leads this left were followed in round 5 and neither separates the calls: the x coordinates of the v40 (ClearType-style) interpreter carrying more than 26.6 precision (unrounded x coordinates throughout, the crudest form, lose 2,819 and 4,349 captures), and GDI+ captures of the private diagnostic fonts through DrawDriverString with the AntiAlias hint (`text-opcode-coverage` and `text-vector-stage-coverage`, 3,072 and 2,560 images), because the references themselves change between runs. Round 5 (V1) closed this item as not reproducible by a stateless converter: no arithmetic condition separates the calls, and the exclusion tracks a native state that changes between runs (see Text, "Grayscale hinting and scan conversion"). **Round 8 (R2) closed:** GDI+ grayscale text answers GETINFO as gray ClearType (15,747 to 17,113 of the 31,584 `text-real-glyphs` captures exact), AntiAliasGridFit spaces glyphs by GDI's own interpreter widths, default-format ClearTypeGridFit by the ClearType phantom-point advance rounded (1,230 of 1,230 whole-number glyphs), and the generic SHPIX helpers stop after IUP[y] (see Text, "GDI+ text (round 8, R2)"); `gpx-rec-textcontrast` 1,994 to 302 differing pixels, `textx-plus-antialiasgridfit` 513 to 14, `textx-plus-cleartype` 2,013 to 361. **Round 9 (W2) closed:** unhinted DrawString advances by design-unit-rounded tracked advances (`textx-plus-singlebit` 125 to 30), AntiAlias underline and strike-out bars at quarter-pixel positions (`textx-plus-antialias` 556 to 42), GDI+ ClearType blends with 8-bit tables and exact sixths (`gpx-text-texture-cleartype` 161 to 0, `gpx-rec-textcontrast-cleartype` 1,898 to 76, `textx-plus-cleartype` 361 to 26), and the recorded playback stretch of GM_COMPATIBLE text (`textpb-segoeui-cell-q3` 4,095 to 93); see "GDI+ text (round 9, W2)". **Remains:** diagonal stems of `W`, `k`, `y`, `v` (302 and 76 pixels of the two `gpx-rec-textcontrast` sheets, about 220 of the brushed text fixtures, 42 of `textx-plus-antialias`), the one-row bar's sub-scanline ends, glyph shapes of Times New Roman's unhinted monochrome serifs, and the stretch of faces other than Segoe UI. **Round 9 (W1) measured, implementation withheld:** why the diagonal letters differ under GDI's compatible-width ClearType (stems are shifted as the program runs; nothing is shifted after IUP[x]); the two candidate commits take `textx-arial-cleartype`/q0/q1/q2 from 7,127 to 6,036 differing pixels but make 7 of 1,260 exact captures inexact (see Text, "GDI ClearType past the stem (round 9, W1)"). |

Scaled EMF inside-frame is exact (`emf-scaled-paths.json.gz`: every shape matches in both arc directions); scaled WMF shapes are exact (`wmf-scaled-paths.json`: 1,499 of 1,500 native paths match exactly; the other, a chord with a pen as wide as its box, is a FIX off at four values; a pen wider than an Ellipse or RoundRect keeps the box without the inset). Continue with half-FIX side/corner ties and arc geometry. Closed since this line was first written: the rotated GDI pen (round 6: the nib, the half-pixel perpendicular and the square-cap extension under rotated, sheared, scaled and mirrored matrices are exact, 6,432 of 6,432 flat-cap corners of `rotated-pen-vectors.json.gz`, 864 of 864 nibs read from long round-capped segments and all 504 outlines of the seven non-trivial matrices of `general-matrix-pen.json.gz`; the rules are in "Round 6: the rotated GDI pen" under GDI path geometry), and miter/bevel joins under such matrices (round 5: widened in device space with the matrix's nib, 221 of 288 native outlines exact against 42; round 6: all 288, 270 with the nib and the rest once the miter limit was tested in logical units, `general-matrix-pen-joins.json.gz`, `rotated-pen-miters.json.gz`), and EMF+ inset and compound pens on closed figures (round 5: compound arrays exact on 24 figures in both modes, Inset pens ring-shaped, aliased 52, 119 and 458 differing pixels of 240,000 for miter, bevel and round joins against 1,582, 1,581 and 1,976; round 7: the aliased sheets are exact and the antialiased ones differed by 6 pixels and are exact since round 8, see EMF+ below). |

Completion requires exact full-output comparisons (including alpha and painted pixels outside the current overlap), matching native environments, independent scale/transform captures, and PNG/SVG verification. Existing tolerance bounds must not be increased to make a candidate fix pass.

Closed in this pass: native axis-aligned Bicubic arithmetic makes the PNG fixture RGB-exact across all 16,000 pixels, with all 144 arithmetic controls exact and no per-case regressions across 64 independent crop/mirror and 384 independent phase controls. Native pen playback now names a pixel source rectangle, and the generator disables process DPI virtualisation; the six anchor-cap and two scale3-dash references now match exactly. PolyTextOut now preserves C1 glyphs as native metafile playback does, reducing its recorded fixture from 39 differing pixels to 12; eight focused controls pin both record families and IGNORELANGUAGE. EMF+ coverage masks retain underline/strike-out bars, use GDI+'s 16-shade mapping, and run its grayscale subpixel interpreter. ClearType uses natural-width interpreter flags and truncates subpixel coverage. Ninety-four expanded playback references (eighty-eight when this paragraph was first written; 101 after round 6) now validate ink beyond the old native surfaces without replacing the original PNGs. Solid GDI pens under unequal axis scales now use native elliptical geometry: all 216 image controls are exact, and all 3,600 widened outlines and a further 16,128 independent direction/corner controls are exact. Path-gradient boundaries share the fixed-point flattener and match all 1,556 native Flatten vertices; gradient contour and scanline corrections tighten twenty-one PNG/SVG focus controls to a one-level channel bound. Fractional text origins close 2,400 grayscale and 288 ClearType controls; nearest-quarter grayscale x placement closes another 1,280 independent fine-origin controls and 88 DrawString controls. The end-to-end unhinted antialias text fixture falls from 1,300 to 650 differing pixels. Native captures and environment manifests accompany these changes.

## EMF+ image effects

The 74 Dual effect fixtures (`plus-effect-*`) and 74 Only equivalents (`plus-only-effect-*`) are in the parity test. They have been regenerated on Windows with corrected metafile type constants. `src/emf-plus-image-effects.fixture.test.ts` also checks the effect algorithms against the effected bitmaps that GDI+ records into each fixture.

- **Not exact yet:**
  - Red-eye follows the measured sector/centroid model in float32 truncating arithmetic; the fixtures are exact. Left: a direct call after earlier calls of the same process (not reproducible by a stateless converter, which reproduces a fresh process) and areas with a far-off centroid in bright fields; see the detailed limitations and row 4 of Current parity priorities.
  - The rotated blur's effected bitmap is exact and its playback is within 0.010% (`plus-effect-blur-r3-rotate30`, two pixels over 8 levels; 0.22% before the blur's first halo column was sampled at the right edge). The 11 pixels left are not effect arithmetic: nine are one level in the rotated bilinear sampling, and two are edge pixels of the rotated image (the pixel at (45, 50) is backdrop in native playback and image in the converter, the one at (101, 81) the reverse, 0.69 pixel beyond the right edge in source coordinates), which belong to the rotated-draw edge coverage rather than the blur.
  - Red-eye residuals (`plus-only-effect-redeye-whole` 379 pixels, `-both` 59, `-left` 3) are closed (0/0/0), and were not process state: native playback of the recordings is the reference in every fresh process and order (`redeye-playback`), and the first direct call equals it (`redeye-fixture-direct`). The 282 + 57 pure-red ramp pixels and the 80 pixels of the (x, 255 - x, 128) band came from the centre of the far-off centroid (the darkness-weighted centroid, 127.641 to 127.644 by 30.564 to 30.567 on the ramps image, not the middle), and the 17 + 3 skin-tone pixels from a pixel exactly at the radius (the float32 truncation decides it).

Blur and sharpen are exact. Large blur reproduces the native per-axis algorithm (reduce each row by the factor measured at all 957 quarter radii, filter with the centre weight applied as two rounded halves, enlarge, then the same for columns, with products kept to 1/256 of a level), and the kernel weights are float32 values formed in truncating arithmetic (accumulated offset, ordered sum, multiply by the reciprocal normaliser; see `blurKernel`). They match native noise images at random radii from 1 to 255, ramps, impulses, the 957-radius and two-pixel-wide captures, the 504-draw expanded capture (fourteen rectangles, twelve radii, opaque/translucent patterns) and 150 narrow and edge-reaching draws, pixel for pixel. Expanded blur follows the native reduction (a cropped source rectangle reduces from its own corner, only its own samples are enlarged, samples beyond the buffer are transparent, a lone sample is continued toward a transparent neighbour).

Sharpen strength now uses the native rational amount curve, quantized to 1/64 with half-down rounding. It matches every integer amount 0–100 and 19 radii when tested independently of blur convolution.

- **JPEG coverage:** accurate integer IDCT, triangle chroma upsampling and fixed-point colour conversion now match the committed standalone JPEG and JPEG-TIFF references exactly. Arithmetic-coded JPEG is exact against the native reference, 12-bit JPEG is refused as Windows refuses it (nothing is drawn), and CMYK/YCCK JPEG, also inside TIFF, goes through a bundled copy of the Windows ICM node table with the module's own integer arithmetic (every channel of all eight CMYK/YCCK references and the TIFF strip file is exact, 30,000 held-out random inks are exact and 43 of 30,000,000 fresh ones differ by one level, mostly with a node beyond the gamut among the five; see limitations.md for the mechanism: ink position map, 1/4096 integer weights, `(S + 4096) >> 20`, 16-bit output table). Open: those last nodes (outside the gamut, at most one 8-bit level) and the 16-bit result of the interpolation (`cmykColour16`: 99.1% of 16-bit values exact, one or two off elsewhere, which only the probes use); the table grew from 196 KB to 275 KB of text (+4.1% of the bundle) because exact integer nodes cost two more bits each. Arithmetic JPEG is now verified beyond `testimgari.jpg`: 28 variants written by `scripts/gdi-fixtures/arith-jpeg-encoder.ts` (proved against Pillow/libjpeg-turbo with Huffman copies of the same coefficients, `arith-jpeg-verify.py`) are decoded by GDI+ and by the converter identically (23 exact; the 5 CMYK/YCCK ones equal their Huffman copies through the shared conversion), so progressive, restart-interval, DAC and 4-component arithmetic need no further data. Arithmetic JPEG with 12-bit precision, arithmetic lossless and hierarchical files are not tested (Windows refuses 12-bit; the others are not decoded). GIF and lossless TIFF references remain exact, including offset/palette GIFs, LZW/multipage/bilevel TIFF, Deflate strips and uncompressed/Deflate tiles.

## EMF+ rotated high-quality DrawImage

A rotated or sheared `HighQualityBicubic`/`HighQualityBilinear` `DrawImage` (a 3-point destination) is two native draws (round 3; probes `HighQualityRotatedProbe.cs`, `HighQualityRotatedFineProbe.cs`, `HighQualityHalfShiftProbe.cs`, `HighQualityAxisProbe.cs`, `HighQualityAxisNoiseProbe.cs`; modes `hq-rotated`, `hq-axis`, `hq-half-shift`; captures `hq-rotated`, `hq-rotated-fine`, `hq-axis`, `hq-axis-noise`, `hq-half-shift`; code `resampleRotatedTwoStage`, `resampleHqAxisAligned`). The two-impulse probe the task asked for separated the footprints at once: the response is not an area-integrated kernel over the rotated pixel, and not separable filtering along the source axes either; it is the source resampled to a whole-pixel intermediate and then rotated.

- **Rotated None draw = pre-scale + plain-kernel draw, byte for byte.** Drawing the source axis-aligned into a `W x H` bitmap with the same high-quality mode, then that bitmap rotated to the same three points with plain Bicubic (for HighQualityBicubic) or Bilinear, equals the one-step draw exactly in 115 of 120 bicubic and 85 of 120 bilinear captures (`twoDiff3`; the others differ by 1 to 24 bytes of 36,864). `W` and `H` are each edge's device length rounded up (`ceil(length * (1 - 1.5e-7))`: 12.0000029 rounds up, 12.000001, 24.0000019 and 40.0000038 do not, so the length is a float32 result); an edge within one pixel of the source length makes the draw skip the pre-scale and sample the source with the plain kernel (the old "unscaled axis" rule is this case; no intermediate size reproduces it). The old far-edge fade table and the half-pixel shift were artefacts of the missing pre-scale: the intermediate's edge fades through the second kernel's transparent taps.
- **The second pass is plain Bicubic with the axis-aligned integer arithmetic** (premultiplied bytes, 16.16 row stepper, 1/64 phase, weights in 16.16, truncating) on the 8-bit premultiplied intermediate; plain Bilinear is float-like with premultiplied bytes rounded (its interior is a level off on a rare pixel and the exact arithmetic is open). Applied to a directly requested rotated Bicubic it improves the independent captures' counts but regresses a few sums at scales below 1, so it is limited to this second pass.
- **Coverage** is the destination parallelogram scan-converted from its 28.4 corners by the aliased fill rule (as NearestNeighbor), on a transparent border two pixels wide; exactly-on-edge pixels at 45 degrees are excluded as native does.
- **PixelOffsetMode Half/HighQuality:** the same draw with the second pass's grid moved by -0.5 device pixels and by +0.5 *intermediate* texels along each of its own axes (not 0.5 device pixels: it matters when the length is not whole, as at 1.1x), and coverage tested at pixel centres. `hq-half-shift.json` (a search over destination translations of the None draw with the source rectangle moved by -0.5) finds the Half draw exactly, byte for byte, for axis-aligned draws and for unscaled rotated draws (destination moved by -0.5), but never for a scaled rotated one (the best translation leaves 1,674 to 20,326 summed levels over 32 draws), which is why the second pass carries the extra half-intermediate-texel term.
- **Almost axis-aligned quads** (far corners within 0.02 pixels over a 48-pixel edge, ratio about 4.2e-4; 0.03 pixels is rotated; 124 captures, whatever the origin's fraction) draw as axis-aligned ones.
- **Axis-aligned high-quality kernels (stage one) follow fixed-point phases:** step `S = round(65536 / scale)` per destination pixel, position `P = k S + offset` from the first covered pixel (offset `d S` along x, mirrored, `(1 - d) S` along y), weights from the phase `floor((P - 1) / 512)` (1/128 texel) evaluated at the bin centre for the cubic and at the bin top for the tent, the horizontal pass first and rounded to 8 bits before the vertical one. This took the axis-aligned arithmetic captures from 6 of 288 exact to 206 and the independent ones from 8 to 38 of 192 (`hq-arithmetic-bounds.json` tightened on 869 metrics). The tent is exact on 46,569 noise values; the cubic differs by one level on 35 (0.08%).

Results (`src/emf-plus-high-quality-rotated.fixture.test.ts`, 600 captures): `gpx-image-rotated-highqualitybicubic` 2,911 pixels (18.5%, up to 10 levels) to 10 pixels (0.064%, one level); the 20 upscaled draws of each group in the scale sweep went from 12,773 to 12,860 differing pixels (every pixel, up to 255 levels) to 54 (bicubic) and 191 (bilinear) under None, none beyond one level; no group has a pixel beyond 8 levels except the Half reductions (24 and 40 pixels, up to 31 levels). Bundle: `dist/index.mjs` 1,893,475 to 1,902,912 bytes.

Tried and rejected: refitting the area-integrated model (stage one alone leaves it 20 levels off at the edge); a rotated pixel footprint integral; separable filtering in the source axes (the two-impulse response is symmetric under the draw's own rotation); a Half translation of the None draw; numerical box integration of the cubic at 8 to 256 samples per texel, weights quantised to 8 to 24 bits (also as a cumulative sum), the Mitchell family around `B = 0, C = 1` (the `a = -1` cubic is the unique minimum) and linear interpolation between phase tables: none removes the cubic's last 0.08% to 1%.

Remaining after round 3 (each item is closed or narrowed in "Round 4" below):
- The cubic's last 0.08% and the up to five levels under Half: closed by the measured integer table (round 4).
- Reductions (scale below one) and mirrored draws: closed for the 8x8 and noise captures (round 4); the leftovers are listed below.
- Plain Bilinear arithmetic was narrowed on 12 October 2026: 1/2048 fractional weights, source byte premultiplication and final byte rounding make all 144 new rectangle controls exact, and leave six one-level pixel differences in 64 independent cropped/mirrored controls. The coordinate stepping behind the recorded fixtures' residuals (float32 rounded toward minus infinity, a row stepper from the first covered pixel) and the rotated plain Bicubic arithmetic were closed in round 7, see "Round 7" below; the signed phase-boundary ties remain, see [Native parity review](./native-parity-review.md).
- Rotated draws with ImageAttributes WrapMode and the three destination corners: narrowed in round 4 (below).

### Round 4: measured cubic weights, reductions, mirrors, wraps and the corner snap

Probes `HighQualityCubicWeightProbe.cs` (mode `hq-cubic-weights`, source rows from `generate-hq-cubic-source.ts`), `HighQualityPhaseProbe.cs` (`hq-phases`), `HighQualityReductionProbe.cs` (`hq-reductions`, scratch analysis only, not committed: 11 MB) and `HighQualityWrapProbe.cs` (`hq-wrap`); captures `hq-cubic-weights`, `hq-phases`, `hq-wrap`; code `src/emf-plus-hq-cubic-weights.ts`, `resampleHqAxisAligned`, `hqReductionTaps`, `snapOriginKeepCorners`; tests `emf-plus-hq-cubic-weights.fixture.test.ts`, `emf-plus-hq-phases.fixture.test.ts`, `emf-plus-hq-wrap.fixture.test.ts`.

Rules found, with the evidence:
- **The cubic weights are a measured integer table.** Rows of designed noise (400 rows, 1,016 texels, drawn 128/127 wider so every destination column visits all 128 phase bins; about 2% of the sums fall within 0.02 of a half level, chosen offline) leave exactly one integer weight vector per bin that reproduces all 1,221,600 native values; the table is exactly symmetric (bin `127 - b` is bin `b` reversed) and sums to 65536 in every row. 88 of the 128 rows equal the kernel integral with each texel edge rounded to 1/65536 (426 of the 1.2 million values differ for that analytic table); the other 40 differ by one unit between two adjacent taps. The deviations are ±0.1 to 0.2 of a unit at the texel edges, larger than a float32 evaluation of the cumulative polynomial gives, and no numerical-integration, bit-depth or kernel-parameter variant reproduces them, so the 64 rows (the other 64 mirror them) ship as data (`HQ_CUBIC_HALF_TABLE`, 640 integers). Result: axis-aligned bicubic noise upscales 35 of 46,569 values off to 0; arithmetic captures 206 to 288 of 288 exact; independent captures 38 to 122 of 192; rotated bicubic upscales and Half draws improve with the same table (Half noise: 0 exact of 20 to 17).
- **Reductions use the same machinery in destination space.** Each source texel edge `e` (relative to the source origin) lands at `s (e - P)` destination pixels, `P = (k S + offset) / 65536`; that offset is rounded down to 1/128 pixel and a texel's weight is the difference of the kernel's running integral at its two edges' offsets: the cubic's half-grid table above (`T(m)` at `(m + 1/2) / 128`, rebuilt from the measured weights), the tent's `512 j - 2 j^2` at whole grid points. Evidence: per-column least squares of 1,200 independent noise rows recovered the weights to 1e-4 and showed centre and width varying per column (the sawtooth of the apparent centre has period 9 at 0.9x) until the 1/128-pixel offset was introduced (rms 174 to 9.6 units of 1/65536 at 0.9x); on `hq-axis-noise` the reduction values that were off (987 bicubic and 1,161 bilinear in scratch counts that are not pinned) become 0 and 2 of 4,536 per kernel (two tent values at 0.4x where an offset falls exactly at the end of the kernel; pinned in `emf-plus-high-quality-rotated.fixture.test.ts`); the 8x8 arithmetic reductions (all 48) and the `hq-phases` reductions at 0.9x, 0.8x, 0.75x, 0.5x (both kernels) are exact. The weights use the float32 scale for the stretch (the 0.75x tie at the left edge needs `0.75`, not `65536 / round(65536 / 0.75)`).
- **Mirrored x axes** (a negative-scale matrix) step backwards from the far end in the source's own direction, `P = (covered pixels + d - k) S` for `d` the distance from the left edge to its first covered pixel (the flipped-source fits show the apparent `- 65536` and the tent's one-bin shift as consequences of the flip), plus a few units that grow with the source width (`round(srcW / 170)`: 3 at 512 texels, 0 at 13 or for a power-of-two step) and the left edge, not the right, is the one snapped to 1/16. The mirrored 2.25x upscales of the independent captures become exact including a 0.375 fractional origin; of the `hq-phases` rectangle draws 6 of 9 mirrored upscales per kernel are exact and all 4 mirrored sub-rectangles.
- **A fractional left edge steps from the unsnapped edge.** The 3.4 origin of `hq-phases` was 26,168 of 43,272 values wrong with the 1/16-snapped edge (the 0.1 and 0.9 origins of the scratch captures likewise) and is exact with the raw one.
- **Clamp to a transparent colour is no WrapMode.** All 28 native Clamp-transparent draws equal the draw without attributes pixel for pixel, in every shape. The other modes change only a ring about two pixels wide inside the quad (24,523 pixels over 140 draws). Axis-aligned draws read the wrapped (or clamp-coloured) texels through the same weights and are exact at 1.55x, 2.25x and 0.625x in every mode (over 2,000 pixels per mode off before); a rotated draw is the two-stage draw with the wrap on the source in the pre-scale and on the intermediate in the plain pass (about 1,085 pixels more than 3 levels off over four 25-degree draws down to 230).
- **Only the first destination corner of a rotated draw snaps to 1/16 pixel**, the other two stay: the edge vectors absorb the snap. 16 native unwrapped rotated and sheared draws: 2,682 pixels more than one level off with the matrix kept and the origin moved (the round 3 model), 1,141 with all three corners snapped, 3,816 and 3,846 with floor or ceil, 1,021 and 3,080 at 1/8 and 1/32 pixel, 704 for this rule.
- Real fixtures (zero tolerance, 15,741 pixels each): `gpx-image-highqualitybicubic` 1,084 to 121 pixels off, `gpx-image-highqualitybilinear` 1,145 to 34, `gpx-image-pom-half-highqualitybicubic` 294 to 28, the three WrapMode HighQualityBicubic fixtures 3,417 to 3,641 down to 77 to 120; every one is now within one level at tolerance 1 (`gdi-parity.fixture.test.ts` tightened from eight levels). Bundle: `dist/index.mjs` 1,915,916 to 1,922,296 bytes.

Hypotheses tried and rejected: weights from a polynomial or numerical integral in float32 or at 8 to 1,024 samples per texel (the table's off-by-one pairs are not float32 noise: of 46,392 noise values the best float32 form leaves 20 mismatches, the rounded analytic integral 16, the table 0); an unrounded step for reductions (drifts by hundreds of units at 0.9x); a reduction pre-decimated by the integer part of the ratio (the least-squares weights are a single stretched kernel); a different cubic parameter or stretch for reductions (`a = -1`, stretch 1 are the unique minimum, rms 5 against 11 for `a = -0.9`); the destination width rounded to a whole pixel for the step (a 461-pixel step for 460.8 leaves rms 4,500); tie rules for the reduction edge (floor, and ceil minus one, tried at every offset; floor fits except as noted); the 28.4 grid snap of the mirrored right edge.

Still open, with the data that would decide it:
- **The step near a half, and the few-unit position jitter.** `S` is the rounded reciprocal, but a reciprocal within 0.004 below a half (1.37x: 47,836.496) rounds either way and which way depends on the draw's height (47,836 at heights 8 to 64, 47,837 at 336, `sprobe` scratch captures): a float32 rounding of the matrix inverse that the width does not fix. Positions are also off by a few units of 1/65536 that grow with the source width (sub-rectangle draws want +1 to +3, full-bitmap draws 0) and are zero for power-of-two steps: sub-rectangle draws with a whole-bin step (250 -> 256, 200 -> 256) still disagree on every column. One capture per source width and height at a step that is not a power of two (a grid of 16 widths by 8 heights at 1.37x and 1.55x) would let the float32 evaluation order of the three-point matrix inverse be fitted. Round 6 captured that grid (17 widths by 10 heights, 680 draws): see "Round 6: the step grid (negative result)" below; the plain rounding reproduces 438 of the draws and nothing tried predicts the rest.
- **Mirrored unit scale and rectangles with negative widths.** Closed in round 5 (below): the converter now tells `EmfPlusDrawImage` from `EmfPlusDrawImagePoints` (`DeferredImageResample.pointsForm`), a rectangle is an exact mirror and points are one only for power-of-two source sizes.
- **Rotated wrap rings.** About 230 pixels over four 25-degree draws (up to the full range at the far edges of the quad) remain after the two-stage wrap; the wrap period of the intermediate (a 19-texel intermediate for 18.6 device pixels) or which stage wraps the pre-scale's own overhang is not decided. Rotated draws at integral lengths (12 x 1.5 = 18) and a second source width would separate the two.
- **Rotated Half at 45 degrees and 0.5x** (`hq-rotated-fine`, 60 pixels up to 30 levels): an intermediate of 8 x 9 reproduces it exactly while the probe's float32 lengths are 7.999999 and 8; the length arithmetic in float32 for a quad whose corners were shifted by the half pixel is not reproduced. Round 5 (below) widens the data to 528 draws and finds no float32 length formula that predicts the pre-scale size; the item stays open.
- **Reduction ties at the end of the kernel** (0.65x, 0.6x and 0.3x leave a few to a few hundred values a level off in `hq-phases`; two bilinear values at 0.4x): which side of the grid an edge exactly at the kernel's end falls on.
- Plain Bilinear arithmetic and the plain Bicubic arithmetic at scales other than one under rotation (independent captures modes 2 and 3) were unchanged here; both are closed in round 7 below.

### Round 5: cropped sources, the record form and unit-scale mirrors

Probes `HighQualityCropImpulseProbe.cs` (mode `hq-crop-impulse`: one white row or column of an opaque black bitmap per draw, so the destination red is 255 x the weight of that texel and the alpha the share of the kernel on texels that exist; 22 source rectangles, both kernels, both record forms, scales 0.5 to 3, five fractional offsets, 235,620 draws), `HighQualityCropAlphaProbe.cs` (`hq-crop-alpha`: 262,144 white draws read at their edges, 65,536 at each of four whole destination offsets), `HighQualityCropHeightProbe.cs` (`hq-crop-height`: the rectangle form's unit-scale margin against the height and the top) and `HighQualityHalfLengthProbe.cs` (`hq-half-length`); captures of the same names; code `resampleHqAxisAligned` (`rowMargin`, `mirroredBias`) and `DeferredImageResample.pointsForm`; test `emf-plus-hq-crop.fixture.test.ts`. Before the changes 28,900 of the 235,620 impulse draws were off (the tent's vertical upscales at 1,413 to 2,355 draws per scale, the mirrored unit-scale axis at 1,889 to 2,269 draws per form and kernel with errors up to 255), after them 4,051 (the exact groups and the residual per group are pinned in the test).

Rules found, with the evidence:
- **A scaled draw's vertical pass reads the source rectangle's rows plus the kernel's radius, and no more.** The tap one texel past the rectangle's end is read, the next is dropped: the last row of a cropped 3x tent draw keeps 251 of 255 alpha although the bitmap has the texels (the unit test `reads the bitmap beyond a source sub-rectangle` expected 255 in that row for every kernel and now expects 251 for HighQualityBilinear). Margin `ceil(support / min(1, scale))` texels with support 1 for the tent and 2 for the cubic; it fits all 22 rectangles at 0.5x to 3x in both forms. The x axis has no such margin (taps past the rectangle's right edge are read). A WrapMode draw wraps instead (the `gpx-image-attr-flipxy-highqualitybicubic` fixture exceeded its 0.1% bound, at 0.14%, when the margin was applied under a wrap, so it is not). Independent crop draws (`hq-independent`): the 48 non-mirrored crops go from 30 to 36 exact.
- **DrawImage and DrawImagePoints differ for a mirrored unit-scale axis.** The destination rectangle with a negative width is an exact mirror (pixel `k` of the covered run sits on texel `covered - 1 - k`; the fractional origin only moves the filter phase; a whole origin is a copy): the model drops two texels for a fractional left edge and one for a whole one (plus one unit for the cubic), and all 22 rectangles x both kernels x five offsets are exact (2,815 draws per kernel). `DrawImagePoints` with reversed points is the same exact mirror only when the source rectangle's width and height are both powers of two, a pure function of the two sizes (0 violations in the 15,360 white draws with the rectangle at the bitmap's origin and `sw` of at least 3: two kernels, two fractions, four offsets); any other size is the general path, an image translated two texels from the exact mirror (the first covered pixel of a 12-wide rectangle samples texel 12 and the last texel 1), whose phase is one unit of 1/65536 texel above the model for most sizes (four of the nineteen rounded sizes measured want none). Rounded mirrored points draws are therefore still off by one or two levels in 256 (tent) and 189 (cubic) of the 2,815 unit-scale impulse draws per kernel, in 552 and 617 of the 2x draws, and in 132 and 72 of the reductions (the same unit is added for them: 1,006 and 1,120 off at 0.5x without it). Independent captures: mirrored 44 of 96 become 88 of 96, all 192 now 172 exact (122 before), differing pixels 6,291 to 231 and summed error 228,640 to 238 (`hq-arithmetic-bounds.json` tightened on 182 metrics, none loosened); the `hq-phases` rectangle draws of mirrored fractional origins go from 4 to 5 of 5 per kernel. The interpretation (float32 rounding of the matrix inferred from three points, exact for power-of-two sizes only) is the only one the data supports: no other quantity separates the exact sizes.
- **The unit-scale vertical margin is not reproduced.** Whether the last row of an unscaled cropped axis reads the tap past the rectangle (alpha 255) or drops it (alpha below) is a deterministic function that the model does not have: it is the same in any draw order (four orders of 65,536 draws each: no difference), it changes with the destination's whole offset (8, 16, 24 and 40 give four different tables), it depends on the height (at offset 8 heights 24 to 31 read it and heights 1 to 7 never do) and, for `DrawImagePoints`, on the width through the width's mantissa alone (widths 3, 6, 12 and 24 give one row of the table, 5, 10 and 20 another), and the rectangle form is a function of the height and the top alone (0 disagreements in 35 groups of equal height and offset; `hq-crop-height`). Float32 products of the reciprocals of the two sizes (six sign tests, at most 649 of 960 table cells where a coin toss scores 480) and areas do not predict it. Scaled axes are exact (above), so the unit axis keeps the unmargined behaviour.
- **Rotated Half at 45 degrees and 0.5x stays open.** `hq-half-length` draws 528 rotated quads (11 angles from 15 to 160 degrees, 12 scale pairs at whole device lengths, None and Half, both kernels, destination at (100, 60)); the pre-scale size among the default and its eight neighbours that best reproduces each draw differs from the rounded-up float32 length in 142 of them (including None draws, where the old data held), and none of four length formulas (float32 square root of float32 squares, double hypot, float32 of the hypot, float32 square root of double squares) with eight tolerances from 0 to 2.4e-7 predicts the best size for more than 222 of the 313 draws that one of the sizes fits to within five pixels; the Half-shifted corners transformed through the float32 matrix (the candidate that gives 8 x 9 for the old draw with a tolerance of 1.0e-7) reach 59 of 111 at 1.0e-7 and 88 of 111 at 2.4e-7 for the Half cubic draws. The size depends on the float32 noise of the destination points, which grows with their magnitude; the native length computation (it is not the points' own differences) would need to be observed, for instance by recording the same draw from several world transforms.

- **The positive end of the plain Bicubic unit-scale copy interval is a power-of-two rule.** Probe `BicubicBoundaryProbe.cs` (mode `bicubic-boundary`, capture `bicubic-boundary`: noise bitmaps of 12 x 12 and 48 x 48 texels drawn 1:1 with `DrawImage(destRect, srcRect)` and the source offset swept in 1/4096 steps through plus and minus 1/64: 11,058 sweep draws and 10,368 size draws). At a whole destination offset the draw is a copy from -64/4096 to +63/4096 for every rectangle size (-65/4096 never copies); at +64/4096 (a source phase of +1/64, a translation of exactly -1/64 from a whole number) it copies only when the rectangle's width and height are both powers of two (the 36 pairs of 1, 2, 4, 8, 16 and 32 copy for some offset of the other axis; the 108 pairs of the 144 sizes sampled with a width or height of 3, 5, 6, 7, 12 or 24 never do), and then for any offset of the other axis when `w * h * max(w, h)` is at most 256 (17 pairs: 1 x 1 to 4 x 8, 8 x 4, 1 x 16 and 16 x 1) and otherwise only when the other axis' offset is a multiple of 1/128 (the other 19 pairs, 8 x 8 among them). At a fractional destination offset the sweep around a whole source offset never reaches the copy interval (the draws are the convolution at the quantised phase `floor(k / 64)`). The model (`unitCopyOffset`) reproduces all 11,058 sweep draws and the copy or convolution of all 10,368 size draws (4,016 copies); the 384 phase captures go from 380 to 384 exact and the 936 independent copy captures from 930 to 936 (the ten residuals listed in `emf-plus-bicubic-arithmetic.fixture.test.ts` are gone). The dependence on powers of two and on the low bits of the other offset is the signature of float32 rounding in the transform that the `DrawImagePoints` mirror shows too; the rule is fitted, not derived.

Hypotheses tried and rejected: closing the positive end for every size (ten independent copy captures that were exact become wrong: 129, 276 to 285, 597 and 744 to 753); a rule on the area alone (4 x 8 and 8 x 4 copy at any offset, 2 x 16, 16 x 2, 1 x 32 and 32 x 1 of the same area need multiples of 1/128); a rule on the destination offset (the whole offset decides only whether the boundary can be a copy at all); clipping the taps to the rectangle on one side (86 to 89 of 192 independent captures exact for the top or left, 76 for the bottom or right, against 122 unclipped); applying the margin at unit scale as well (109 exact against 128); a half-bin or whole-bin extra bias for the unit-scale mirror (the sizes want none or one unit, nothing else); the power-of-two test on the width alone (the height decides as well); a record-form flag for the vertical margin (the rectangle form is as irregular, only independent of the width).

### Round 6: the step grid (negative result)

Probe `HighQualityStepGridProbe.cs` (mode `hq-step-grid`, capture `hq-step-grid`, test `emf-plus-hq-step-grid.fixture.test.ts`): independent grey noise of 17 widths (8 to 40) by 10 heights (8 to 336), both kernels, `DrawImage(destRect, srcRect)` at (4, 4) with 1.37x and 1.55x (680 draws). The plain rounded reciprocal reproduces 438 of 680 (83 of 170 per kernel at 1.37x, 136 of 170 at 1.55x). Fitting an integer step and an integer start offset per axis to each draw (612 of 680 exact with them; the fitted and rejected-variant counts in this section are generator figures from scratch analysis, only the 438 and its split per kernel and scale are pinned by the test) gives:
- **1.37x (65536 / 1.37 = 47,836.496):** the x step is 47,836 for widths 8 to 32 and 47,837 for width 40, at every height. The y step is 47,836 for heights 8 to 32 and 47,837 for 48 and above, except that a height of 64 with a power-of-two width (8, 16, 32) steps 47,836: the vertical step depends on the width, consistent with a matrix inferred from three points even for the rectangle form (exact for power-of-two sizes). Heights 100 and 200 leave one wrong row after the step fit and 336 wants a start offset of -1 unit with 47,837.
- **1.55x (42,281.29):** the plain step is right for every width and height up to 100; the y axis wants an offset of -1 unit at height 200 and -3 at 336, a drift that a constant integer step cannot hold, so the per-row position of tall sources is not `k S + offset`.

Rejected on this data (scored against the fitted steps; the plain rounding scores 588 of 680 axis values): a fractional step kept unrounded (365 draws exact), floor (438) and ceil (304) of the reciprocal, the reciprocal in float32, the scale from the destination snapped through a float32 corner at 4 (`fr(fr(4 + d) - 4) / n`), a scale from `d * fr(1 / n)`, round-down float32 divisions and reciprocals, and determinant-based 2x2 inverses of the matrix (float32 or double determinant, reciprocal or ratio). The best form (a round-down reciprocal) reaches 592 of 680 and mispredicts the widths from 9 up at 1.37x, so nothing is shipped. Data that would decide it: the same grid with `DrawImagePoints` (three points, so the matrix is the quantity under test), a finer height grid (every height 40 to 70) to see where the y step flips, and single-row impulse draws to read the per-row position of tall sources directly.

### Round 7: source-coordinate stepping, translucent clamp and rotated plain Bicubic

The residuals of the recorded texture and image fixtures (1 to 2 levels on 0.1% to 2% of the pixels) were a coordinate-stage question that whole-fixture fitting could not answer, so the stage was isolated (probes `TextureCoordinateProbe.cs`, `ImageCoordinateProbe.cs`, `ImageLevelProbe.cs`, `MatrixInvertProbe.cs`, `TranslucentClampProbe.cs`; generators `generate-texture-coordinate.ps1` and `generate-translucent-clamp.ps1`; tests `emf-plus-texture-coordinate.fixture.test.ts`, `emf-plus-translucent-clamp.fixture.test.ts`, `emf-plus-hq-step-grid.fixture.test.ts`, `emf-plus-bicubic-arithmetic.fixture.test.ts`).

- **The experiment.** A 2 x 2 Tile texture (red = texel column parity, green = row parity, values 0 and 1; for `DrawImage` a stripe bitmap) is filled while the brush translation (or the destination origin) moves in 65,536 steps of a power of two (2^-14 to 2^-17 pixel). The filtered red is `round(fraction)` of the 11-bit weight, so a pixel flips between 0 and 1 where its quantised source coordinate crosses a half texel (rising edges at fraction 1023.5/2048, falling ones at 1024.5/2048: round half up). The draw at which a pixel flips measures that pixel's native coordinate to a thousandth of a bin, independently of any model; each capture stores the first two flip draws of every pixel. Sixteen texture sweeps (the three fixture matrices, further scales, a large offset, a fill starting at x = 40 as a rectangle and as a clip, PixelOffsetMode Half) are 61,916 flips; seven image sweeps are 27,969.
- **Vertical coordinates are exact, horizontal ones drift.** Rows are independent (the vertical flips of a 0.45x/0.4x fill agree with the double-precision position to the sweep step), but the horizontal residual against the ideal grows with x: for a 0.45x row 0 bins up to pixel 36, 0.17 at 64, 1.0 at 112, 3.0 at 156, and 8 bins by texel 560 at a large offset. The slope per pixel doubles with each octave of the coordinate (about 0.001, 0.017 and 0.047 bins per pixel measured for texels 64 to 128, 128 to 256 and 256 to 512 at step 2.2222, against 0.0017, 0.0174 and 0.0488 for the remainder of the step on the float32 grid of that octave), which is float32 accumulation that rounds toward minus infinity (the 0.75x row: a constant 0.0104 bins per pixel from texel 64 on).
- **The inverse matrix is float32 with every operation rounded toward minus infinity.** `det = fl(fl(a d) - fl(b c))`, `r = fl(1 / det)`, then `fl(d r)`, `fl(-b r)`, `fl(-c r)`, `fl(a r)` and `fl(fl(fl(c f) - fl(d e)) r)`, `fl(fl(fl(b e) - fl(a f)) r)`: 4,000 of 4,000 native `Matrix.Invert` results (`matrix-invert.json.gz`; half of them sheared) where rounding to nearest matches 898 of 2,000 axis-aligned linear elements and 1,089 of their translations, and truncation toward zero 1,218 to 1,252 of the linear elements (the existing `float32-down.ts` rounding of the path-gradient and Blend code).
- **The stepper.** The row's first position is `fl(fl(fl(a x) + fl(c y)) + e)` from the float32 inverse, and each pixel adds `a` (the x column of the inverse) with the same rounding; the row starts at the left edge of the fill (rectangle or clip alike: 0 misses at X0 = 40 in all four sweeps), rows are independent, PixelOffsetMode Half moves the point by half a pixel and the texel back. A `DrawImage` measures from the device position of the source rectangle's corner (the real origin, not the 1/16-snapped one: snapped, every flip of the origin sweep is missed), adds the corner after the products, and steps from the first covered pixel. Against the sweeps the double-precision per-pixel position misses 34,903 flips (1,608 to 3,856 per sweep, none for the identity matrix) and the stepper 1,959.
- **Rejected, with the data.** Fixed-point steppers of 16 to 28 bits with the step and start rounded, floored or ceiled: at most 59 of 160 pixels of a 0.45x row (Half) agree; float32 accumulation rounded to nearest: 60 of 320 flips of a 0.45x row against 270 of 320 for truncation toward zero (and more for rounding down); wrapping the start into the texture period before stepping: worse in every sweep but the identity (1,032 against 224 misses in sweep 0); the inverse translation in place of the offset-from-corner form for `DrawImage` leaves 16 of the 18 pixels of `gpx-image-bilinear` (the corner form: 0); the order of the three terms of the start changes at most one pixel of the texture fixtures.
- **Result.** Texture fixtures: tile 181 to 1 differing pixels, flipx 138 to 1, flipy 163 to 1, nearest-tile 181 to 1, pom-half-tile 318 to 2, flipxy 115 to 0, hqbicubic-flipxy 115 to 0, clamp 1 to 0 (all 240 texture controls stay exact through the stepper). Image fixtures: bilinear, default, low 18 to 0, attr-clamp/flipxy/tile-bilinear 3 to 0, rotated-bilinear 4 to 0; `codec-jpeg-arith-prog-420-dri2-playback` 113 to 22 differing pixels and `codec-jpeg-cmyk-photo-playback` 407 to 386. Left in the sweeps: the first position's last bit when the start is below zero or under Half (40 to 616 of 3,472 to 5,120 flips in each of those sweeps). The second pass of a rotated high-quality draw stays on the per-pixel model (the stepper trades as many pixels as it fixes over the 600 rotated captures).
- **Translucent Clamp colour.** Native reads the RGB of `SetWrapMode(WrapMode.Clamp, Color.FromArgb(160, 217, 43, 129))` as already premultiplied: the clamp pixel is (217, 43, 129, 160). `TranslucentClampProbe.cs` draws a 4 x 3 mixed-alpha source at 1x and 2.5x with alpha 40, 160 and 255 under Bilinear, Bicubic, HighQualityBilinear and HighQualityBicubic onto straight and premultiplied surfaces over six backdrops with SourceCopy and SourceOver (576 draws). In the premultiplied domain, with a channel above its alpha saturated, literal bytes reproduce every pixel under Bilinear (without the per-line clamp to the line's alpha: with it 28 of 78 pixels differ), Bicubic (with the per-line clamp to the line's alpha; the earlier reading differed on 66 of 75 pixels at alpha 160) and HighQualityBilinear (the horizontal intermediate not clamped to the alpha); HighQualityBicubic goes from 160 to 35 differing pixels and keeps a red a few levels high on the edge columns (its negative lobes meet a clamp above the alpha). **Premultiplied intermediate, feasibility:** a pixel with a channel above its alpha cannot be written to a canvas, and what native does with one on a SourceOver draw is a 32-bit add of the pixel and `round(backdrop (255 - alpha) / 255)` per channel with carries (6,578 of 6,578 touched pixels over six backdrops, `src/emf-plus-translucent-clamp.fixture.test.ts`): a red excess carries into the alpha and leaves alpha 0 with colour. Reproducing that needs the unclamped premultiplied block from the resampler plus a readback of the backdrop for those edge pixels and a way to store alpha 0 with colour (not possible in a canvas); on an opaque white backdrop the saturated result is white where native garbles it. It is not built: the saturated straight result is what a transparent backdrop or a PNG of the SourceCopy case gives, and every pixel without an excess channel is now exact.
- **Rotated and sheared plain Bicubic.** The 64 independent shear and rotation captures (`bicubic-independent`, modes 2 and 3) were 10,296 differing pixels up to 255 levels with the float convolution; a typical capture differs by one to eight levels on 97 of its 99 covered pixels and by coverage on one to three edge pixels. Native runs the integer pipeline of the axis-aligned draw (premultiplied bytes, 1/64 phase, 16.16 weights, truncated passes), steps the position with the float32 stepper above (the axis-aligned draw keeps its nearest-16.16 stepper, which fits it exactly) and covers the parallelogram by `rasterizePlusFill` from its unsnapped 28.4 corners, as NearestNeighbor does. Result: 44 differing pixels of at most 5 levels (21 shear, 23 rotation; 34 of the 64 captures exact). The independent-capture ceilings of 64 of the 128 captures are tightened and none rises. Mirrored (mode 1: 94 pixels) and axis-aligned (mode 0: 38) independent captures are unchanged.
- **High-quality step.** The 680-draw grid of round 6 is explained to 572 draws: the dest/source scale is a float32 and its last bit decides the 16.16 step of an upscale. With `a` and `d` the scales rounded to nearest, `r = fl(1 / fl(a d))`, the x step is `round(65536 fl(d r))` and the y step `round(65536 fl(a r))` (so the vertical step depends on the width), and the extent of the quad uses the scale rounded toward minus infinity (otherwise a whole-pixel destination edge adds a column). 243 combinations of nearest, down and up rounding of the scales, determinant, reciprocal and products give at most this 572; the 108 left are tall sources (one to four rows each: 8x200 to 40x336 at 1.55x, 8x336 to 40x336 at 1.37x; for example row 285 fails in both the 200 and the 336 high draws at 1.55x) and the 8, 16 and 32 wide sources of height 64 at 1.37x (native y step 47,836 where the chain gives 47,837). The recorded high-quality fixtures do not change (121, 120, 97, 77, 28 to 49 and 34 differing pixels stay).
- **High-quality residuals (negative results).** Level sequences under a swept origin (`image-levels.json.gz`: grey noise, HighQualityBilinear and HighQualityBicubic, one axis moving at a time) agree with the integer `k S + offset` model for 135 of 224 changes exactly and 155 within one draw (58-wide reduction), the rest being one draw late or early or a level apart at a 1/128-pixel phase-bin edge; a float32 round-down stepper in its place drifts by up to 12 draws by pixel 40, an unrounded or ceiled vertical step is worse (342 to 445 of 1,160 pixels differ against 30 to 62) and a floored one too (62 to 106), floor or ceil of the offset (78 and 79 exact of 224 against 135), float32 round-down evaluation of the edge offsets `q` of the reductions changes nothing, and truncating or flooring either pass instead of rounding it leaves 4,863 to 7,499 of 16,000 pixels (rounding both: 121 and 34). The unmatched changes are the vertical axis' ±1 level dithering between neighbouring bins, which the monotone model lacks (a pixel steps 150, 149, 150, 149 ... 140 over 21,333 draws); the fixture residual is concentrated in single rows (rows 2 and 3 of the 58 x 20 tent draw differ on 13 and 14 of 58 pixels, row 7 of the cubic draw on 23), so the vertical positions or weights of particular rows are what is left.

### Round 8: reductions in 16.16 fixed point, unsnapped edges

Probe `HighQualityReductionGridProbe.cs` (mode `hq-reduction-grid`, capture `hq-reduction-grid`, test `emf-plus-hq-reduction-grid.fixture.test.ts`): grey noise through axis-aligned reductions, both kernels, `DrawImage(destRect, srcRect)`: 64 x 40 to 64 x h for h 9 to 37, 40 x 8 to w x 8 for w 11 to 30, 40 x 8 to 20 x 8 at 15 fractional origins and 64 x 40 to 64 x 20 at 8 fractional tops (4 + 1/64 to 4 + 63/64), and the two reduced geometries of the `gpx-image-highqualitybicubic` fixture (148 draws). The weights themselves were first read as impulse intervals (one bright row or column at 255 intensities per draw, which reads each destination pixel's weight of each texel to 1/510: scratch analysis, not committed) and the edge bins were inverted from them.

Rules found, with the evidence:
- **Texel edges are fixed point.** A texel edge at `(t - 1/2)` lands in 1/128 destination pixel at `floor(((t - 1/2 - origin) 65536 - P) s16 / 2^25)`, `P = k step + offset` (the position of pixel `k` in 1/65536 texel), and the weight is the difference of the kernel's running integral at its two edges' bins, as before. The floating-point form `floor(128 s (edge - P))` put the exact-tie edges (scales such as 20/48, 21.6/48, 0.5, 0.4 give an integer `x` at every third or second edge) one bin low at half of them. The multiplier `s16` is `round(2^32 / step)` for the tent and the even number `2 round(s 32768)` for the cubic: of the 29 vertical scales 9/40 to 37/40, 28 tent and all 29 cubic sweeps are exact with them (the cubic's multipliers are even in every reduction measured, about 30 scales: 27,306 for 20/48 where the tent has 27,307, 39,322 for 0.6, 29,492 for 21.6/48); the cubic edges are centre-based (`centre -/+ s16 / 2`) with a half scale rounded to an integer, which is why only even multipliers fit. The horizontal axis follows the same rules (the 8 horizontal reductions give the same OK ranges, vertical and horizontal, to the unit).
- **A reduction's step rounds up from a fraction of about 0.47.** 65536 / s of 21/40 and 29/40 (124,830.48 and 90,394.48) step by 124,831 and 90,395 where 73,156.47 (43/48) and every fraction up to 0.40 step down (about 30 scales; the threshold is fitted between 0.469 and 0.477, `HQ_REDUCTION_STEP_NUDGE`); an upscale keeps the plain rounding (1.37x, 47,836.496, steps down). The mechanism is not found: float32 rounding of the reciprocal in any of 81 combinations moves it by at most 0.01, and a step derived from the quantised scale (`round(2^32 / s16)`) lands 1 to 7 off.
- **Edges are the unsnapped ones.** The destination edge is rounded to the 1/16 grid with ties down (79.5 sixteenths is 79), and an edge that lands on a whole pixel is still tested unsnapped for coverage: 4 + 1/64 and 4 + 1/32 leave the pixel 4 out and cover 5..24 (the converter covered 4..23). The vertical phase is the distance from the unsnapped top to the first row (all 64 fractional tops of both kernels exact). The horizontal phase is `1 - dx` of the unsnapped edge, except that an edge that snapped down onto a whole pixel steps from it, one step further (`(1 + f) S`), and one that snapped up one step less (`(f - 1) S`): 4 + 1/64 .. 4 + 1/32 and 4 + 63/64 (all 64 fractions of both kernels and both axes of a 40 -> 20 reduction exact, 256 of 256 draws, scratch sweep `fs-*.json`; the capture keeps 23 of them).
- **The float32 chain of the horizontal edge.** The Half-mode fixture draw (64 to 25.6 wide at x = 64) has a left edge of 63.4999962 (3.8e-6 pixel of noise from a double sum of float32 products) and a horizontal scale of exactly 0.4, all ties: native lands 1 to 2 units of 1/65536 texel earlier than that edge gives; forming the edge as `down32(down32(fr(a) srcX) + down32(e))` (the row stepper's rounding) reproduces it.

Results: `hq-reduction-grid` 73 of 148 draws exact (15,332 values off) to 144 (25 values off); `hq-phases` reductions: bicubic plain 6 to 9 of 9 exact (326 values to 0), bilinear plain 6 to 8 (85 to 10), bicubic mirror 689 to 396 values (6 of 9 exact, unchanged), bilinear mirror 354 to 359 (6 of 9 exact, unchanged: five values worse in an already inexact draw); the noise captures' bilinear reductions 2 to 1 values off. Fixtures at zero tolerance: `gpx-image-highqualitybicubic`/`-high` 121 to 12, `-highqualitybilinear` 34 to 7, `-pom-half-`, `-pom-highquality-` and `-pom-highspeed-highqualitybicubic` 28, 28 and 49 to 0 (now `exact` in `gdi-parity.fixture.test.ts`), `gpx-image-attr-clamp/flipxy/tile-highqualitybicubic` 97, 77 and 120 to 67, 41 and 90 (pixels beyond one level under TileFlipXY, Tile and Clamp 4, 15 and 2 to 1, 13 and 0).

Left, with the data that would decide it: the first two columns of the 64 x 48 to 58 x 20 horizontal reduction (the x values of 6 edges sit within 0.002 of an integer and native takes the other bin at k = 1 and 2 only, as if the position were one unit of 1/65536 texel early there; a constant -1 or -2 unit offset for k > 0 is worse overall, 604 and 735 against 430 values), one value in each of the 64 x 40 to 64 x 16 and 64 x 48 to 25.6 x 21.6 tent draws, the remaining wrap-mode pixels (the wrapped reductions and the sub-rectangle upscale of draw 3), and the mirrored bilinear reductions (359 values). The JPEG one-level pixels are float ties of the plain Bilinear stepper, not partial alpha (see EMF+ below, last bullet of "Image codecs").

### Round 9 (W4): DrawImagePoints is the same sampler with different float arithmetic

Research. Microsoft's documentation (Graphics.DrawImage overloads, InterpolationMode, WrapMode; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/gdiplusgraphics/nf-gdiplusgraphics-graphics-drawimage(image_constpoint_int)) says only that a three-point overload maps the source rectangle onto the parallelogram whose fourth corner is derived from the first and second and the first and third points. It gives no sampling arithmetic, no prefilter rule for HighQualityBicubic and no edge rule beyond WrapMode; the PhotoSauce article (https://photosauce.net/blog/post/image-scaling-with-gdi-part-3-drawimage-and-the-settings-that-affect-it) is empirical and consistent with what is measured here. Wine's and libgdiplus' resamplers were not consulted for numerics: they are independent float re-implementations and cannot carry GDI+'s bit-level rounding, so nothing is inferred from them. Everything below is measured here.

Answer to the key question (measured). A metafile records `DrawImage(image, rectangle)` as `DrawImagePoints` with float32 noise in the points ((20, 10) becomes 20.000030517578125, 10.000015258789062), and native drawing of exactly those points reproduces the JPEG playback (0 differing pixels), while the same rectangle drawn as `DrawImage(destRect)` differs from it by 22 pixels. The sampler is the same bilinear resampler (same 1/2048 fractions, same stepping); its float arithmetic differs. Rectangle and three-point draws of one rectangle flip at origins that differ by 20 to 30 float32 units on every one of 144 columns (`ImagePointsProbe.cs`, `generate-image-points.ps1`, captures `image-points.json.gz`, `image-points-positions.json.gz`, test `emf-plus-image-points.fixture.test.ts`).

Shipped (axis-aligned plain Bilinear DrawImagePoints only): the vertical position runs a relative 2^-21 above the exact one (959 flip origins bisected to one float32 value in eight geometries fit e = 4.3e-7 to 5.5e-7; best 4.8e-7 to 5.0e-7; no float32 rounding of the matrix, product or sum reproduces it, and the source or destination DPI does not change it; the mechanism is unknown, so the constant is the nearest power of two), and the horizontal step is the nearest float32 reciprocal of the scale (a 100 -> 100 draw with a 10 -> 8 vertical reduction: 3,155 differing pixels of 27,648 to 2; a 360 -> 162 reduction 31 to 6; the 37 -> 260 upscale is unchanged). `codec-jpeg-arith-prog-420-dri2-playback` 22 to 12 differing pixels, `codec-jpeg-arithmetic-playback` 2 to 1, `codec-jpeg-cmyk-photo-playback` 7 to 3; all image fixtures otherwise unchanged. Negative result: accumulating x rounded to nearest (the round-7 hint) is worse on every points geometry (12 -> 9,172 differing pixels in the JPEG-sized noise draws) and was not shipped.

Still missing (open). The 12 JPEG pixels left are columns (57, 66, 176, 185, 194) whose native position moves by up to 44 float32 units (8e-5 pixel, 0.02 of a bin) when the origin's y changes by 2^-16 pixel, independently of the row read (row 30, 60, 95 and 150 give identical shifts) and with no dependence on the origin's y at 10.0, 10.5 or multiples of 1/128. It is not a function of the matrix entries the model has (the model's x position does not depend on y), so the arithmetic that couples them is unidentified. The same round did not touch the HighQuality reductions, wrap-mode residuals or texture single pixels (attr-tile/clamp/flipxy HQ 90/67/41, hq 12/12/7, rotated 5, texture 2/1/1/1/1 pixels): no new evidence was gathered for them, and the reduction-step nudge (reciprocal fraction 0.47) is not explained by the 2^-21 bias (it would push 73,156.47 up, which native does not do).

## EMF+ gradients

Path gradients with uniform surrounds are closed to the step and, but for some of the pixels exactly on a copy's edge, to the pixel; ones with per-vertex surround colours are Gouraud fan triangles. GDI+ draws a path gradient as `N = ceil(2 * hypot(w, h))` nested copies of its boundary (`w` x `h` is the device-space bounding box: a 600 x 80 rectangle has 1,211 steps, 2,422 under a world or brush scale 2, and a diagonal that is a whole number of quarter pixels is not rounded past itself), each a step closer to the centre colour, and a pixel takes the colour of the innermost copy that holds it. `pathGradientQuantum` and `quantisedPathArgb` (`emf-plus-brush-gradient.ts`) rebuild that: the ratio rounds to a whole step, the outer half counts steps from the boundary colour and the inner half from the centre colour (they round a level apart at half-way; round 6 replaced both halves by the float32 accumulation from the centre described below), premultiplied channels and alpha interpolate together, and Blend and InterpolationColors curves are read at the stepped position. The sampler in `emf-plus-exact-fill.ts` passes the step count; the nudge that selects a left-inclusive span no longer moves the sampled ratio.

How it was found: the probe in `PathGradientStepProbe.cs` captures whole rows through white-to-black rectangles, where the path-gradient geometry is trivial (a row is the plane `255 x / half-width` up to rounding). 97% of values matched round(255 t); the remainder looked like noise until it was seen to depend on the rectangle's HEIGHT (rows of identical width and different height fall into groups whose boundaries sit where `floor(4 * L)` changes, `L` the half diagonal). Left and right of the centre mirror exactly; the left half is `round(255 k / N)` and the right half `255 - round(255 k / N)` for the step count `k = round(t N)`. The step count follows the half diagonal of the bounding box for rectangles of every size and centre, for triangles and ellipses (centred or not), under world/brush scales (device-space bounds), with Blend and preset curves read at `k / N`, and for isotropic focus (contours scaled about the centre). 72 rows reach 25,390 of 25,568 pixels; the 64 images 99.25-100%; the earlier 120 colour, 240 alpha, 252 focus-contour captures rise by 98,306, 20,132 (translucent), 35,628 (uniform alpha) and 18,270 exact pixels (see limitations.md for the totals), with every per-capture ceiling in `path-gradient-color-bounds.json` tightened (176 of 360 entries) and none loosened.

Per-vertex surround colours are not nested copies at all. GDI+ fans the boundary into triangles (centre, vertex i, vertex i + 1) and Gouraud-shades each with a whole-pixel scan converter; `emf-plus-path-gradient-vertices.ts` reproduces it. Found by isolating one channel at a time (a red-only vertex makes each channel the weight map of one vertex; the three channels of an RGB triangle are independent and identical to the three single-channel captures) and then reading how the colour behaves along one scanline of one fan triangle:
(1) a span runs from `ceil(xLeft)` to `ceil(xRight)` at the integer row, with no sub-pixel correction, so the colour at the first pixel is the left edge's colour rather than the colour at the crossing (this alone is the x shift of about -0.5 pixel that a plane fit to the colour shows, and it is why a smooth fan model is up to four levels off on slanted edges but not on a horizontal one); the right edge's colour belongs to `ceil(xRight)`, the first pixel past the span, so the per-pixel step is the end difference over `xe - xs`, not over the true width. (2) Each edge is walked along its major axis (x when |dx| > |dy|) in `trunc(|d|)` whole steps from `floor` of its first vertex, taking the edges in the order centre -> vertex i, vertex i -> vertex i + 1, vertex i + 1 -> centre; the edge parameter at a column or row `u` is `|u - floor(a)| / trunc(|b - a|)` (the colour at `x = 32` on one edge was `k / 11` for six rows, with the denominator `trunc(11.69)`; rows give `k / 13` for a 13.75-row edge). (3) The span interpolates in 16.16 fixed point with the step taken by `floor` (a toward-minus-infinity shift), the ends truncated, and the pixel rounded half up: a tie such as a constant 127.5 row rounds up while a drifting row loses a fraction to the floored step, which is why ties looked like they round toward the centre colour in an earlier fit. (4) Alpha and the premultiplied channels interpolate together (translucent captures: 2,180 of 2,181 exact against 2 of 2,181 for straight-alpha interpolation). (5) Device vertices reach the scan converter rounded UP to 1/16 pixel (`ceil(16 v) / 16`; rounding to nearest, truncation and floor all lose 10-20 points of exactness on scaled and rotated captures; quarter turns, 0.75 and 0.6 scales and 10, 25, 30 and 90 degree rotations are the evidence). (6) FocusScales do nothing when the surrounds differ per vertex: 40 captures with isotropic (0.5, 0.5) and anisotropic (0.25, 0.75) scales are 98.7% exact (14,577 of 14,766, none beyond a level) from the plain fan, against 0.5% for the previous model, and the differing-pixel ceilings of the 240 alpha controls (80 of them with a focus) fall from 255,177 to 33,604 in total. Measured on 320 captures (`path-gradient-vertices.json.gz`, `src/emf-plus-path-gradient-vertices.fixture.test.ts`): 120 random polygons on a 1/16 grid 98.78% exact (44,454 pixels, none beyond one level; 0.83% before), 60 translucent ones 99.31% (0.08% before), the four RGB triangles (black and white centres, scales 4 and 0.5) 99.7% (29% before), eight triangle orientations 94.2% (22% before), the single-channel set 98.9% (52% before), quad 97.3% and pentagon 97.7% (11% and 21% before), scaled and rotated sets 95.8-99.0% (0.2-1.0% before); every pixel of these is within one level. `grad-path-triangle-clamp` falls from 1,421 to 13 differing pixels at zero tolerance (none beyond a level), the tiled triangles from about 6,000 (38%) to 70-72 (0.45%); all 120 per-vertex captures of the 240 alpha controls tighten.

Rotated, sheared and anisotropically scaled uniform path gradients take their step count from the UNtransformed bounds: `N = ceil(|M(w, h)| + |M(w, -h)|)` for the brush-space bounds `w` x `h` and the linear part `M` of the brush-to-device matrix (the sum of the two device diagonals of the bounds; it is `ceil(2 * hypot(w, h))` for an unrotated scale, and a rotation does not change it). `PathGradientRotateProbe.cs` captured 70 gradients (a rectangle, a triangle and a wide rectangle, centre off the middle, fractional translation) as world and as brush transforms (the two give identical pixels): rotations of 0 to 200 degrees, a 1.5 x 0.7 scale, a 30-degree rotation of a 2 x 1 scale, shears of 0.25 to 2 in either axis and mixed matrices. Scoring every step count from 60 to 700 against each capture put the best count at the formula's in all 70 (no other count within 12 of it scored as well); the device bounding box of the rotated shape (which grows with the angle, 194 against 145 for a 60 x 40 rectangle at 30 degrees) scored 61% over the first 48 captures, the untransformed bounds 74%, the column norms of `M` 84% (right for rotation and scale, 3 to 6 steps too many under shear), and the diagonal sum 90%. The rest of the 10% was the rounding of the copies, next paragraph (fractional geometry puts a pixel within a few hundredths of a step of a copy edge often: an unrotated rectangle scored 95%). The step count was the only thing wrong with rotation: the nested copies are scaled about the centre in brush space and transformed, which the sampler already did.

Which copy holds a pixel is decided by the geometry GDI+ actually scan-converts, and that closed the ties and near-ties the ratio model rounded the wrong way (`emf-plus-path-gradient-copies.ts`, `pathGradientStepColor`). (1) The boundary's vertices and the centre are rounded to the nearest 1/16 pixel in device space (28.4 fixed point) before anything is scaled; copy `m` is then the exact scale of those points about the rounded centre by `(m + 1/2) / N`, with no rounding of its own, and a pixel takes the innermost copy holding it (an isotropic focus `f` scales a copy by `f + (1 - f)(m + 1/2)/N`, independent focus scales do it per axis). Scoring four fractional-geometry captures (a rectangle and a triangle, each at two rotations, 7,423 pixels): rounding the points to nearest and scaling exactly 100.00%; rounding nothing 91.1%; rounding the copies as well to 1/16 82%, to 1/256 91%; rounding the original points up 81% or down 77%. Over the 46 world-transform captures of the rotated set (107,809 pixels) the exact-scale model is 100.00%, where the smooth ratio with the right step count scores about 90%. (2) A step's colour rounds a value exactly half way between two levels toward the centre colour: 59% of the pixels of an opaque capture with a small colour range (alpha control 236: red 173 to 155, green 43 to 125, blue 6 to 123 over 164 steps) were on the ramp with half-up rounding and 99.93% with the tie rule; 186 of the 240 alpha controls and 20 of the 120 colour controls tighten, and the sum of the 240 alpha differing-pixel ceilings falls from 255,177 to 1,389, and a wider tie margin (1e-4 to 1e-2 of a level) scores worse, so the 0.01-0.02 margin the step-count round measured was the geometry's near-ties. (3) Independent focus scales are the same copies scaled per axis: ten scale pairs on a rectangle (100% of 100,000 pixels in step-reading captures, 99.93% by colour: the rest are one-level ties of a fully focused axis), on a diamond (98.9%) and on a triangle (99.6%), and 40 random triangles and quads (99.2%); the focus-contour captures' anisotropic exact pixels rise from 1,594,271 to 1,725,250 of 1,728,000 with every per-capture ceiling held or lowered, `grad-path-focus-*` rectangles with scales 75/25, 25/75, 50/0 and 0/50 are pixel-exact, `grad-path-ellipse-clamp` falls from 14 differing pixels to none and `gpx-pen-pathgrad` from 48 to 2.

Hypotheses rejected, with evidence: (1) a function of the ratio alone quantised to a fixed number of steps, or to any 2^k, in any rounding (rows of equal width and different height differ); (2) a fixed-point linear ramp with a rounded slope and offset per row (no linear fit exists for either half); (3) the linear-gradient knot table with 8- or 16-bit interpolation (best 259 misses of 8,677 values against 259 for plain rounding); (4) the vertex-distance quantisation as a scale `L / Lq` on the ratio (no better than plain rounding); (5) a 16- to 24-bit fixed-point weight or reciprocal for the sub-level residual below (best 405 misses of 138,188 left-half values against 637 for exact arithmetic); (6) float32 barycentric or edge-DDA arithmetic, edge left/right inclusion and a dx-sign rule for the exact half-step ties (each fits some of the 493 tie pixels and none all: the same geometry in the opposite vertex order flips about half of them); (7) whole-triangle Gouraud or fan-apex Gouraud models for per-vertex surrounds as exact barycentric fills (50% of pixels and errors up to four levels): the fan model was right, the missing piece was the scan converter (see above). (8) rounding the copies to 1/16 or a finer grid, or rounding the original points up or down, and float32 evaluation of the copy scale or of the vertex arithmetic in any of ten orders (see above; none changes the integer-sized ties); (9) a wider tie margin for the colour rounding; (10) a step count from the device bounding box of a rotated shape, from the untransformed bounds, from the column norms of the matrix or from its determinant (61%, 74%, 84% and 75% against 90% for the diagonal sum on the rotated captures, before the copies model took the set to 100%).

Pixels on a copy's edge are float32 (`emf-plus-path-gradient-copies.ts`, `floatCopyIndex`; found after the binary-fraction rule below, which it supersedes for every uniform path gradient without a focus). The single-edge, slope and corner captures put 76,000 pixels exactly on a copy's edge in front of the arithmetic, and the rule that fits all of them is: with `a` the first vertex of the fan triangle's boundary edge relative to the centre, `e` the edge vector and `d` the pixel relative to the centre (all in 1/16 pixel multiples, exact in float32), `u = (ay dx - ax dy) / (ex dy - ey dx)`, `b = a + u e` componentwise (the product `u e` rounded, then the sum), the pixel's larger coordinate of `b` (by magnitude, the x coordinate when equal) picks the component, `r = |d_c / b_c|`, and the copy is `floor(N r + 1/2)`, where every operation (the products and difference of the numerator and denominator of `u`, the division for `u`, the product `u e`, the sum, the quotient, the product with `N`, the sum with 1/2) is a float32 operation rounded toward minus infinity. The captures cannot say whether native rounds the products and differences of `u`: computing them exactly, or rounding the difference only, gives the same copy for every pixel of every committed capture, including twelve shapes of 300 to 700 pixels at fractional vertices (`PathGradientLargeProbe.cs`, 984,120 pixels, where the products do need more than 24 bits). A step colour is the float32 ramp of the step (round 6, closed): the 24 of 13,672 steps seen in the large captures (N between 954 and 1,278) that native rounds up when `255 k / N` is 0.0005 to 0.009 below a half level are reproduced, and with them every one of the 984,120 pixels of `path-gradient-large.json.gz` (2,534 differed) and the 76 inexact pixels of the step rows (25,568 of 25,568 exact). Native accumulates the ramp from the centre colour: the step `1 / N` and the running sum are float32 rounded toward minus infinity (`centreWeight`), so ring `j` from the centre sits a little short of `j / N`, about 6e-6 levels per ring, and a value exactly half way between two levels falls toward the centre colour (the tie rule of round 5, now a consequence). Found by scanning accumulation forms over the 13,672 (N, k, red) triples: a shift linear in `j` is feasible for 9 of the 12 shapes and not for the others, which ruled out any closed form; closed-form fixed-point steps `floor(255 / N * 2^b) / 2^b` from the centre best 11 wrong (b = 17 on a 256 scale), weights `k / N` to 8 to 24 bits best 18, float32 products 24 to 34, float32 accumulation of `1 / N` rounded to nearest or up 29 to 56, from the boundary or from both ends 27 to 58, and rounded down from the centre 0.
