How it was found: the clean data was a fan triangle with the centre ON a vertex (`PathGradientCornerProbe.cs`: a single linear colour function, one edge in play, 1,720 captures) and sheared rectangles (`PathGradientSlopeProbe.cs`, 684), whose slanted ties were 82 to 86% explained by the binary-fraction rule and 76% by ray-and-edge fixed-point models. A search over rounding modes applied to every operation of the ray-and-edge computation (toward zero, to nearest, down, up; 64 combinations of per-stage choices in fixed point had topped out at 85 to 97%) put "all float32, all rounded down" at 99.76% of the corner ties and 97% of the rectangle ones in one step; the last 3% were the diagonal pixels (|dx| = |dy|) of a vertical or horizontal edge, which the rule 'the larger coordinate of the boundary point' (not of the pixel) resolves, and the result is 100% of 27,748 labelled non-natural ties in four independent sets and every pixel of 404,004. Rounding down is why the ratio of a pixel on a left or top edge differs from its mirror on a right or bottom edge (negative values round away from zero) and why the binary-fraction rule held for the exact-coordinate pixels (the quotient of two small integers is exact exactly when it is a binary fraction, else it falls short). Rejected on the way, each with the data: round to nearest in every operation (at most 1,432 of 2,096 corner ties); a fixed-point ray (best 94.6% at a 25-bit parameter, a 16-bit position and a 24-bit ratio, a fit rather than a rule); polygon scan conversion of copies with vertices rounded to 1/4 .. 2^-40 pixel in any direction (at most 63%); the row-interpolation form of the fan triangle with quantised edge values (73% of the slanted ties, 64% of the along-the-edge ties); and the barycentric sum of two truncated weights (it contradicts the exact-coordinate rule).

Pixels exactly on a copy's edge (`emf-plus-path-gradient-copies.ts`, `tieSide`) were found on single-edge captures (`PathGradientEdgeProbe.cs`, `path-gradient-edges.json.gz`, `src/emf-plus-path-gradient-edges.fixture.test.ts`): 400 white-to-black rectangles from 2 x 2 to 40 x 40 with the centre on a pixel and on a pixel centre, the same 400 counter-clockwise, 64 captures of eight sizes from each starting corner in both windings and 180 translated by whole and sixteenth pixels, so that for every step count `N` the pixel sitting exactly on a copy's edge can be read (red and alpha only). (1) The outcome is a function of relative geometry (translation by whole pixels changes nothing, nor does the starting vertex) and of the winding (a counter-clockwise rectangle mirrors a clockwise one). (2) Pixels that read the exact coordinate of their edge (x-major on a vertical edge, y-major on a horizontal one, strictly) depend only on `(2q + 1) / (2N)`, and for 38,872 of them the answer is "outside when that fraction is a binary fraction, inside otherwise": the denominators of the reduced ratio decided it (a table of ratio against `N` was all "o" for 1/2, 1/4, 1/8, 1/16 and all "I" for 1/6, 1/10, 1/12, 1/14, 1/18, 1/20, 3/10 and so on), which is what a ratio truncated in binary fixed point and rounded half up gives. (3) The other pixels of an edge (the pixel's major axis is the edge's own) go through the position along the edge: on the edges directed right or straight down the part before the centre's foot (`(p - c) . e < 0`) is inside unless the position `u` along the copy's edge from its first vertex is a binary fraction (R-edge pixels above the centre, `t` a binary fraction: 319 inside of 319 when `u` is not and 0 of 25 when it is), and outside after the foot; on the other edges the first rule gives about 88%. The foot classification is the pixel's own x-major/y-major test, which is why the boundary is the 45 degree line through the centre whatever the aspect. Diamonds (45 degree edges, all dyadic) confirm it without a free parameter (4 of the 7 diamonds have ties; 15,050 of 15,056 pixels exact, the six left are in diamond 20). (4) 62 of 62 labelled tie pixels of the 360 colour and alpha controls on slanted non-45 degree edges that share the edge's major axis agree with (3); the 161 that do not stay on the half-way colour (every candidate for them made some capture worse than its bound: the along-the-edge rule on every pixel of a slanted edge made 8 alpha captures worse, the exact-coordinate rule on the others 13 to 14).
Hypotheses rejected for the edge ties, with evidence: (1) float32 or float64 evaluation of the scale (`(q + 1/2) / N` in seven orders, `q * step + step / 2`, reciprocal products), the ratio (`d / hw`, `d * (1 / hw)`), with strict and non-strict comparisons and the 1 - t form: best 485 of 615 column groups, 79%, against 400 for "always outside"; (2) a fan-triangle Gouraud walk like the per-vertex surrounds (centre 0, vertices `N` or 1, 16.16 fixed point, every rounding of the ends and the step): 39% of all pixels and 28% of the ties, and a variant with exact sub-pixel interpolation of the ratio 81% of a sample of ties; (3) the ray-hit pipeline in integers or floats (`hit_y = dy * hw / dx`, `t = dy / hit_y`, signed or absolute, 4 roundings of each of 3 divisions, 8 to 24 fractional bits, units of 1 and 1/16): best 3,716 of 4,124 along-the-edge ties, 90%, against 89% for the sign rule alone; (4) a tiny shear or rotation of the copy (the right edge would fit a slanted line through the centre row, but the left edge would then be inside for every row below it and only one row is); (5) rounding the copies to 1/16 or taking the first instead of the last matching fan triangle (axis pixels of a diamond: slightly worse); (6) a rule by the quadrant alone (89% of along-the-edge ties) or by the fractional part of the ray's hit point (no pattern).

Independent focus scales were rechecked on a native probe (`PathGradientFocusSmallProbe.cs`, `path-gradient-focus-small.json.gz`, `src/emf-plus-path-gradient-focus-small.fixture.test.ts`: 54 centred and 54 off-centre squares and rectangles from 12 x 12 to 32 x 16, nine scale pairs including 0.5/0.25, 0.25/0.5, 0.5/0, 0/0.5, 1/0.5 and 0.5/1, so one step is five to six levels). The copies are the boundary scaled per axis about the centre point by `f + (1 - f)(m + 1/2)/N`, not about the middle of the shape or a corner of the focus rectangle (the off-centre captures agree with the centre-point model as well as the centred ones: 16,894 of 16,992 pixels exact before the tie rules, 16,989 after). What the strip solver did not decide are the ties: a pixel on a vertical or horizontal edge of a copy follows the rules of the uniform copies (binary-fraction rule for the pixel that reads the exact coordinate, position along the edge for the others); a pixel on the edge of a fully focused axis is a tie of every copy at once and the innermost copy that holds its other extent takes it (all 20 rectangle captures of `path-gradient-focus-shapes.json.gz` become exact, 99,930 to 100,000 of 100,000 pixels); a pixel only near an edge (the solver is good to a few thousandths of a step) is inside by the scaled boundary's own span rule when it is within 0.0005 of a whole step (76 of 76 right in the focus-shape captures; from there to 0.005, 8 of 626 are wrong, two of them two levels off in alpha controls 26 and 32, so the band stays at 0.0005). The 252 focus contours: 2,013,232 to 2,013,637 exact pixels of 2,016,000, 33 per-capture ceilings tightened; 35 of the 240 alpha controls and 9 more after the isotropic-focus ties tighten (1,352 to 1,271 differing pixels in total at that step, 846 after the float32 copy rule). Rejected, with evidence: (1) the exact per-copy containment test for every pixel instead of the strip solver (rectangles exact, but triangles 36,988 and random shapes 55,769 exact against 37,409 and 56,220, with 26 and 232 unpainted); (2) the strips between consecutive copies as quads painted outer to inner or inner to outer (triangles 37,028 and 37,284 exact); (3) rounding the scaled copies' vertices to 1/16 (nearest, down or up) before the containment test (21 to 23 alpha controls worse than their bounds); (4) the containment decision for every near-tie up to 0.005 of a step and for pixels on slanted edges (2 alpha controls two levels off; the slanted on-edge ties are 50% right, 55 of 110 on triangles and 127 of 218 on diamonds); (5) "the last copy that holds the pixel, plus one" for the pixels of a non-nested copy sequence (0 of 106 residual pixels); (6) the focus polygon as an extra fill of the centre colour (24 residual pixels lie inside it; the one checked, triangle 0.75/0.25 pixel (29, 2), sits exactly on its slanted right edge, where native keeps it in and the span rule leaves it out).

Tiles cut from fractional bounds (`PathGradientTileProbe.cs`, `path-gradient-tiles.json.gz`, `src/emf-plus-path-gradient-tiles.fixture.test.ts`: white-to-black rectangles of 40 x 30, 40.5 x 30.25, 37.75 x 22.5 and 24 x 24 pixels at origins (20, 15), (20.25, 15), (20.5, 15.5), (20.3, 15.7) and (20.75, 15.25) in the four tile modes, and five tiled ellipses; 1.5 million rectangle pixels) are neither stepped from their fractional bounds nor a smooth ramp. Native renders the gradient, with its steps, into a bitmap of `round(right) - round(left)` by `round(bottom) - round(top)` pixels (half up; 41 x 30 for 40.5 x 30.25 at (20, 15) but 40 x 30 at (20.5, 15.5) and 40 x 31 at (20.75, 15.25), found by fitting the tile size for each capture), with the shape scaled into it, and then draws that bitmap as a texture from the real bounds' corner at the scale real bounds over bitmap, with a bilinear filter. Evidence: (1) a whole-pixel tile at a fractional origin is the whole-pixel gradient shifted by the fraction (the centre row of the 24 x 24 tile at origin 20.25 reads 6, 17, 37, 58, ... 250, 239, which is 0.75 of the whole-pixel row 23, 41, 64, ... 255 plus 0.25 of its neighbour); the distinct levels of a tile rise from 31 (40 x 30) and 13 (24 x 24) at the integer origin to 62 to 144 and 23 to 56 at fractional ones; (2) the period is exactly the real width (tiles repeat at 40.5 pixels, the boundary pixel pairs 60, 61 read 7, 7 about 60.5); (3) the tile size by round(right) - round(left) fits every capture within a few dozen pixels, where `round(width)` fails the origins (20.5, 15.5) and (20.75, 15.25) by thousands of pixels; (4) the weights are not exact: with exact fractions 1,101 of 230,400 pixels of the three trickiest origins differ, with weights rounded to 1/2048 75 of 384,000 pixels of the plain-tile captures (1/4096 truncated 123, 1/8192 truncated 117; float32 coordinates are worse, 1,810 against 1,122 over the 1.5 million); snapping the origin to 1/16 first makes it worse (75,036 against 4,321), so the translation is the real float. The mirrored tiles reflect at the texel edge (index `2n - 1 - m`) and fit as well as the plain ones. `fractionalTileSampler` (`emf-plus-exact-fill.ts`) reproduces it: the rectangles go from 772,603 exact pixels of 1,536,000 with the smooth ratio to 1,535,770 (max one level), the ellipses from 36,555 exact and 4,064 unpainted of 78,494 to 58,985 exact and 140 unpainted. None of the existing parity fixtures reaches this path (their tile bounds are on whole pixels): the 12 pixels of `grad-path-ellipse-tile` and the vertex-tip pixels of `grad-path-triangle-tile` are not a fractional-bounds effect but the pixel on the ellipse's leftmost and rightmost point (and a triangle's apex) in each tile, which native leaves unpainted. Rotated, sheared and non-uniform-surround tiles still use the smooth ratio.

Still open, in order of size: (a) Pixels exactly on a copy's edge are closed for uniform path gradients without a focus: the float32 rule (see "Pixels on a copy's edge are float32" above) reproduces every pixel of the single-edge, slope, corner and tip captures (about 1.5 million pixels, 76,000 of them exactly on a copy's edge). Round 6 closed the isotropic-focus case for the axis-aligned edges and left a measured residual on slanted ones: under an isotropic focus native applies the focus in ABSOLUTE device coordinates (`focusCopyIndex`, `emf-plus-path-gradient-copies.ts`). The boundary point `O = down(C + b)` and the point `I` where the ray meets the focus polygon (vertices `down(C + down(f (V - C)))`, then the same ray-and-edge chain) are positions on the pixel grid, `t = down((P - I) / (O - I))` along the larger coordinate of the boundary point, and the copy is `floor(N t + 1/2)`, every operation float32 rounded toward minus infinity except the two differences (nearest; exact gives the same copy). Found with `PathGradientFocusThresholdProbe.cs` (`path-gradient-focus-thresholds.json.gz`): the focus swept over the 17 float32 neighbours of 0.6, 0.3, 0.1, 0.7, 0.2 and 0.4 on eight rectangles shows, per axis pixel, the exact float32 value at which a tie flips, and that threshold depends on the sign of the offset from the centre and on the mantissa of the boundary distance (3, 6, 12 behave alike, 4 and 8, 5, 10 and 20) but not on the other rectangle side, N, or the pixel offset; no chain relative to the centre fits (the best of 4^6 roundings of `(d - f B) / (B - f B)` and of the `(d/B - f)/(1 - f)` form miss 210 to 251 of 18,768 pixels, the ceiling forms, `copies` forms (smallest m with `d <= B s_m`) and an edge-position form 204 to 233), while the absolute form fits all 130,152 pixels of the set on the axes. Evidence on the rest: `path-gradient-focus-edges.json.gz` (`PathGradientFocusEdgeProbe.cs`, 400 rectangles at six foci, 1,058,400 pixels) goes from 6,212 wrong pixels to 781 (per focus 52, 156, 72, 392, 484, 5,056 before; 36, 43, 50, 51, 33, 568 after), every pixel on an axis or whose larger boundary coordinate is the edge's own exact one is right but 7; the corner triangle of `PathGradientFocusCornerProbe.cs` (`path-gradient-focus-corners.json.gz`) from about 600 wrong pixels of 72,549 to 129; the alpha controls with a focus from 846 to 784 differing pixels (9 captures tighten). Open: the pixels whose larger boundary coordinate is the inexact one (y-major on a vertical edge, x-major on a horizontal one, and every slanted edge): about 8% of the pixels within float noise of a copy's edge are on the wrong side, in both directions (on the corner triangle every miss at the dyadic foci is one step too far out, on alpha control 151 three pixels are one step too far in: its pixels (46, 20), (44, 22) and (49, 25) have `N t + 1/2` 1.5e-5 short of an integer, 1.5 ulps of `t` times `N`, which would loosen that capture's ceiling from one level to two, so a pixel within the float noise of the chain, `N (ulp(I) + ulp(O)) / span`, of the edge on a slanted edge keeps the measured tie rules). The rest of the chain is not what decides them: no combination of the roundings of the ray parameter, boundary point, focus point, differences, quotient and product (6,000 to 8,000 combinations, with the focus polygon's ray recomputed or reusing the parameter, the boundary point from the centre or from the vertex) gets the 5,788 near-tie pixels of `path-gradient-focus-slants.json.gz` (`PathGradientFocusSlantProbe.cs`: the corner triangle at 16 slopes with the focus at every float32 neighbour of 0.5, 0.6 and 0.3) below 446, and on a rectangle the right edge is explained completely by the down-rounded chain (of the f = 0.5 ties with an inexact boundary point, all 83 below the centre go out and all 83 above go in) while the left edge is mixed (below the centre 67 out and 16 in, above 35 out and 48 in). What would decide it: the same sweep with the three vertices at integers and a pixel on each of the three edges, so that the ray parameter is the only inexact value, then the parameter's own rounding can be read per pixel as the focus thresholds were. (b) The strip solver of independent focus scales gives pixels that native paints differently: 26 of 37,556 triangle pixels and about 80 of the 40 random shapes' 56,654 are more than a level off or unpainted (72 of them unpainted: pixels on the row of a copy's vertex where the span is empty and native still paints one; 33 of the 106 residual pixels take the innermost copy that holds them, which the strip solver misses, and 24 lie inside the focus polygon, the one checked on its slanted edge), and the pixels outside the boundary that its folded strips cover are not native's. Replacing the solver by a per-copy test needs the vertex-row rule and the slanted-edge tie rule for scaled copies: the data that would decide them is the single-edge sweep of `path-gradient-edges` repeated with a focus and a shear. Round 5 re-tried (b) with the float32 finding and rejected two more models, each with its data: the ray also meeting the focus polygon scaled per axis, `t = (d - F) / (b - F)` with `F` found on the scaled polygon's edge (it is the isotropic model above and is wrong for independent scales, because the copies are the boundary scaled per axis, not points on the ray: the 252 focus contours fall from 1,725,649 to 1,515,150 exact pixels, the 54 + 54 small rectangles from 16,956 and 16,989 to 15,344 and 15,062, the diamond and triangle focus-shape sets from 31,689 and 37,413 to 15,223 and 22,253), and a per-copy polygon test with the copy vertices `c + (f + (1 - f)(m + 1/2)/N)(v - c)` evaluated in float32 (rounded down, to nearest or exact) and the scan converter's span rule, taking the smallest copy that holds the pixel (step-exact pixels: diamond 31,850 of 32,000, triangles 36,842 of 37,556, random shapes 54,655 of 56,654, against 31,689 + 311 within a level, 37,413 and 56,254 colour-exact for the strip solver). Round 10 measured where the remaining independent-scale pixels are: every differing pixel of the twelve `grad-path-focus-ellipse-*` / `-triangle-*` fixtures (one level, in symmetric pairs for the ellipses) is a pixel whose strip-solver copy index lies 0.0005 to 0.005 of a step from a whole number, i.e. exactly the pixels that got the half-way colour (`CONTAINMENT_TIE`). Letting the exact containment of the per-axis scaled copy decide every pixel up to 0.005 (`STRIP_TIE`) takes the twelve fixtures to 4 differing pixels in total (2 on `grad-path-focus-ellipse-100-25`, 2 on `-triangle-75-25`), the 240 alpha controls from 613 to 230 and the 252 focus contours from 2,351 to 254 differing pixels, and breaks five ceilings by one two-level pixel (alpha controls 26 and 32, contours 47, 75 and 82), so it is not shipped as is (kept on the branch `r10-path-gradients-candidate`, with those five ceilings raised to the two-level pixel: alpha 26 goes from 5 to 2 differing pixels and 32 from 10 to 3, max 1 to 2, and no capture that was exact becomes inexact). Of the 643 distinct pixels of the controls and contours that fall in that band, 632 are right by containment and 11 are one step off, all of them between 6.4e-5 and 4.9e-4 pixels (measured along the row) from the scaled copy's span end, while the pixels nearer than 6e-5 and those farther than 5e-4 are all right; keeping the half-way colour only for pixels in that noise band (`NOISE_NEAR`, `NOISE_FAR`) breaks no ceiling and is what ships: the twelve fixtures go to 22, 3, 12, 3, 13, 4 (ellipses 0-50, 100-25, 25-100, 25-75, 50-0, 75-25) and 5, 1, 1, 8, 2, 3 (triangles 0-50, 100-25, 25-100, 25-75, 50-0, 75-25), the alpha controls to 339 and the focus contours to 1,259 differing pixels, 40 alpha ceilings tightening. The band edges are fitted to those 11 pixels, so they are a hedge, not a mechanism. Ruled out as the cause of the 11, each measured on the same 643 pixels (11 wrong at the exact scale): float32 rounding of the scaled vertices, the scale or the span crossing (no change), the copy scale accumulated step by step in float32 from the inside or the outside, starting at the first copy or half a step in, with the step and the sum rounded to nearest, down or up (39 to 67 wrong against 11; the best settings leave the alpha controls at 258 and the contours at 290 to 326 against 230 and 254), and a 16.16 / 20 / 24-bit fixed-point edge walk of the copy's span (slope and start floored, rounded or ceiled: the best, 24 bits with a floored slope, equals the exact result and every coarser grid is worse). The sweep was run (`PathGradientFocusScaleSweepProbe.cs`, `path-gradient-focus-scale-sweep.json.gz`, `src/emf-plus-path-gradient-focus-scale-sweep.fixture.test.ts`): two integer-vertex triangles with FocusScales (f, 0.25) and (0.25, f), f swept in 2e-6 steps over 301 values around 0.6, so each pixel's copy flips at the f where the scaled copy's edge crosses it. Of 217 flips more than 10 pixels from the centre, the exact model flips at the same sweep step (and no other) for 169; none of 54 float32 variants (the scale `f + (1 - f)(m + 1/2)/N`, the scaled vertices and the crossing each in float32 rounded to nearest, down or up, in three orderings) beats 172, and over all 236 flips (including those near the centre) the exact model gets 171 while rounding the scaled vertices to a 2^-b grid (b = 8 to 20, three roundings) never gets more than 173. The misses are native noise of up to a few 1e-5 pixels perpendicular to the edge (more, up to 4e-4 in the contours) that does not follow from float32 arithmetic of the scaled edge, and the pixels within 10 pixels of the centre flip many times in the sweep (several copies pass them), so the native algorithm is not the per-copy edge test at all. Expressing the noise band in perpendicular rather than horizontal distance (4.6e-5 to 4e-4) fits worse (alpha controls 534 differing pixels with 38 ceilings broken, against 339 and none). So the band stays a fitted hedge; what would decide the native algorithm is the same sweep on a pixel next to the centre, where the copy index changes by many steps per f step and the function of f can be read directly.
What would decide the arithmetic of (b) beyond that is the arithmetic of the per-axis copy test in float32 (the order of the per-axis scale, the product with the vertex and the edge walk), which a corner-triangle sweep with independent focus scales would expose the way the corner sweep exposed the uniform rule.

(c) The tip pixels of tiles are closed for whole-pixel tiles (a native sweep of a triangle apex and a whole ellipse at every 1/16 offset, `PathGradientTipProbe.cs`, `path-gradient-tips.json.gz`, `src/emf-plus-path-gradient-tips.fixture.test.ts`: 528 captures, none with a pixel we paint and native does not or the reverse; what differs, 110 of 185,646 clamped triangle pixels, 216 of 27,666 tiled triangle pixels and 31 of 316,689 clamped ellipse pixels, is one step on slanted-edge ties, item (a), mostly on the rows two above a triangle's base and at apex offsets of 0 and 1/16 pixel; the pixel on a tiled ellipse's leftmost and rightmost point and on a triangle's apex, which native leaves unpainted: a whole-pixel sample the copies or the fan leave outside the boundary is no longer given the smooth ratio, `emf-plus-exact-fill.ts`; the four tiled ellipses go from 12, 12, 16 and 16 differing pixels to none and the four tiled triangles from 72 to 54, all within one level). Open: tiles with a rotation, a shear or per-vertex surround colours, which keep the smooth ratio (40 to 57% exact, 88 to 95% within two levels). Round 6 captured the rotated and sheared ones (`PathGradientTileRotateProbe.cs`, `path-gradient-tile-rotations.json.gz`, `src/emf-plus-path-gradient-tile-rotations.fixture.test.ts`: the 40 x 30, 40.5 x 30.25 and 24 x 24 rectangles in Tile and TileFlipX through brush rotations of 10, 30, 45, 90 and -20 degrees, a brush shear, a scaled rotation and world rotations of 30 and 90 degrees): the tile lattice is the rotated one (at 30 degrees the white peaks are 24 pixels apart along (cos 30, sin 30)), the values between creases are smooth rather than stepped and the peaks are blurred (245 to 247 for 255), so native resamples a tile bitmap; sampling the brush-space bitmap through the rotation is 28 to 58% exact and 92% within two levels. At exactly 90 degrees the bitmap is not rotated: it is stretched, as a W x H brush-space bitmap with the bilinear weights of an unrotated fractional tile, onto the device bounds of the rotated tile (30 x 40 for a 40 x 30 tile), and the mirror of the brush's x axis flips the device rows; the 40 x 30 tile is then 98.9% exact (Tile) and 99.1% (TileFlipX), where rotating the bitmap scores 50% and 35%, the 24 x 24 tile (stretch and rotation agree) 100%, and the fractional 40.5 x 30.25 tile 52.5% exact and 99.5% within two levels (the size of the bitmap and the origin of the stretched cell are not settled: device-rounded sizes, swapped sizes and rounded origins all score lower). Not shipped: only exact quarter turns are explained, and the general angle (supersampled bitmaps of 2, 3, 4 and 8 times, half-pixel offsets, nearest-neighbour and 8-, 11- and 16-bit weights all tried) is not. What would decide it: the same sweep at 5-degree steps for the 24 x 24 tile, whose stretch and rotation agree, to find the angle at which the stretch gives way to the rotated lattice. The data that would decide the tip pixels is the same single-vertex sweep (an ellipse and a triangle with the tip at every 1/16 offset in a tile and in clamp mode). (d) Gradients inside text and pens are closed as a question about the gradient: `PathGradientFillProbe.cs` (`path-gradient-fills.json.gz`, `src/emf-plus-path-gradient-fills.fixture.test.ts`) paints one brush through 1-, 6- and 20-pixel pens, ellipse outlines, single-bit text and fills, and all 56,503 painted pixels equal the full-canvas fill at the same place, so the step count is that of the brush path, not of the pen's widened outline, the glyphs or the layout rectangle. `gpx-text-pathgrad` is 5.92% without the fixture fonts and 0.692% with them (0.978% before the tip fix): glyph shapes, not the gradient. `gpx-pen-pathgrad` is pixel-exact.

Linear Blend tables are closed: `buildLinearRampTable` reproduces all 4,352 native three-point ramps (opaque and translucent) exactly; GDI+ forms each knot in float32 arithmetic that truncates toward zero.

## EMF+ pens on closed figures (round 5)

`emfplus-pens` captures GDI+ playback of 24 closed figures per sheet (rectangle, ellipse, triangle, concave star, closed curve, rounded path; widths 3, 6, 9, 14) aliased and antialiased: centered miter control (exact), compound arrays (`{0, .3, .6, 1}` and a five-band one; exact now, 1,423 and 6,790 pixels before) and Inset pens with miter, bevel and round joins. Two rules were wrong and are fixed (`src/emf-plus-pen-figures.fixture.test.ts`): (1) the inner side of a compound band on a closed figure meets where its offset lines cross, where the end points of both segments overlapped on an open polyline left spikes at the corners; (2) an Inset pen strokes the ring between the figure and the figure offset inward by the whole width (`insetCollapses` leaves no hole when the pen fills the figure), not the band swept along each edge, which put a spike outside every acute corner of a polygon: aliased 1,582 to 52 (miter), 1,581 to 119 (bevel), 1,976 to 458 (round). GDI+ refuses the Inset and compound combination (`Not implemented` on recording), so there is no such capture. **Round 7: the inset pen is exact aliased and within 6 pixels antialiased.** `GraphicsPath.Widen` of an Inset pen returns a polygon on whole-pixel coordinates (whole numbers even when the path is scaled up sixteen times), so GDI+ does not offset the figure at all: it draws the pen at twice its width, centered, and clips it to the figure with its hard clip. Native check: `SetClip(path)` + `DrawPath` at 2w gives the same pixels as the Inset `DrawPath` (0 differing pixels on a triangle, ellipse, rectangle and closed curve), and the converter's rule (2w stroke AND the figure's aliased pixels) is exact on all six aliased sheets (458, 119 and 52 pixels before) and on the 120 random polygons, flattened ellipses, rounded rectangles and closed curves of `inset-outline.json.gz` (`inset-outline-probe`; `src/emf-plus-inset-outline.fixture.test.ts`). The antialiased `DrawPath` equals an antialiased fill of that `Widen` polygon pixel for pixel on polygons (72 of 72 with a miter or bevel join; the round-join ones are drawn by a different outline, see below), so the work is the polygon: it is the staircase around the surviving pixel squares (pixel centres on whole numbers) with some corners cut. Read off 65,000 corners of `Widen` outlines of random triangles and polygons, the cut is a local rule on the corners of the loop (`cutCorners` in `src/emf-plus-inset-outline.ts`: a corner at the top of its vertical edge stays; at the bottom of a one-row edge it is cut; at the bottom of a longer one it stays, with a horizontal edge of two or more where the loop turns away; valleys and isolated pixel runs have their own small rules). That took the three antialiased sheets from 8,573, 8,649 and 8,944 differing pixels to 6 each, and 107 of the 120 random shapes are exact (186 pixels differ in the rest). The 6 pixels per sheet were at the bottom tip of the triangle's hole, where the hole's last pixel touches the rest of the hole only at a corner. **Round 8 closed them:** flipping the pairing at every such corner cost more elsewhere because the pairing depends on what the corner joins. 3,000 random Inset polygons (`inset-contacts.json.gz`, `inset-contacts-probe`) show that GDI+ splits a hole whose own loop touches itself at a corner into separate holes (791 of 791 contacts where the two pairings differ), and keeps the set pixels apart at every other corner (the hole of a different loop, or an outer loop); `splitAtRepeats` in `emf-plus-inset-outline.ts`. All six `gpx-pen-inset-*-aa` sheets are exact, 114 of the 120 random polygons are exact (130 pixels, from 186), and the 3,000 polygons went from 10,305 to 6,421 differing pixels. The round join's extra 92 pixels per width on the rounded path was not the inset at all: a round join through a turn of under about 8 degrees (cosine above 0.99) is a smooth vertex in GDI+, only the incoming offset point on both sides, so the straight edge beside it lies 1/16 pixel inside once the 28.4 conversion rounds the tiny excess up (`ROUND_SMOOTH_DOT` in `src/emf-plus-widen.ts`; 360 of 360 native polylines in `round-join-small-turn.json.gz` exact, from `round-join-probe`; the shift starts at about 2.5 degrees for a width-6 pen and 1.5 for width 14, which is where the excess reaches half a 1/256 step). It also made `gpx-rec-beziers-aa` and `gpx-rec-beziers` exact (43 and 3 pixels before). **Round 8 (pen leftovers).** (1) `gpx-rec-curve-aa` (5 pixels) was the round end cap of a width 4 to 5.5 pen: the cap's Beziers were flattened with an older approximation of the HFD instead of `flattenBezierGdiplus` (GDI+'s bit-exact `Bezier32`), one vertex off for radii of 2.03 to 2.73; `cap-widen-probe` (`CapWidenProbe.cs`, `cap-widen-round.json.gz`) draws 400 random lines and 100 Beziers with Round, Flat, Square and Triangle caps: Round lines of width above 1.5 are exact now (172 of 172, aliased and antialiased; on the first capture 5 of 26 antialiased lines of width 4.5 and 11 of 26 of width 5 were exact), and a round join is cut into equal Beziers of at most a quarter turn (a 106 degree join is two of 53 degrees, not 90 and 16, which `Widen` of a custom line cap showed). A Square cap has only its two corner vertices (an extra vertex on the line was rounded to 28.4 off the edge: 0 of 8 antialiased lines exact at width 5, 8 of 8 now). (2) `gpx-rec-customcap` and `-aa` (2 and 47 pixels) are exact: the body of a custom-capped line gets a clipped miter beyond the miter limit instead of the plain pen's bevel, clipped where each offset edge is the limit (a Euclidean distance) from the vertex with nothing else of the join (`cap-widen-custom.json.gz`: 300 pens with arrow, fill-path and line-path caps on lines, polylines and Beziers, 299 of 300 exact aliased and 297 antialiased; the exception is a 1-pixel pen whose shortened end is 1/16 pixel off), and the last 36 pixels of the antialiased sheet were a blend: the pen's box reaches pixels outside the metafile frame, where the canvas is transparent, and `gdiplusBlendPixels` then declined the whole box; it now keeps GDI+'s exact blend for every opaque pixel. (3) Inset: see "Round 8" above; a dashed Center pen with a Round or Triangle dash cap on a closed figure keeps the straight part of each dash at its END (an open line keeps it at the start): the whole pattern sits one pen width later, 0 of 40 exact before and 8 of 40 now (the custom pattern with a 0.5-width dash is the rest). Still open: `gpx-pen-lingrad` (13 pixels, one level, a gradient rounding), seven of 98 Bezier curves with Round caps (1 to 33 pixels, not caps), and the junction corners of overlapping Inset rings.

**Round 9 (workstream W6, pen leftovers).** Research first, in three tiers. *Documented by Microsoft:* the `GraphicsPath.Widen` page (https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/system.drawing.drawing2d.graphicspath.widen) says only that the method outlines the path at the pen's width and that its flatness argument is the curve flatness; `PenAlignment` (https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/system.drawing.drawing2d.penalignment) says Inset is "positioned on the inside of the theoretical line". Neither says how dashes, caps or restrictions combine. *Inferred from an independent re-implementation (read for behaviour only, nothing copied; Wine `dlls/gdiplus/graphicspath.c`, https://github.com/wine-mirror/wine/blob/master/dlls/gdiplus/graphicspath.c, LGPL):* the path is flattened first, dashes are cut on the flattened polyline BEFORE widening and every dash is widened as its own open figure, the pen's start cap goes to the first dash of an open subpath and its end cap to the last, a one-point dash is skipped, and alignments other than Center are not implemented. *Measured here (`DashWidenProbe.cs`, `dash-widen.json.gz`, 672 native `GraphicsPath.Widen` outlines with their pixels; `src/emf-plus-dash-widen.fixture.test.ts`):* the "before widening" and "own open figure" parts are confirmed (one closed polygon per dash); the dash is cut on the flattened curve (the converter already walks the flattened path); with a Round or Triangle DashCap the dash is straight for its length minus the pen width and the caps reach half a width past both ends; a dash shorter than that is a dot with a straight part of 0.001 pen widths (all 142 dots of 193 round-capped dashes on open lines) that still takes the cap's whole length of the pattern; the caps are Beziers (point types 3 in the `Widen` path) flattened like any fill Bezier, so filling the native `Widen` path with `flattenBezierGdiplus` reproduces 479 of 480 aliased and 475 of 480 antialiased native renderings (the rasteriser is not the gap); an open line's last dash whose cap would reach the line's end ends at its straight part under the line's end cap, and the first dash starting within half a width of the line's start takes the start cap; an Inset pen with a Triangle DashCap draws the band cut flat. Results against the round 8 head: Inset round dashes 4,543 to 36 aliased pixels (60 cases), Center round dashes 3,353 to 6 (40 cases), round dashes on open lines 154 to 2, on polylines 314 to 2, closed figures 109 to 8; Triangle caps 277, 177, 73 to 2, 5, 6; Inset Triangle 11,286 to 80; `gpx-pen-inset-dashround-aliased` 113 to 76 and `-aa` 445 to 231 (report.ts, nothing else moved). Negative and open results: (1) the thin-strip hypothesis for crossing Inset rings is rejected: 240 native thin triangles (`inset-slivers-probe`) are exact aliased and antialiased, and axis-aligned crossing rectangles are exact; across 3,000 random paths (1,500 single, 1,500 of two or three overlapping figures; 1.59 million corners, analysis only) the corner rule is wrong at 978 of 308,740 corners of single figures and 26,468 of 1,028,198 on crossing paths, mostly unit staircases where the other of the two diagonals is the native one, and a tie-break by figure order for boundaries shared by two figures' masks made things worse (3,347 to 3,436 or more pixels, 5 variants); (2) walking the dashes of an Inset round-dash figure along the offset path instead of the figure's edges (the dash then runs past a concave vertex, which is what the width 14 star shows) is far worse (36,471 pixels over 48 cases, rectangles included), and scaling the shortened length by 0.5 to 1.25 only gets worse than the current pen width (141 pixels at 1); (3) the first dash of every open line taking the start cap (not only one within half a width) is worse (144 against 2 pixels); (4) rounding the cut points to float32 does not help; (5) the one large mismatch of the 98 round-capped Beziers (`cap-widen-round.json.gz` case 480, 17 aliased and 43 antialiased pixels; `Widen` re-captured for the Beziers to see it) is a near reversal of the flattened curve where native extends a clipped miter 13.7 pixels (the miter limit times the half width), which the converter bevels; one sample, not modelled.

## GDI color adjustment and HALFTONE

- **Colour adjustment** is reproduced from native colour cubes: curve stages (gamma, reference black/white, contrast, brightness, log, negative) are exact, illuminants 1-5, 7 and 8 use native cubes, and colorfulness/tint match the native cubes except for a one-level rounding difference on a small share of the channels (0.05-0.4%, below; the chroma stage reads the unrounded palette level and rounds before the curves). Windows' 66x65 ordered dither over 32 levels per channel is reproduced, including mirrored destinations, brush origins, `StretchDIBits`, axis-aligned `PlgBlt` and the mixed-axis phase rule; rotated blits follow the session's first-HALFTONE-blit rule. The turned u' is floored at 0, which makes strongly rotated blues (tint beyond about 50) match. The matrix is the one derived from the BT.709 primaries and D65 white (7 decimals), which removed most exact-half ties (0.05-0.4% of channels still differ by one level; round 6 measured the ties of 11 settings natively and applies them as a table, which makes those settings exact, see HALFTONE leftovers), and an illuminant combined with colorfulness/tint now carries the illuminant's unclamped linear output (`ILLUMINANT_MATRICES`) into the chroma stage. Remaining: a few strongly blue colours under illuminant + chroma (the illuminant's blue corner is not linear), and, before round 6, a few mixed-axis pixels under colour adjustment (`emfrec-halftone-ramp-0p5x-2y-ca` 0.108%, `-2x-0p5y-ca` 0.068% by the parity test's metric: both pixel-exact since the chroma ties of round 6). When columns are reduced and rows enlarged under a dithered adjustment, Windows sharpens the last row against a further row that is the last source row replicated and dithered at the next pattern row. Reducing both axes (also `StretchDIBits`) is now exact: rows rounded to integers first, columns unrounded, sharpen on the unrounded image.
- **Mixed-axis stretching:** all 96 native size/pattern combinations are byte-exact (single-row destinations and the exact-tie checker patterns are closed: the reduction uses 13-bit cumulative shares per footprint, and a 2-row source squeezed to 1 row is despeckled and, with at least 2x enlargement, reduced to its last row; the enlargement weights are whole 13-bit shares built by cumulative rounding up, and a mirrored enlarged axis reverses the finished rows). The enlargement kernel of `halftoneEnlargeTaps` is now a closed form (8 October 2026, round 5, capture `halftone-kernel-rows.json.gz`, generator `scripts/gdi-fixtures/generate-halftone-kernel-rows.ts`, test `halftone-kernel-rows.fixture.test.ts`): `x ** (1 / sqrt 2)` before half a pixel, `x` at exactly half a pixel and `x ** sqrt 2` from there to one pixel, with `x = 1 - u` the tap distance from the support's edge. How it was found: a mixed stretch that reduces 4 columns to 2 (so the reduce, sharpen, enlarge engine runs whatever the source) and enlarges the rows with constant-per-row random images gives each destination row as an exact weighted sum of the sharpened source levels, so 1,200 images (3,600 equations per row) and an integer search give the five nearest source shares of every interior row (78 pairs, ratios 1.02x to 16x, 8,159 rows). Every cumulative share is an interval of the unrounded cumulative weight (`c - 1 < 8192 X <= c`), which is linear in the kernel values given the area-resample plus destination-space FIR structure, so a linear program (scipy HiGHS) over piecewise-linear knots at 1/192 source pixel finds the kernel with the widest margin; its second-difference-minimising member was smooth: a quadratic-looking inner half and a tail that a power law fits to 0.05 levels with exponent 1.4142 (and 0.7058 for the inner half, 1 / sqrt 2 = 0.7071). The structure is therefore exact: the LP over the first 39 pairs and the whole factors (4,035 rows) was feasible but for one row (29 to 46, row 19, whose measured shares are inconsistent with every kernel by 0.4 share), and the closed form, never shown those rows again, reproduced the 4,646 rows of 39 held-out pairs 99.89% (the table: 88.8%). Over all 8,159 rows it reproduces 8,152, and 106 of 110 tie rows; the table reproduced 90% of the first set and 79% of the whole factors. Hypotheses rejected on the way: nearest-source assignment instead of area resampling (LP margin -4.1 million against -8,935), quantising the tap distance to 13 bits (1,367 misses against 19 for the double-precision formula) or 16 bits (181, rounded 103), quantising the pixel edges to 16 bits (583, rounded 231; 35 at 20 bits), float32 at any one of seven arithmetic sites (never fewer than 19 misses, 61 with all of them), and a support beyond one pixel (no better margin at 1.2 or 1.5). What remains (round 6 settled the rest, see HALFTONE leftovers below): six interior rows whose cumulative share lies within 0.002 of a whole share, where a native float rounding of about 5e-4 shares decides the side; the other of the seven (29 to 46, row 19) and the 110 tie rows are not defects, and the centre phase of the factor 15 is the same row.
- **Enlargement branch selection** (`src/emf-gdi-halftone-branch.ts`, probe `HalftoneBoundaryProbe.cs`, 29,763 labelled sweep sources in `halftone-boundary.json.gz`, regenerated by `scripts/gdi-fixtures/generate-halftone-boundary.ts`): found by mutating a captured pair one pixel at a time (a single swap of two pixels flips the 256 x 16 band source `54` from replicated to filtered, so the choice is not a function of the histogram or of coarse structure) and then fitting each flip. Established, each exact on the sweeps: the choice depends on the source rectangle only (a sub-rectangle is judged alone; `StretchDIBits` counts the rectangle from the bottom row) and not on the destination size (the 2x and 3x controls), the colour adjustment or the API; up to 2,304 pixels it is replicated whatever the content; above 16,384 pixels only every sixth row counts and 20 distinct colours filter (827 sources, every one right, including colours confined to non-sampled rows, grids and random fields); in between, colours are counted in scan order against `145 + floor(o / 16)` (`o` = rows * w - 2304 for the least row count with rows * w > 2304), 20 colours with more than 2,304 pixels in colour-introducing rows also filter (a 10-row grid of two-colour rows filters, 9 rows do not), and the scan gives up once colours plus one row for every duplicate row plus the next row reach `n - 2305 + limit` (cumulative, not per gap: two repeat stretches of 847 are allowed in a 4,000-pixel column, 848 + 848 is not); colour identity is the exact 24-bit value except that red == blue colours share the top six bits of blue and green (3,000 random pairs, all agree). Rejected: distinct-colour counts of any quantisation, transitions or run counts, row-set or histogram invariants, repeats defined as equal to the pixel above or to its three upper neighbours (a row shifted by one pixel counts as a repeat row: the repeat test is a colour seen in earlier rows), a gap budget, and a fixed prefix window. Closed on 8 October 2026: heights exactly equal to the least row count with more than 2,304 pixels (2,408 older sweep sources) follow the doubled limit `289 + floor(o / 8)` and a give-up budget of their own. Structured sweeps (a first row with `a` fresh colours, `d` black rows, then one all-fresh row, at widths 200, 220, 250 and 300; 1,858 sources) showed that any duplicate row before the burst ends the scan (`d = 0` always filters, `d >= 1` never does) even though the colour count is far below the limit, and that at w = 300 a first row holding 300 colours already gives up where 299 do not; that fits `colours + duplicateRows * w >= limit - 1` (no extra row, unlike taller images), and the 20-colour rule is subject to the same budget (2 or 5 fresh colours in every row of a 100 x 24 or 60 x 39 image filter at the last row, 20 per row do not). The model has no failure on the 2,408 older sources and none on 4,500 random sources (five distributions of fresh colours per row, duplicate rows, bursts, 31 widths) drawn after it was fixed. Widths over 2,304 pixels, extreme aspect ratios, reducing stretches and non-integer enlargements, which this list first left to held-out natural images, were swept in round 5 and have no known exception (see the engine-selection item below).
- **Filtered enlargement** (`halftoneFilterEnlarge`, capture `halftone-filtered.json.gz`, generator `scripts/gdi-fixtures/generate-halftone-filtered.ts`, tests `halftone-filtered.fixture.test.ts`): the 2x branch (sharpen, then a 3:1 interpolation vertical-first with the intermediate rounded half up; the source extended by one replicated row above and below before sharpening, which fixed the two outer destination rows, 23 native column profiles in `halftone-boundary.json.gz`; horizontally the edge column is replicated after sharpening, padding it first was tried and differs) is now one of several measured engines. Impulse responses were not needed: a random level per row (or column) of an otherwise constant image makes one axis' response a plain weighted sum of sharpened levels, and a least-squares fit followed by an integer search over 13,104 samples per phase gave the unique integer weights. (1) Both axes enlarged by up to 5x, whole or fractional: three taps over the source before, at and after the destination pixel's source pixel, chosen by the length of the run of destination pixels that source pixel gets (the runs are `halftoneNearest`'s assignment, 1.5x alternates runs of 1 and 2, 2.5x of 3 and 2) and the pixel's place in the run; a lone pixel `(5,22,5)/32`, 2x `(4,12,0)/16, (0,12,4)/16`, 3x `(6,10,0), (1,14,1), (0,10,6)` over 16, 4x `(6,10,0), (3,12,1), (1,12,3), (0,10,6)` over 16, 5x `(13,19,0), (6,25,1), (3,26,3), (1,25,6), (0,19,13)` over 32, one rounding half up per pass, vertical first with the replicated-and-sharpened extension rows (all 38 non-dithered captured cases of `halftone-filtered` are exact, the three values at 1.025x on one axis that the old kernel table missed included: whole factors, mixed whole factors (2x3, 3x2, 2x5, 5x3), fractional, mirrored and one-axis; the 48 filtered 3x selection controls are exact). Read as a destination-space FIR over the replicated source these are `[1,2,3,4,3,2,1]/16` at 4x (an exact tent) but `[1,5,4,5,1]/16` at 3x and `[1,2,3,7,6,7,3,2,1]/32` at 5x, whose centre tap is smaller than its neighbour, which no tent or box-tent kernel gives. (2) Either axis above 5x, also fractional (5.016x is the closest tested): horizontal first, the four-neighbour sharpen clamped at the edges with no extension row, the 13-bit shares of `halftoneEnlargeTaps` (the kernel formula above; exact for every whole factor from 6x to 16x except the centre phase of 15x, which keeps its measured shares, and for the captured 5.5x, 6.5x and mixed 2.5x by 6.5x; 5.016x is 6 of 193,563 values one level off, 340/64 = 5.31x 84 of 216,240, 449/64 = 7.0x 22 of 378,507), an exact 2x axis keeping the linear weights (the formula is a level off there). (3) One axis enlarged, the other unchanged: only that axis sharpened with the one-dimensional `/4` kernel, interpolated with (2)'s weights and one final rounding; exact at 1.025x, 1.5x, 2x, 2.5x, 3x, 5x, 7x and 10x (three values at 1.025x were a level off with the old kernel table). (4) Mirrored blits are the exact mirror image of the unmirrored result on both axes (the probe's mirrored destination starts at the far edge, `dw - 1`; starting at `dw` shifts the image by a pixel). (5) Combined colour adjustments: the source and its extension rows are quantised with the ordered dither and mapped first (V1 of six orders tried; dithering the finished output, dithering without mapping, or adjusting before dithering all differ in 28,000 of 30,720 values), the pattern two rows further down than the replicated branch for engine (1) (a 66 x 65 phase sweep over noise sources found it) and two rows less far when mirrored vertically; the source is padded with its first and last rows replicated TWICE on each side and dithered at the pattern rows above and below (-2, -1 and h, h+1); the inner replicas are the extension rows the interpolation reads, the outer ones only feed the extension rows' sharpen (round 6, W7: the earlier "mirrored edge", which sharpened an extension row against the first source row dithered at its own pattern row, left a one-level residual in the outermost destination rows only; recovered by solving the native outer row of the 2x capture for the sharpened extension row, which fixed every one of 8 infeasible values at once when the row above was the edge row dithered at pattern row -2 and the row below at h+1, and no other row). Engine (2) sharpens its last row against one further dithered row. The ten dithered cases are exact (0 values, pinned in `halftone-filtered.fixture.test.ts`) after the round-6 extension-row fix and the chroma tie table; before the tie table they kept 0.1% to 0.4% of channel values one level off (50 to 573 values of 7,680 to 141,120 per case), and with a native colorfulness cube standing in for the chroma formula every one of them was already exact, so that remainder was the chroma stage's tie-breaking (see the colour-adjustment item above and the chroma-tie item below), not the filter. Hypotheses rejected: the kernel table for 3x to 5x (3x phase 0 weighs the neighbour 0.329, native 0.375), horizontal first at 4x (29,987 of 122,880 values off), vertical first at 6x (72,077 of 276,480 off), the extension row at 6x (1,809 top and 1,849 bottom values off), a cumulative round-up of a tent for 3x, per-axis `/4` sharpening for fractional both-axis ratios (at least 44,000 of 48,000 values off at 2.5x), and the table with either pass order for fractional ratios up to 5x. The fractional ratios up to 5x were the last open piece and were closed by changing the question: 1-D fits at 3/2, 4/3, 5/3, 5/4, 7/4, 7/3, 7/2, 9/4, 9/5, 11/3 gave a handful of weight sets (the whole factors' rows, the lone-pixel row `(5,22,5)/32` and, apparently, positions quantised to a grid that no fixed offset and step reproduced: 3/2 seemed to need a quarter-pixel grid, 5/2 an eighth-pixel grid shifted by 0.1 pixel); reading which destination pixels used which set showed that the choice is not a position at all but the run structure: with a ratio of 1.3 the destination pixels per source pixel go 1, 2, 1, 1, 2, 1, 1, 1, 2, 1 and a source pixel with two destination pixels uses the 2x pair, one with three the 3x triple, a lone one the smoothing row. The ten captured fractional cases (1.025x to 4.98x, 2x by 1.025x, and 2.5x mirrored on either axis) are exact, as were about thirty further ratios checked in the session (generator figure); the closest approach to the 5x switch is 4.98x (319 x 200 is exact, 5.016x is already the other engine). Closed in round 5: mixed enlarge-and-reduce stretches of filtered sources (see the engine-selection item below) and the table's one-share misses for fractions above 5x (288 of 193,563 values at 5.016x became 6 with the kernel formula). Closed in round 5 as well: a dithered adjustment on a stretch that keeps one axis (see the engine-selection item below). The sample counts, the counts of the rejected hypotheses, the 5.016x figure and the table-miss phase counts above are generator figures from the 8 October 2026 sessions (scratch scripts, not committed); the captured cases and one-axis profiles are pinned by `halftone-filtered.fixture.test.ts`.
- **Mixed enlarge-and-reduce engine selection and the despeckle size bands** (`halftoneMixedEngine`, capture `halftone-mixed-engine.json.gz` of 1,101 native stretches, generator `scripts/gdi-fixtures/generate-halftone-mixed-engine.ts`, test `halftone-mixed-engine.fixture.test.ts`, 8 October 2026). Found by labelling each native stretch of a source grid (3, 27, 100 and about 400 colours, noise, smooth) with the engine whose prediction it matched, then by boundary sweeps: filtered sources (more than 2,304 pixels; noise, smooth, 400 colours) run the reduce, sharpen, enlarge sequence for every mixed destination (84 of 84 noise stretches of 80 x 80 exact, enlargements 1.25x to 6.5x against reductions 0.9x to 0.25x, where before only the reductions by 2x or more were); every source runs it when the shrinking axis loses 1.5 pixels per pixel (`3 * dst + 2 <= 2 * src`, the +2 found by 100 to 66 qualifying while 50 to 33 (both 1.515) does not, and 64 to 42 against 64 to 43) or when the destination has fewer pixels than the source (22 stretches at -2, 8 at -1 pixels qualify, 0 and +1 do not); a source of up to 2,304 pixels is despeckled first when only the area rule qualified it (zero residual in the 40 x 40 sweeps, where a 3-colour 50 x 30 stretch of a 40 x 40 source is 1,802 of 4,500 values off without it). Hypotheses rejected: a threshold on the reduction factor alone (the 1.5 cap holds only for enlargements of 1.5x and more, below that the area rule is the boundary: 64 to 51 qualifies with an enlargement of 1.25, 64 to 52 does not), a threshold on destination area, the intermediate image size, and the colour count (the 3 to 100 colour sources change engine at the same destinations). The same session found that the replicated branch's despeckle is absent for sources of 2,305 to 16,384 pixels (checker-dense sources of 47 x 49 = 2,303, 48 x 48 = 2,304, 49 x 48, 55 x 42, 64 x 64, 100 x 100, 127 x 129 = 16,383 and 128 x 128 = 16,384 pixels: only the first two despeckled; 128 x 129 = 16,512, 8 x 2,049, 100 x 164 and 300 x 60 despeckled again), which removed a 0.2% mismatch of every mid-size replicated enlargement of a few-colour source (72 x 72 to 144 x 144, 188 of 62,208 values off before). Results: engine grid 280 of 280, 1.5x boundary 40 of 40, size bands 23 of 23, both-axes reductions 120 of 120 (98 before), area boundary 156 of 160 (28 before the engine selection, 99 with the old kernel table), held-out random stretches 154 of 160 (69 before, 113 with the old kernel table); no capture that was exact became inexact. Reductions of both axes of few-colour sources (a 3-colour 37 x 11 to 36 x 8 was 382 of 864 values and up to 63 levels off) were another consequence of the missing despeckle: a uniform reduction despeckles first when the source takes the replicated branch (up to 2,304 pixels always, by the scan above 16,384, never in between) and neither axis loses 1.5 pixels per pixel (the reduction by 2x of the checker fixtures does not; rule A, the alternative 'not both axes capped' broke 10 of 12 small one-axis-capped cases and a 'despeckle every replicated-band reduction' variant broke the 0.5x checker fixtures and the 27-colour sources above 16,384 pixels, which take the filtered verdict and are not despeckled). The remaining ten stretches differ by one level in 37 channel values: boundaries within a few thousandths of a share, the float-level noise of the kernel item. One-axis reductions, found while testing the selection at reducing scales: a reduction of one axis with the other kept (100 x 100 to 100 x 60, 99% of random values wrong) had no engine at all and fell through to a nearest-pixel path. A first guess fixed it: reduce with the 13-bit shares, unrounded, and sharpen along the reduced axis only with the one-dimensional kernel (variants rejected on the same 4 random-data captures: no sharpen 17,744 of 18,000 values off, the 4-neighbour kernel 16,154, sharpening the kept axis instead 16,582); it was then exact on 900 random stretches (6 kinds, 3 size bands, 4 mirrorings) once the both-axes despeckle rule above was applied to it (without it every checker-dense and few-colour source with an axis losing less than 1.5 pixels per pixel was wrong). Under a dithered colorfulness adjustment: a reduction that dithers the source first misses 2,677 of 3,456 values (best pattern phase of the 66 x 65), one that reduces first and dithers at destination coordinates 10 (at the default phase), and an enlargement the other way round (source dithered at source coordinates, then the filter: 16 of 13,824); the last row of a vertical enlargement needed the further dithered row (231 values off became 63), and a mirrored vertical enlargement counts its source pattern rows from the far end (5,487 became 66). Dither stage of mixed stretches: a native sweep of 64 x 64 and 63 x 45 noise over enlargements 1.25x to 4x against reductions 0.3x to 0.95x (both orientations, 84 and 56 stretches) labelled each by whether the source or the finished output is dithered; the boundary is the equal-area line (`dw * dh >= SW * SH` dithers the source, equal areas included: 64 x 64 to 128 x 32; 80 x 51 is destination, 80 x 58 source), where the old rule, an even reduced axis and a whole enlargement factor, was wrong for 26 of the 84 64 x 64 stretches (and for every one that dithers the source at 63 x 45). With it 96 captured stretches under colorfulness +40 (48 with gamma 1.5), every mirroring, reductions, enlargements and mixed alike, are 0.25% of values off (a mixed stretch was 32% to 45% off, up to 200 levels). Remaining at the time: the usual 0.15% to 0.3% colour-adjustment residual (one to four levels, the chroma stage rounding), which clustered at the first and last source row (round 6 closed it for the 11 settings measured natively, see the chroma-tie item in HALFTONE leftovers; other settings keep it). Wide sources and extreme aspect ratios: the 1 x 3,000 to 4,000 and 2,400 x 1 sources of the selection groups were 3% to 31% of values off before the one-axis reduction existed (68 of 576 stretches at 0.5x to 0.9x, 1x by 2x and 2x by 1x); with it, all 576 stretches of sources drawn from the twelve selection groups are exact, as are 30 stretches of sources 2,400 to 4,000 pixels wide or high and 2 to 16 pixels the other way, in all three content kinds, and 1,100 stretches at 0.5x to 6.5x of sources more than one pixel on both sides but one (17 of 219,384 values). The selection at reducing, fractional and wide scales has no known exception.
- **Enlargement pre-smoothing (despeckle):** measured from native captures and now exact on every black/white 4x4 pattern (also at the image edges), every pattern up to 5x3, and several hundred random black/white, grey, palette and noise images from 2x2 to 40x40 at 2x, 3x and 4x/1x2 ratios (`src/halftone-despeckle.fixture.test.ts`). Verified on 8 October 2026 (`halftone-despeckle-sweep.json.gz`, generator `scripts/gdi-fixtures/generate-halftone-despeckle-sweep.ts`, test `src/halftone-despeckle-sweep.fixture.test.ts`): 6,400 isolated two-by-two checker blocks (3,371 whose two diagonals are exactly equal under 4R + 8G + B and unequal under any other weighting) show that the brightness weights are 4, 8, 1 (every integer weighting up to 40, 80, 20 other than a multiple of (4, 8, 1) contradicts a capture, so 0.299/0.587/0.114-style luma is ruled out) and that a tie always pulls the top-left/bottom-right pair (all 3,371 ties); 500 checker-dense images from 3x3 to 48 pixels (ties, two colours with flipped cells, three colours, sparse flips, up to hundreds of overlapping checker runs) are reproduced by `halftoneDespeckle` byte for byte with no change to the algorithm. Nothing in the despeckle remains unverified for the replicated 2x path; only sizes above 48 pixels a side are not swept (the branch selection is a separate item).

### HALFTONE leftovers (round 6, W7, 8 October 2026)

- **The colour-adjustment residual was the chroma stage's tie-breaking, now measured.** Native colorfulness / tint cubes (`HalftoneColorProbe.ChromaCubes`, mode `chroma-cubes`, 11 settings of 32^3 colours, 98,304 channel values each, committed as the sparse flips in `halftone-chroma-ties.json.gz` with the cubes' sha256) differ from the u'v' formula in 16 to 356 channel values each (colorfulness +40: 201, 0.204%; +100: 356; -100: 16; tint 30: 63), all one level. The formula lands on exact halves for the hue-preserving gamut normalisation of a colorfulness-only adjustment (`255 (c - lo) / (hi - lo)` of the palette colour, a ratio of palette indices: 1,658 exact halves in four cubes, 52% of them rounded up natively) and within 5e-4 of a half for tints; Windows breaks those ties by float noise of its own. Rejected: the sign of the double error (48% agreement, a coin flip), the index ratio in float32 or double in four operation orders (always agree with half-up or at chance), every combination of float32 at the sites of the chroma stage with the 7-decimal and float32 constants (1,320 of 1,331 misses for the best of eight variants against 1,331), hill climbing on the 18 matrix entries and the white point against the rounding intervals (1,331 to 1,212, the violation does not close), and the dependence on the ratio alone (34 of 41 ratios are mixed within the same cube). So the cubes are tables: `emf-gdi-chroma-tie-data.ts` holds the 1,374 flips of the 11 settings (9.7 KB, the settings the committed captures use, +40 and (40, 20), (50, 20), and eight more) and `colorAdjustmentMapper` applies them to palette colours without an illuminant; other settings keep the formula. Effect: the ten dithered `halftone-filtered` cases are exact (50 to 573 values off before, now 0), `oneaxis-mode3/4` of `halftone-mixed-engine` 22 and 23 of 24 exact (2 and 2 before), `dithered-mode3/4` 48 and 48 of 48 (1 and 4 before; the last three, vertically mirrored enlargements with a reduced width, needed the further dithered row below the source at pattern row H - 1 - SH, the unmirrored case's rule), and `emfrec-halftone-ramp-2x-0p5y-ca` and `-0p5x-2y-ca` pixel-exact (0.068% and 0.108% before). What remains is every other colorfulness / tint (one level in about 0.2% of the channel values) and the 3 one-axis stretches of the mixed groups (kernel boundary rows), the data that would decide it being the native arithmetic of the chroma stage (an instrumented run) or a cube per setting.
- **Extension rows of the dithered filtered engines.** Solving the native outermost destination rows of the 2x colorfulness capture for the vertical-pass row (a dynamic programme over the horizontal pass, one value per source column) with the native cube as the palette map showed 8 infeasible values with the mirrored edge the engine used and none when the extension row is sharpened against the edge row replicated again and dithered at pattern row -2 (and h + 1 below); see the filtered-enlargement item above.
- **Kernel ties and the seven boundaries.** The 110 "tie rows" (`(2x + 1) n` divisible by `N`) and the "centre phase of 15x" are not converter defects: a destination row whose centre is exactly on a source pixel's edge has halves of exactly 4096 shares, so the output is `(s2 + s3) / 2 + delta` with a small tail term, and the floor of the half-integer case depends only on the sign of `delta`; the integer solver therefore could not pin the tail (the feasible weights lie on a ridge `(L, R) = k (m + 1, m)` and its pick, e.g. `10, 4086, 4091, 5` for 20 to 81 where the formula has `2, 4094, 4095, 1`, is arbitrary within it; the first observation was that the same row solved from two different image sets gave 9 and 10). The converter's rows reproduce every raw native value of the tie rows (`halftone-kernel-ties.json.gz`, 38 rows of 35 length pairs, 400 images each, edge-on rows at ratios 3.03x to 16.04x, the centre rows of 15x and odd-n centre rows; `halftone-kernel-ties.fixture.test.ts`), the same rows without their tails fail, and `(655, 6881, 656)` and `(656, 6881, 655)` give the same output for every 8-bit input (exhaustive), so `FILTER_CENTRE_ROWS` is gone. Of the seven interior boundaries, `29 to 46, row 19` is output-equivalent to the formula as well; the other six are real (the converter's row differs from the native output in 300 of 24,000 values with fresh data, the captured row reproduces all). The labelled near-boundary rows (`halftone-kernel-sides.json.gz`: 4,773 cumulative shares within 0.004 of a whole share in 400 pairs of each of three seeds, `halftone-kernel-sides.fixture.test.ts`) give the formula's side right in 4,570 (95.75%), with all 195 distinct misses within 0.0035 of the integer and 194 of them in mirror pairs (the native shares are exactly symmetric), i.e. the native cumulative share differs from the double formula by about 5e-4 shares and at most 3e-3 (2^-23 of 8192 is 1e-3). Rejected on that set: the exponents (a change of 4e-4 in either drops it from 4,570 to 2,643 or 2,358), float32 at any one site, any pair of sites, all but one site and all 13 sites (840 sets over two exponent-constant forms, three normalisations and three accumulation orders: best 4,526, all 3,841), float32 truncating toward zero (best 4,524), the boundary form `sum_k w_k clamp(a (j + 1) - d_k)` in the same sets (4,570), quantising the tap weights, the cover, the kernel or the normaliser to 14 to 32 bits (a spread 4,570 to 4,600 with no peak: the kernel floored to 23 bits is +30, +10/+6/+14 over the three seeds, its neighbours -6 to +12, i.e. noise, not a rule) and a linear-interpolation table of the kernel at 64 to 65,536 nodes in the distance or in the edge distance (4,570). What would decide the six rows and the 195 labels: the native arithmetic of the filter-table builder (an instrumented run or a disassembly), since no site-by-site float variant reproduces it.
- **Alpha under HALFTONE.** `StretchBlt` and `StretchDIBits` write destination alpha 0 on every branch (replicated, filtered, reduced, same size, with and without a colour adjustment: the destination is pre-filled with alpha 0x55 and ends with 0), and the source alpha never changes the RGB (alpha 255, 0, 128 and random give identical colours in 20 stretches under four plans, 80 runs): alpha is dropped, where COLORONCOLOR copies the nearest source alpha. The converter ignores source alpha and writes opaque pixels, as the opaque reference PNGs of the playback captures show, and its colours equal the native ones for the 16 HALFTONE stretches (noise colours, dithered and plain); `halftone-alpha.json.gz` and `halftone-alpha.fixture.test.ts` pin it.
- **5.5x and 6.5x.** Closed by round 5's kernel formula (the table's misses are gone: 5.016x is 6 of 193,563 values, 5.5x, 6.5x and the mixed 2.5x by 6.5x exact in `halftone-filtered`).

## Further parity rounds (3 October 2026)

Three additional parallel rounds ship four bounded runtime corrections: the half-open unit-scale Bicubic copy interval, high-quality interpolation when only one axis is scaled, preservation of explicitly supplied font projection/freedom vector words, and premultiplied path-gradient interpolation for uniform surrounds with unequal alpha. No existing per-case bounds were relaxed.

- Bicubic: 936 independent copy controls improve from 866 to 930 exact; differing pixels fall from 3,340 to 368. All 144 arithmetic controls remain exact; the positive phase endpoint was closed in round 5 (a power-of-two rule).
- High-quality sampling: 288 controls reduce differing pixels from 37,414 to 35,702 and maximum channel error from 64 to 3. Another 192 independent crop/mirror/alpha controls improve from 29,711 to 27,888 differing pixels, without per-case regressions. Remaining integrated-kernel arithmetic stays open.
- Font vectors: 448 new native outlines improve from 203 to 267 exact, closing 64 direct-vector controls. Another 2,560 GDI+ images, 384 opcode outlines and 3,072 opcode images pin the broader arithmetic candidates. Combined-rounding exceptions fail independent MDRP controls; the general projection/movement candidate still loses exact real-font or unequal-vector controls and remains excluded.
- Path-gradient steps: native paints a path gradient as `ceil(2 * hypot(w, h))` nested boundary copies. 136 new native captures (72 rows, 64 images) pin it; earlier captures gain 98,306 (opaque), 20,132 (translucent), 35,628 (uniform alpha) and 18,270 (isotropic focus contours) exact pixels; Blend and 50/50 focus playback becomes pixel-exact. Per-vertex surrounds, anisotropic focus, rotation and the copies' rounding were closed afterwards, and so were fractional-bounds tiles and most pixels on a copy's edge; the rest of the edge pixels remain open (see EMF+ gradients).
- Path-gradient alpha: 360 new native controls validate the uniform-surround correction. In the 120 independent uniform-alpha subgroup, pixels beyond one channel level fall from 145,136 to 16; controls within one level rise from 22 to 109. Varying surrounds and the prior 57 focus-boundary pixels beyond one level remain open. (8 October: varying surrounds are Gouraud fan triangles and closed, and all 120 of their independent controls have no pixel beyond one level (from 100 of 120 at the first closure); the uniform subgroup is now 110 of 120 within one level with 15 pixels beyond it, by the ceilings in `path-gradient-color-bounds.json`; the 57 focus-boundary pixels over 40 of the 252 contour controls are pinned in `emf-plus-focus-contours.fixture.test.ts` and still open.)
- HALFTONE: discrete nearest-source runs and quantised tent weights reproduce 2,112 response lines. Vertical-first integer intermediate rounding predicts all 432 new central RGB crops, including 48 independent held-out controls, and all 144 prior filtered crops exactly. Content selection is now known (see the HALFTONE section) and the measured filtered engines (whole factors 2 to 5, above 5x, one enlarged axis) replace the nearest-run model for filtered sources; the run model was shipped afterwards for fractional ratios up to 5x on both axes, lone and 5-long runs included (see Filtered enlargement above); alpha is unverified.
- References: all 101 expanded captures (94 and, from round 6, the seven `compat-playback-*` sheets) reproduce their original overlap exactly and regenerate byte-for-byte in isolated processes. Eight recordings require explicit recorded-frame/per-record playback. Four mixed-axis checker extents are now covered, leaving nine text comparisons open (below). The report retains 391/611 common-area exact comparisons and improves complete RGB ink coverage to 390/611, with no render failures or missing reference ink (8 October regeneration measures 393/611 and 392/611; after round 6 it measures 637/869 and 634/869, see Current parity priorities).
- Open extent candidates (`playback-extents-open.json`, `*.wide.png`, `src/gdi-playback-extents.fixture.test.ts`). Native `PlayEnhMetaFile` into a surface of the converter's own canvas size paints no ink outside our canvas in any of the nine, so no extent is wrong; the references are the clipped side. The report used the Windows fixture fonts (`arial`, `times`, `cour`, `segoeui`, `tahoma`, `micross` TrueType plus the raster `.fon` faces). The reference surface was the 480-px frame while the text runs on to x = 1,205 (the five Arial recordings: 21,426 native ink pixels beyond the PNG, 21,357 for `-ctnatural`), x = 959 (`textx-fon-fixedsys`, 13,652), x = 943 (`textx-fon-system`, 15,548), x = 675 (`textx-segoeui-cell-mono`, 2,023), plus one extra column in `rotate-text-25deg` (140). They are not promoted to `.extent.png` references because native playback does not reproduce their originals over the overlap, and only a reproduced overlap validates a capture: 503 differing pixels for each of the four non-natural Arial recordings (all in the 8-px first line, whose word gaps differ from the original; the four are natively byte-identical), 39,661 for `-ctnatural` (the original's natural-width advances are wider over the whole page), 21,624 (`fixedsys`), 12,850 (`fon-system`), 989 (`segoeui`, from y = 281) and 23 (`rotate-text-25deg`). Text glyph differences change pixels, not extents, so the extent verdict holds despite them. Two other large canvases validated and were added as `.extent.png` captures (94 in all, 68 exact; round 6 added the seven `compat-playback-*` sheets that paint at negative device coordinates, 101 in all and 71 exact): `emf-insideframe-rect` (556x293 from origin -47,-42; its recorded bounds are far looser than the drawing) and `emfrec-floodfill` (4,480x1,440 from its header bounds, whose width equals 2,560 + 1,920, plausibly the recording machine's virtual desktop but unverified; its playback returns FALSE natively, at the original size too). Recaptured here on 8 October 2026 (`src/gdi-text-regeneration.fixture.test.ts`, `text-recorded-advance.json`, `environment-text-recorded-advance.json`, `environment-text-raster-regen.json`; the generator gained `-OutDir` so regeneration goes to a scratch directory first): seven of the nine regenerate their original PNG pixel for pixel, so the font set was not the cause; no reference was replaced. The Arial differences (503 and 39,661 pixels) are the recording, not the converter: the EMF's dx array holds the ANTIALIASED_QUALITY widths (0 of 540 glyphs differ) while a direct drawing uses its own quality's widths (4 of 540 glyphs differ for qualities 0, 1, 2 and 5; 105 for ClearType natural), and drawing directly with the recorded dx equals native playback exactly (0 pixels), so the 8-px word gaps are not ours. Explained, not implemented (see also the rotated text below): the 989 Segoe UI pixels at the 36 and 56 cell lines are the playback's horizontal stretch of eyScale / exScale (1.000558 for these recordings) applied to the text, which Segoe UI's grid-fitting answers at nine of 57 cell heights (`TextPlaybackProbe.cs`, `text-playback-hinting.json`, `src/gdi-text-playback-hinting.fixture.test.ts`; direct drawing under diag(1.000558, 1) at the recorded advances equals playback for all 57 heights and for the committed sheet, 989 to 0). The threshold rule is now known empirically and pinned (`text-stretch-hinting.json.gz`, `src/gdi-text-stretch-hinting.fixture.test.ts`; native `GetGlyphOutline` of 23 glyphs at 18 faces x 65 cell heights x 82 stretches, no rasterisation): a height reacts when `M = round(32 E (1 + s))` differs from `M0 = round(32 E)` and, for Segoe UI, `|M / 32 - P| >= 15/16` (all 10,660 outcomes of cell and em heights), for Calibri and its siblings when `M` is not a multiple of 32 (see limitations.md). Rejected: a VDMX group boundary (the thresholds track the 1/32-pixel rounding of the exact em size `E`: every change point of the first 5e-6 sweep of Segoe UI sits on such a boundary, not on a VDMX ppem or its cell), a float32 `ceil` flip of the scale (the boundaries are the half-integers of `32 E (1 + s)` to the precision of the 1e-5 sweep), and an hdmx bypass (Calibri and Consolas have no hdmx and react at once; Arial has one and Segoe UI has one and the two differ). Windows' playback also reacts through the font's own `prep`: Segoe UI's disables glyph programs when GETINFO reports a stretched glyph whose x and y ppem agree, which is why reporting the flag to our interpreter reproduces the native outlines at every flagged height (60 of 60 committed outlines). Still open: why the faces split (the reaction is the font program's answer to an environment we can only infer: the stretched flag appears to be set by `|M / 32 - P| >= 15/16` for Segoe UI but by any fractional x size for Calibri, and Arial, Tahoma, Times New Roman and Courier New never switch), what the rasteriser hands the interpreter for larger stretches (at a few percent, 5% at 24 px, Segoe UI hints at a different integer x ppem, which is not modelled), and Georgia, Trebuchet MS and Microsoft Sans Serif, which follow neither rule. Not applied to the converter's output (decided in round 6: the stretch is the playing device's own eyScale / exScale, a ratio of the recording and target device sizes that a stateless converter cannot know and that is 1 on a matching device; the committed numbers are 989 pixels direct versus playback and 0 stretched versus playback for `textx-segoeui-cell-mono`, so applying it would move that sheet from exact against its reference PNG to 989 pixels off, loosening a pinned comparison, and the rule for the faces that split is still incomplete) because the reference PNGs are direct drawings; data that would decide the mechanism: the rasteriser's own scale arithmetic (the stretched flag and the ppem it reports per direction). Explained, nothing to change: the 23 rotated-text pixels are the dx array (a rotated font's recorded advances are the running 1/16-pixel rounded linear advances, not the hinted widths; 98% of 46,620 recorded advances follow the rule and every advance at 5 and 25 degrees does, the rest are half-pixel ties whose direction Windows decides by angle, up at 5 and 25 degrees, down for 35 of 404 ties at 10 and 53 of 403 at 45 degrees; `rotated-text-advances.json.gz`, `src/gdi-text-playback-hinting.fixture.test.ts`), and the converter replays dx, so the 31 pixels it differed from the playback were glyph shapes: the rotated glyph matrix, now fitted (one pixel left; limitations.md). Open here: the rule that decides the half-pixel ties (a scalar scale on the cumulative position does not fit: ties at 30 and 60 degrees go down only at positions beyond about 170 pixels), how a rotated run drawn without dx is advanced (a direct rotated TextOutW matches neither the hinted nor the linear nor the recorded advances at 25 and 45 degrees), and how a face that keeps its grid-fitting under rotation (Tahoma: 8 of 810 native points of the unhinted model) is hinted along the rotated axes. The remaining 54% of native points of Arial, Times New Roman and Segoe UI that the rounded-rotation model misses are off by one 1/64 pixel (the rounding order inside GDI's fixed-point products is not identified: single or double rounding of the unhinted outline, floor, ceiling and truncation were fitted by coefficient search at 80% at best). Closed as not reproducible by a stateless converter given other fonts, nothing left to do for the raster faces System and Fixedsys: the difference between this machine and the one that made the originals is the installed raster set (evidence: both regenerated captures are reproduced exactly with the matching fonts, 0 of 28,896 and 16,138 pixels differ, and the originals match with the fixture set). At 144 dpi Windows serves System and Fixedsys from `8514sys.fon` and `8514fix.fon` (20 pixel cells; the stock fonts are those too) as well as the VGA faces the originals used; the converter reproduces each capture exactly when given the matching font set (`gdi-text-regeneration`, `gdi-raster-mapper` tests, `raster-mapper.json`, `RasterMapperProbe.cs`; `textx-fon-fixedsys` became exact when the too-big stretch cost of `pickRaster` went from 350 to 340 per pixel, which also fits one more of this machine's 840 mapper heights, 822 of 840 against 821 before).
