Not verified: that a 96 dpi session is what withholds the 8514 faces (the stock fonts and the picks are consistent with it; this machine's display cannot be switched and no memory, enhanced-metafile or DPI-unaware device context here reports another resolution, so a capture at 100% display scaling would decide it). The mapper is structurally closer: a face is stretched by up to 8 vertically and at most 5 horizontally (`raster-mapper-wide.json.gz`, the seven families at lfHeight -130..-61 and 61..130 and weight 700 at -60..60, `RasterMapperProbe.cs`; every native pick above 5x has the width of a 5x stretch, e.g. System 20 pixel at 7x: 140 high, 45 wide), and costing the factors 6 to 8 at 1,150, 1,280 and 1,840 (from a hill climb that leaves the 822 of 840 inner and 818 of 840 bold heights alone) lifts the lfHeight 61..130 agreement from 631 to 919 of 980; fitted on the 490 negative heights alone the same three costs pick 456 of the 490 positive ones (340 before). Not closed: the remaining 61 of those 980 and the 18 inner heights above (all with equal horizontal and vertical stretch, so independent scales are not the cause). No cost of the form gap-below + gap-above + factor can fit them: a linear program with a free cost per pixel of gap (below and above separately) and per factor leaves 122 of 1,820 requests unsatisfiable, and the picks alternate in ways no monotone cost gives (System, cell height 94, 95, 96, 97: 96 = 16x6, 100 = 20x5, 96, 100). Rejected: absolute and relative gaps, quadratic and square-root gap terms and a width-aspect term (none lowers the 122). A free cost per (face size, factor) does fit the 840 inner heights, but with about a hundred parameters and no formula. Data that would decide it: the same sweep at 100% and 125% display scaling (which sizes each session offers), or the mapper's own penalty arithmetic.

- `codec-jpeg-{12bit,arithmetic,arith-prog-420-dri2,cmyk-photo}-playback` (round 6): the EMF header bounds are 0..279 x 0..179 and the frame is 4338 x 2784 hundredths of a millimetre, 280 x 180 pixels on this device, so the converter's 280x180 is the metafile extent. `CodecProbe.JpegPlayback` played the metafile onto a 300x200 magenta surface, and the 9,600 pixels right and below the metafile were that surface (all magenta, nothing else; no magenta inside the 280x180). Native playback was repeated on this machine (the four files regenerate pixel for pixel on the surface, including the 70, 1,974 and 534 one-level pixels of the three that decode, which are unchanged). The reference `.png` is now the 280x180 extent (`mf.Width` x `mf.Height`), the original 300x200 surface is kept as `.surface.png`, and `src/image-codec-native.test.ts` pins both (the surface margin is magenta, its 280x180 equals the reference). The recording `.emf` files were left as committed (a regeneration writes different bytes, same pixels). Draw stage of the three that decode (round 7): each recording draws the placeholder's own 37 x 19 source rectangle (not the decoded size: a 227 x 149 image is cropped to its top-left 37 x 19) as a DrawImagePoints to 260 x 170 at default (point-sampled bilinear) interpolation, so every residual is sampling. With the decoders exact and the CMYK table exact the residual is 22 pixels (arith-prog-420-dri2), 2 (arithmetic) and 7 (cmyk-photo, 1 level each). Isolated by feeding the native source PNG through a stand-alone bilinear model: of the original 113 pixels 67 interior ones were all ties of the 11-bit fractions that float32 accumulation of the source coordinate per pixel and per scanline (as merged from the sampling workstream) removes (113 to 35 in a scratch run, 9 interior left), and the rest sit in the first column where the right tap leaves the bitmap (partial alpha: the native result is the straight colour times the rounded integer alpha, which this experiment could not reproduce without breaking other fade pixels, so it is left open). Round 8 re-measured it: the 22, 2 and 7 residual pixels are not on the first column but on columns 57, 66, 158, 176, 185 and 194 and on row 147, each a column or row whose 1/2048 fraction of the stepped coordinate lies 0.006 to 0.014 bin below a rounding boundary (the stepped value is at most 14 float32 units low there); with the x accumulator rounded to nearest instead of down the same seven columns (and no other) cross the boundary and the three fixtures fall to 10, 1 and 4 pixels, but the same change moves 2 to 5 pixels in each plain Bilinear fixture (columns 141 and 150 of the 4.5x draw), so the rounding of the accumulator cannot be one rule for both; and a pure shift of the destination origin (-2^-14 in x, -2^-15 in y, the sign flip of the recorded 20 + 2^-15 and 10 + 2^-16) reaches 6, 0 and 7. A sweep of the recorded geometry (`ImageCoordinateProbe` at 37 x 19 to 260 x 170, origin 20 + 2^-15) matches the model to one draw (2^-10 pixel) on 258 of 260 columns of an interior row, so the stepper is right to well under a bin; what remains is a drift of a few hundredths of a bin that the 1-draw sweep cannot resolve.
- Every other report line whose rendered and reference dimensions differ (round 6; 166 fixtures that are not expanded captures: one pixel from the inclusive EMF bounds, the `plus-effect-*` 305x209 against 304x208, the pen, `gpx-*` and `compat-*` sheets whose bounds include pen width or control points, the text sheets): `generate.ps1 playback-extent-survey` (`PlaybackExtentProbe.Survey`) played each natively into a surface of the converter's own origin and size. 149 playbacks reproduce the original PNG pixel for pixel over its extent. The 17 that do not are: the three `emfrec-ca-dither-*` sheets (18,461 to 18,521 pixels: the colour-adjustment state of the recording process, closed as not reproducible by a stateless converter), the nine clipped text references of References, `gpx-pen-center-miter-aa` (7 pixels, at most 15 levels), `gpx-pen-lingrad` (13, while the converter equals the playback exactly), `gpx-text-lingrad` (3) and the two `textx-rotalign-*` sheets (1,454 and 1,354, where the converter is closer to the playback, 258 and 477, than the original is). In none of the 155 non-clipped cases does Windows paint ink beyond the reference PNG, and neither does the converter: the extra rows and columns are blank margins, so the larger converter extent costs no ink and the references are not clipped. The ink bounds of the converter equal the playback's in 162 of 166 cases (the three dithers, whose playback differs from the original, and `textx-segoeui-cell-mono`, one pixel short at the right and bottom, its known 989-pixel hinting difference). Pinned by `src/gdi-playback-extent-survey.fixture.test.ts`; the data is `playback-extent-survey.json` (generator figures). The eight comparisons whose reference is larger than the render (the six `textx-plus-*` sheets, 202x102 against 360x140, and the two `textpb-segoeui-*-q3` sheets) were played into surfaces of the reference size in a scratch run: the native ink bounds (for example 8,8 to 198,99 for `textx-plus-antialias`, 5,8 to 507,2880 for `textpb-segoeui-cell-q3`) lie inside the converter extent, so the reference excess is the probe surface padding (white), as for the JPEG sheets above; the originals are reproduced over the overlap (0 pixels, 8 for the two `textx-plus-singlebitgridfit`/`systemdefault` sheets). The captures themselves were not committed (8 MB of mostly blank PNGs); only the ink counts, ink bounds and original-overlap mismatches are.

## GDI path geometry

RoundRect controls now match native `GetPath` captures without exceptions. The compatible-mode cases also match at 1/16-pixel precision, including mirrored viewports and clockwise paths; `path-probe` regenerates both captures.
- Native `wide-path-probe` now covers 2,136 three-point paths with 2–64 px pens, all cap/join combinations and near reversals. All 128 round-cap/round-join fills match exactly in the regression test. Miter limit tests now follow native whole-pixel rounding of the fixed-point left-side vector (and its negation for the right side); 766 of 768 decisions around 384 measured thresholds match. The two retained decisions share a 7 px perpendicular error. All 228 square/flat-cap miter fills at 2/5 px now match exactly, including near reversals. Selecting cap/join arc vertices from the unrounded pen boundary point also fixes missed/extra endpoints. These changes reduce differing fills from 86 to 68, without changing any formerly exact fill in the capture. The half-pixel rounding of the flattened-pen perpendicular is a closed rule (no per-width table): the point of the pen edge between the support vertex and its interpolation partner, plus a half-unit bias along the segment, plus half a unit lower when the edge's extent in that channel is odd (half a unit higher on the edge that closes the pen), with a rounding boundary rounding up except in y for a segment running upward, and a reversed segment parallel to the closing edge resting on that edge's first vertex; vertical segments follow from the same rule (no horizontal bias). `flat-pen-probe` captures 1,176 native perpendiculars at fractional and wider-than-100 px widths, and a test checks every one. Two vertices resting on the same pen vertex at a flat-capped round join enclose no other pen vertex. All 2,136 native wide-path fills and all 768 miter-limit decisions now match.
- `wide-outline-probe` captures 72 polyline/ellipse outlines across four widths and every cap/join combination. All 36 ellipse outlines match every native vertex, including the duplicated inner triangles; `emfrec-path-widen-outline` now matches exactly (previously 1.184% mismatches).
- Native `wmf-insideframe-curves` now covers 100 ellipse/RoundRect/chord/pie/arc cases at 0.5–10 px. Fractional fitting and widening, plus scaling RoundRect corners onto the inset box, reduce closed-curve mismatches from 1.67–2.79% to 0.11–0.16%; arc retained 0.039% until the arc geometry below. Small boundary differences remain.
- Arc, Chord and Pie geometry follows native `GetPath` captures (`arc-path-probe`: 900 arcs under both arc directions, `arc-paths.json` and `arc-paths-cw.json`, and 260 arcs on a 40,000 px circle, `arc-precise.json`, where one FIX is 3 millionths of the radius). The model, which reproduces 900 of 900 counter-clockwise and 898 of 900 clockwise paths exactly (the other two one FIX off in one point; before round 4: 898 and 898; before that 662 and 234 one FIX off, 4 up to 5 FIX), and 191 of the 260 large-circle arcs exactly (187 before round 5, 182 before round 4), none more than 3 FIX off at a radius of 320,000 FIX:
  - An arc radial's angle is Windows' own approximate arc tangent: in each octant the slope (smaller over larger component) goes through 32 equal slope intervals with linear interpolation of the exact arc tangent at the knots, so the angle sits up to 80 microradians below the true one (a sawtooth every 1/32 of the slope; measured to 2 microradians). The direction is the radial over the box's half axes, measured on the unrounded logical coordinates (not on device-rounded points).
  - Points are those of a regular 128-gon inscribed in the unit circle (`polygonTrig`), linear in the angle between its vertices, so they sit up to `1 - cos(pi / 128)` inside the circle (the old table of 33 fitted node values was this polygon plus the arc-tangent error, mistaken for node errors). Arcs whose two radials are 3 degrees apart or less as angles in `[0, 2 pi)` (not across the 0 axis, and not the same radial twice) use exact cosines and sines for their points, but not for the control distance.
  - Round 4 (sample 206): the arithmetic is single precision. The 129 cosine and sine nodes of the polygon are float32 values, the angle over the node step (`b / (2 pi / 128)`) and the slope of the arc tangent are rounded to float32, and a point is the centre plus the float32 product of the radius and the float32 unit coordinate (`cx + fround(rx * fround(u))`, the sum exact: rounding the sum to float32 as well breaks counter-clockwise paths and was worse on the large circles). It resolves the rounding tie y = 3627.49993 of `curve-widen.json` sample 206 and the same tie in 108 of its 169 neighbouring chords (168 of 169 now equal native). Found (an analysis of the committed captures, not a test bound) by toggling float32 rounding at sixteen places of the pipeline over the 3,581 captures of `arc-paths.json`, `arc-paths-cw.json`, `arc-precise.json` and `chord-closing.json.gz`: the product, the node table, the angle over the node step and the slope are the places that helped. Rejected: float32 angles (the arc tangent's result or the final angle; 255 to 435 of the 1,800 paths broken), float32 interpolation between nodes or in the control construction (no gain, a few paths lost), a float32 sum with the centre, and a fixed-point unit coordinate at 2^-14 to 2^-31 (the start points of the neighbourhood fit at 2^-22 and 2^-23, but fewer whole paths). Not exact: two clockwise paths and one neighbouring chord (one FIX off in one point, where the unrounded value is within 0.0001 of a tie), and 69 of the 260 large-circle arcs (control points of pieces under a few degrees, where the model is too coarse for a radius of 320,000 FIX; round 5 explained the axis-aligned ones, see the arc-small capture below). Round 5 captured that data (`arc-small-probe`, `arc-small.json.gz`, `src/gdi-raster-arc-small.fixture.test.ts`: 624 arcs of 0.01 to 3 degrees on radii of 100,000 to 400,000 FIX, Arc, Chord and Pie, both directions). The controls of such an arc are not on the chord (a dozen FIX off it at 0.01 degrees on 100,000 FIX), and an arc that starts on an axis has its first control exactly on the start point and its second a third of the way: single precision, where the cosine of the sweep rounds to 1.0 and the numerator of the tangent-line intersection cancels to zero. The converter now solves the tangent lines of an arc of 3 degrees or less in single precision (51 of the 52 axis-aligned arcs are exact; 162 to 207 of the 624; the large-circle captures 187 to 191 of 260; none of the 900, 898, 600 and 1,500 other captures changes; applied only where the single-precision error is half a FIX or more, `6e-8 / sin(sweep)` of the radius, because below that one WMF pie differs at a rounding tie). Still open: 417 of the 624 (none more than 146 FIX off, almost all within 2 FIX from 0.5 degrees up), which no single-precision arrangement tried reproduces: cos(fround(angle)), the end point as the start rotated by the single-precision sweep, polygon trigonometry, sin(sweep) as the determinant, the determinant with double products or fused multiply-adds, the controls from the normalised radial vectors, a line equation instead of Cramer's rule, X = p0 + tan(h) t0; and perturbing the four single-precision inputs by two units of the last place leaves 23 of 96 small single-piece arcs unreproduced, so the missing step is not in the rounding of the inputs. Round 6 captured exactly that, native `GetPath` of arcs on radii that make one FIX 1e-7 to 1e-9 of the radius (`arc-huge-probe`, `arc-huge.json.gz`, `src/gdi-raster-arc-huge.fixture.test.ts`: 720 arcs of 0.05 to 3 degrees in boxes of 2^20, 2^22 and 2^24 pixels, radials 8 million pixels away, both directions, a fifth starting on the 0 axis). One rule is closed and the rest is a precise negative result:
  - **A clockwise arc from the 0 axis is a small arc.** Its end radial is at 359.x degrees as an angle in [0, 2 pi) and the converter took the 359 degrees between the two for a large arc (polygon trigonometry, a control point 353,943 FIX off at 2^22 pixels); Windows draws the 0.05 to 3 degrees it is. A clockwise arc is the y mirror image of a counter-clockwise one, so its radials are now compared as mirrored angles, 0 staying 0 (the counter-clockwise arc from 0 to 359 degrees and the arcs whose radials straddle the axis are large, as before: the 900, 900 and 260 arcs of `arc-paths.json`, `arc-paths-cw.json` and `arc-precise.json` unchanged). The arcs of the 2^20 box that match natively go from 22 to 42 of 240.
  - **Coordinates are single precision values.** Above 2^26 FIX every native point is a multiple of 8 and above 2^27 of 16, the centre plus the float32 product of the radius and the unit coordinate summed in single precision. Below 2^20 FIX the sum is exact: the nearest FIX of the exact sum fits 52 of 52 axis-aligned small arcs (a float32 sum rounded half up 51, half down 48: 399964.515625 goes up and 399915.484375 down). On the huge boxes the float32 sum floored fits 48, 36 and 44 of the 48 axis-aligned end points (2^20, 2^22, 2^24 pixels) and the exact sum rounded to nearest 48, 12 and 0: no single rule covers both ranges, so the model keeps the exact sum and the big boxes stay inexact (42, 0 and 0 of 240).
  - **The angle is not the source.** The converter's angle model reproduces the native end points to 3e-7 radians (rms 2.6e-7 at 2^22 and 3.8e-7 at 2^24 pixels, the quantisation of the float32 product), so the missing step is not a different arc tangent.
  - **The control offset.** For 256 off-axis arcs of 0.05 to 1 degrees the two controls are the unit-circle Bezier of the tangent-line intersection `X` of two single precision unit vectors (`kappa (X - P)`, the native `X` read back from the controls to 1e-7 or better), and the native `X` differs from the exact one by a shared offset: along the mid radial it is 0.14 to 0.24 of `6e-8 / sin(sweep)` rms, across it 0.66 to 1.05, the signature of two unit vectors whose lengths differ by an ulp (the length of one line's normal moves the intersection by that over the sweep). The nearest-rounded vectors of the converter are no closer to the native `X` (median distance 0.68 against 0.59 for the exact `X`, in units of `6e-8 / sin(sweep)`; the values truncated, rounded away, rotated from the start by a single precision sweep, from single precision angles or from a single precision sweep: 0.63 to 0.79, none below 0.59); perturbing the four components by up to three ulps solves 128 of 156 small-arc cases and perturbing the two single precision angles by up to four ulps 68 of 78, with no pattern in the perturbations. Candidate arithmetic in device space (a Cramer solve of lines at the radius, with the product before or after, double or single precision) reproduces fewer (10 of 39 axis-aligned and 6 of 39 off-axis, against 34 and 5).
  - **What would decide it.** The vectors one at a time: an arc series sharing one start radial and ending 1 to 64 pixels further along the same far circle (sweeps of 1.3e-7 to 8e-6 radians, where the intersection is `ulp / sweep` away and every control point is far off the circle): the intersections lie on the line `x . q0 = 1`, whose fit gives the start vector itself, and the same fit on the end gives `q3` for each step.
  - Each piece (the arc cut at the quadrant boundaries) is a cubic with end points `P0`, `P3` (the polygon points) whose controls divide `P -> X` at `kappa = 4/3 c / (1 + c)`, `c` being the polygon cosine of half the piece's sweep and `X` the intersection of the lines `x . P0 = 1` and `x . P3 = 1` (each a sag outside the true tangent). This single rule replaces the earlier anchored-piece and quadrant-interior models.
  - A whole quadrant between other pieces takes the full ellipse's own Bezier, and so does the first piece of an arc starting on 90 or 180 degrees (270 and 0 mirrored for a clockwise arc, which is the y mirror image) and the last piece of one ending on 270 or 360 (90 and 180 clockwise); a whole-quadrant first or last piece of the other kind, and an arc that is just that one quadrant, is built like any partial piece (as if a zero-length piece came first or last).
  - Arcs are measured as before for the radials, and a RoundRect scales its corner onto the drawn box from the unrounded device size: fractional extents under a cosmetic pen, whole FIX (truncated) under wide and null pens, replacing the earlier even-pixel rule, which matched only the 12 x 9 corner of `wmf-shapes` (`wmf-roundrect-corners`: 75 wide-pen and 25 null-pen corners at two scales; mismatches 500 and 813 pixels to 0 and 0). wmf-insideframe-curved-chord/pie/arc and the inside-frame Chord/Pie/Arc pixels are exact, and `wmf-shapes-scaled` fell from 0.158% to 0.008% (7 pixels).
  - `AngleArc` takes its angles directly (no radial, no arc tangent): the same polygon trigonometry and piece rule, with exact trigonometry for sweeps of 3 degrees or less, now give 599 of 600 native paths exactly (`angle-arc-paths.json`, in the `arc-path-probe` group; before: 71, with 86 having a different number of Beziers). An arc of less than 90 degrees is a single Bezier even across a multiple of 90 degrees (the earlier rule cut it there); from 90 degrees on it is cut at the multiples of 90 and ends with a zero-length piece on a boundary, as before.
  - Not reproduced: four paths with one control or end point one FIX off (arcs close to a full turn or to the 3 degree threshold, where the exact value is within 0.15 FIX of a rounding boundary), arcs of under 1 degree (a native point is up to 2 FIX away at a radius of 320,000 FIX, from fixed-point arithmetic that is not modelled), and the zero-length leading or trailing Bezier Windows emits where an arc starts or ends exactly on a boundary (dropped by the comparison).
- Flat-capped round joins no longer include a vertex that lies exactly on the second ray, and keep a steep segment's extreme-x vertex on the left side (flat/round 5,999 random paths: 86 differing to 1). A wide pen on a curve is widened with per-segment curve rules: the pen rests on a flattened curve's support vertices and round joins, a joint between two cubics takes the tangent of the cubic with the longer control arm, and a curve end's perpendicular and square-cap extension follow its end tangent. `curve-widen-probe` captures 320 native `WidenPath` outlines (80 Beziers, 80 each of arcs, chords and pies; flat, square and round caps; round, bevel and miter joins): the Beziers, all 80 arcs and all 80 pies are exact, and all 80 chords (round 4: the last outline, sample 206, differed in 1 pixel until the arc points became single precision; previously 148 of 240 arc/chord/pie outlines in 1,437). Rules found with `arc-cap-sweep-probe` (`arc-cap-sweep.json.gz`: 1,436 square-capped arcs over the end angle, all exact) and the curve captures: (1) at one logical unit per device pixel (`wholePixelDashVectors`; the 0.75 scale of `wide-pen-axis-probe` stays exact without it) a segment's square-cap extension is half the width times its vector over the length of that vector cut down to whole pixels (`floor(component / 16)` per component, the same measure dashes use), rounding ties away from zero; it shortens the extension by up to a fifth on tiny arcs, applies to a curve end's tangent, to a curve cut to a single flattened segment (its chord) and to every straight segment, and is zero for a vector under a pixel in both components (`emfrec-anglearc` and `emfrec-path-flatten` become exact); (2) the cap extension and the perpendicular come from the same tangent, and the join of a line with a curve end takes the curve's tangent perpendicular instead of a pen support vertex (chords and pies: 683 and 280 differing pixels to 327 and 0); (3) the cubic-boundary rule (both sides share the longer tangent's perpendicular) applies only where the earlier segment holds a cubic's end tangent and the later one the next cubic's start tangent, not between the first and last flattened pieces of one cubic. Rejected: the cap follows the chord of the last flattened segment (it follows the tangent for long arms and the chord only for a cubic cut to one segment); a fixed fraction of the half width (the shortening varies with the tangent in a sawtooth with a three-degree period that is exactly the whole-pixel norm); hidden extra precision in the arc (the 1,436 sweeps are reproduced from the rounded `GetPath` points alone). Other scales: `scaled-line-caps-probe`, `scaled-cap-sweep-probe` and `scaled-pen-widths-probe` show the cut is in whole logical units, rounded to the nearest unit (floor of the logical vector plus half a unit) as soon as the device vector is converted back, with the numerator the rounded device vector and half the unrounded pen width; a segment under a 0.7 scale extends 94 FIX where its exact vector gives 96, which the device-pixel cut cannot produce (device units 16, 8 and 4 were tried at scales 0.5, 0.75, 1.5: worse than no cut) and floor with no half unit fits 13 to 74 of 358 arcs (the exact vector 2 to 170) against 315 to 344 with it. Round 3 closed the first and last of those with no logical coordinates carried: the arc cut rounds each component to the nearest whole logical unit with ties away from zero (a logical component of -3.5 normalises by 4; the earlier half-unit-then-floor tied toward +infinity, which is the whole 4% of arcs: 358 of 358 under scales 2, 0.5, 0.75 and 1.5, 356 and 358 under the anisotropic maps), and the pen nib is the device width in FIX rather than a width rounded to whole pixels (the whole-pixel extent on an axis-aligned line is the perpendicular's rounding: 400 of 400 horizontal and vertical pens, 323 of 323 vectors at 0.7, 1.3, 2.5 and 10:7). Open there after round 3: dashed square caps (closed in round 4: a dashed pen under a world scale or anisotropic map lays its pattern out in logical units and measures every segment by its logical vector rounded to the nearest whole unit, the cut and the dash-end extensions then follow from `x0 + d * t / len` and the scaled extension; `scaled-dash-cap-probe` captures 1,416 dashed arcs under scales 2, 0.5, 0.75, 1.5 and the 1/16 and 2 by 1 maps: 95 fill-identical before, 1,416 after; the device vector's exact length, its floor to whole device pixels and its nearest device pixel all drift along the arc by hundreds of pixels), rotated arcs (first cap midpoint 92 of 358 and no exact outline: the rotated pen nib; closed in round 6, see "Round 6: the rotated GDI pen"), the two 1:2 arcs ending in a one-FIX segment (closed in round 4: the Bezier runs past its end point and returns, so the end tangent opposes the last flattened segment; native turns the last join along the tangent, a round wedge on the leading side, and so does the converter now: `curve-end-reversal.json.gz`, 1,440 outlines of 240 curves under three caps and two maps, 304 differing before, 0 after; a threshold under which native drops a short final segment does not exist: collinear final segments of 1 to 24 FIX after a long one, `tiny-final-segment-lines.json.gz`, 588 outlines along the thin axis of a 3 by 48 pixel nib, are fill-identical except 24 flat-capped reversals that differ by one pixel), and GM_COMPATIBLE direct drawing (device points on whole pixels, `c3:4` of `scaled-line-caps.json.gz`, 117 of 323 vertices exact; an EMF never meets this because its playback keeps fractional points, so it is a WMF matter). Carrying the exact logical vector for the cut was tried on those captures and rejected: the cut from the logical vector with the extension numerator from the device vector fits 209 of 323 at 4/3 but breaks the compatible 4:3 and 10:7 maps that the device-derived cut fits exactly (323 to 271 each), and using the logical vector for the numerator as well is worse everywhere (14 of 323 at 4/3). `emfrec-path-widen` (16 pixels) was not the widener: 200 native `WidenPath` outlines of RoundRects under a round pen are exact from their own `GetPath` points. The corners came from the RoundRect geometry of that playback: in GM_COMPATIBLE the EMF record holds the call's right and bottom edge less one pixel and GDI scales the corner ellipse from the call's box onto the drawn one (floor to whole FIX under a wide pen; `emf-roundrect-wide-probe` captures 1,800 native RoundRects, 450 per pen kind, and the wide plain and wide geometric pens are all exact; `emfrec-path-widen` is now exact). Superseded by the graphics-mode probe (`emf-roundrect-mode-probe`, round 3): the scaling above is what a GM_COMPATIBLE application sees drawing the call straight onto a device, not what `PlayEnhMetaFile` draws from the record it leaves. An EMF has no graphics-mode record (`SetGraphicsMode` is not recorded; the compatible and advanced recordings of the same calls differ only in the `EMR_ROUNDRECT` right and bottom, one pixel less in compatible mode), and native playback (`GetPath` read when the recorded `EndPath` has played, 800 shapes in `emf-roundrect-mode-paths.json.gz`) is the same function of the record's box in both: the corner ellipse stays unscaled under cosmetic, wide and geometric pens, and a null pen's quarter-pixel grown box takes the corner scaled onto it (floor to whole FIX). All 800 playback paths are matched and the six playback sheets are pixel-exact; the compatible-draw scaling was removed from the replay (the 450 direct-draw `GetPath` shapes of `emf-roundrect-wide-paths.json.gz` now pin how far playback is from it: 0 to 19 of 450 equal), and `emfrec-path-widen` was re-referenced to its native playback (the old direct-draw PNG is `emfrec-path-widen.direct.png`; 16 pixels at the corners). Round 5 re-measured the other compat-only rules against playback of a recording (`compat-playback-probe`, `src/emf-compat-playback.fixture.test.ts`: 42 sheets of 36 mixed shapes under six map modes and seven pens, plus 60 sweep sheets of 315 Rectangles and 315 null-pen Ellipses each at every fractional phase of ten maps and world scales; the direct-drawing PNGs are kept as `.direct.png`; on the six sheets measured the converter differs from the direct drawing by 49 to 251 pixels per shape kind). Two rules were wrong for playback and are fixed: (1) a Rectangle drawn with a cosmetic or dotted pen takes all four edges to `ceil(device coordinate)` (outline and fill; 5,040 rectangles, edges differ by one pixel at every fractional position; a null pen already matched, the integral maps were never affected), 133,868 differing pixels over the 102 sheets before, 259 after; (2) the null pen's quarter-pixel growth (Ellipse, RoundRect, Chord, Pie) happens whenever the four device edges are whole pixels, not only at the identity scale. Hypotheses rejected: growth per axis (when only that axis is integral: 342 against 192 pixels on the 2:3 by 1:1 sheet), growth whenever the scale is integral or the reciprocal of one (misses the integral boxes of fractional maps), growth under every axis-aligned map (451 against 192), and measuring the arc radials on the unrounded device points (4 to 44 pixels per sheet better on some, worse on others). Closed in round 6: the Arc, Chord and Pie radials under non-unit maps (1 to 4 pixels per arc; 259 pixels over the 42 sheets, 37 of them one dotted Chord at 0.7). The data that decided it is `GetPath` of the played-back arcs, which `PlayEnhMetaFile` cannot give (a path bracket around it stays empty, and the path is lost when the playback restores the DC) but a per-record playback can: `CompatPlaybackProbe.PathPlayback` (`compat-playback-paths-probe`) runs `EnumEnhMetaFile` over a one-shape recording, plays every record with `PlayEnhMetaFileRecord` (the recorded `SetWindowExtEx`/`SetViewportExtEx` records do apply) and wraps only the Arc, Chord or Pie record in `SaveDC`, `BeginPath`, the record, `EndPath`, a path read under an identity world transform at 16 units per pixel, `RestoreDC`; `compat-playback-paths.json.gz` holds 3,600 shapes (600 under each of the six maps, Arc, Chord and Pie, a quarter clockwise), each with the call, the record Windows wrote (its right and bottom edge are the call's less one device pixel in logical units: one under the 1:1 and 4:3 maps, two under 2:1, one or two under 3:2 by 1:1) and the native FIX path. The rule, found by scoring four candidates over all 3,600 paths: Windows measures a radial's angle in logical space, as the fraction of the record's box the radial sits at (`(x - l) / (r - l)`), and carries that fraction onto the device box it draws; the converter rounded each radial to FIX first, which moves it by up to half a FIX against the box. Exact paths per map before and after (10:7, 3:4 and 3:2 by 1:1 are the maps whose ratio is not a multiple of 1/16): 1:1, 4:3 and 2:1 600 and 600; 10:7 175 to 600; 3:4 213 to 600; 3:2 by 1:1 353 to 600 (2,541 to 3,600 of 3,600; `src/emf-compat-playback-paths.fixture.test.ts`). Rejected, with the same scoring: the unrounded device radial (258, 264 and 434 of 600 on the three maps: better than FIX but not the rule), the radial rounded to a whole device pixel by round, floor, ceil or truncation (2,734 to 4,551 differing pixels over the 42 sheets against 259: the box is not on whole pixels; measured on the sheets, before the path capture), the unrounded radial truncated, floored or ceiled to FIX (81 to 295 of 600 on the three maps), the logical fraction taken against the call's box instead of the record's (`r + 1`: 0 to 4 of 600). The sheets' arcs are pixel-exact now (259 differing pixels over the 42 sheets, 24 after, every one in a RoundRect cell: `compat-playback-r34-geosq9` 14, `r107-wide7` 3, `r107-cosmetic` 2, `r34-null` 2, and one each in `r32x11-cosmetic`, `r34-dash`, `r34-geo5`). The same fraction replaces the unrounded-device-point rule of the inside-frame arcs (`emf-scaled-paths`, 2,400 shapes, still exact). A chord whose arc flattens to one segment is a closed two-vertex polygon (joins, no caps; `chord-sweep-probe`: 1,044 native chords on 1 to 15 degree arcs fill identically, 248 of them (three flattened vertices or more) vertex for vertex, the two-vertex ones differ only in figure order, start and a repeated vertex, which does not change the fill). Round 3 (`chord-closing-probe`, `dash-neighbourhood-probe`): sample 128 is closed (flat caps: a curve's inner join takes the wedge between its two sides when it holds two or more pen vertices and the join is not between two cubics; 147 width-5 arcs of the neighbourhood and the sample are exact, a one-vertex wedge breaks a width-8 arc and the cubic boundary breaks curve-widen sample 193, so those two conditions are the measured ones), sample 206 (closed in round 4) was not the widener (native `GetPath` of the chord widens exactly from its own points in 5,820 of 5,841 captures; our arc starts a FIX off at a rounding tie, y = 3627.49993; 108 of 169 neighbouring arcs share it; float32 evaluation of the whole pipeline fixes them and costs two of 900 arcs and 5 to 13 large-circle points; round 4 found the float32 places that fix them without those costs, and 168 of the 169 neighbouring chords are now equal: see the arc geometry bullet above), and sample 117 belongs to a family (24 of 1,300 neighbouring square-capped Beziers, 1 to 3 pixels): the end corners of a dash that GDI scales by real over whole-pixel length, where the extension is an exact half (110.5 native 110, 3.5 native 4, 57.5 native 58, -62.5 native -63): four tie directions and nineteen double and float32 evaluation orders each broke some of the 18 samples with exact ties (closed in round 4: native computes the dash cut points and the extension in single precision, see below; curve-dash is now 300 of 300 and the neighbourhood 2,483 of 2,484). Round 4 captured 2,027 flat-capped dashed Beziers whose cut points sit within 6e-4 FIX of a rounding tie (`dash-cut-ties.json.gz`, picked by `find-dash-cut-ties.ts`; 349 rounding decisions are forced by the outlines). Rules: (1) a square cap at a scaled dash end extends by the vector component times a float32 ratio (half the width over the whole-pixel length), the product a float32, rounded half away from zero (the 110.5 gives 110 because the float32 ratio 104/176 is a little under, the 3.5 and 57.5 are exact and round away); every one of the 42 forced extension ties in `curve-dash.json` and the neighbourhood fits; (2) a cut coordinate is the float32 sum `x0 + d * (t / len)` (`d` the segment vector component, `t` the distance walked, `len` the whole-pixel length), and a sum that lands on x.5 rounds up when the component is positive and down when it is negative (exact ties: 132 of 133 forced decisions follow the sign of `d` alone; whether the cut ends or starts a dash and the axis are irrelevant). Fit: 321 of the 349 forced decisions (an analysis of the committed capture, not a test bound: each decision is the rounding of one cut that makes that outline fill-identical); 108 of the 2,027 captures differed with double-precision cuts, 36 now (largest 6 pixels; pinned in `gdi-raster-dash-cut-ties.fixture.test.ts`). Not explained: the 28 forced decisions that follow the exact double value instead of the float32 tie (the cut lies 9e-6 to 1.2e-4 from the tie and native saw it on the exact side; d positive and below the tie, or negative and above it; no function of the cut coordinate magnitude, the dash end/start, the axis or the real/whole-pixel ratio separates them from the 321, and seven formula orders with float32 at every subset of their operations do not either). Round 5 captured that data (`dash-lengthened-probe`, `dash-lengthened.json.gz`, `src/gdi-raster-dash-lengthened.fixture.test.ts`): 76 of the curves (the 36 that differ and every 50th) behind a straight run of 1, 64 and 512 whole periods of the dash pattern, ending at the curve's start so every cut falls on the same fractions. Native cuts the curve at the same points at every distance: the dash polygons of the curve are identical at 1, 64 and 512 periods for all 76 (an accumulated single-precision walk distance would move a cut by up to 0.016 FIX at 512 periods, against tie gaps under 6e-4), and the converter's per-segment walk differs in 36, 34 and 32 of them (the cases that stop differing do so while the native polygons of the curve are unchanged, so the difference there is in the run or its join, not at a cut). Accumulated distance is therefore rejected, as is a float32 walk position in the converter (variant with `t` and the remaining dash in single precision: 41 of 2,027 differ, from 36; a cumulative single-precision boundary position against the segment start: 74). The 36 stay with the rounding of the sum itself, where the 28 forced decisions that follow the exact double and the seven formula orders above are the open part; what would decide it is the native value of a cut itself, which no outline exposes: the item stays open at the 321 of 349 forced decisions, and since no outline exposes the native value of a cut it is not reproducible from outside. 21 of 5,841 near-complete chords (closing line under 8 FIX) keep one-FIX final segment differences (the two 1:2 anisotropic arcs that did too are closed: see above). A dash that starts or ends on a flattened vertex keeps its zero-length piece along the segment it was cut from (native joins it: flat caps 5 of 100 samples by 24 pixels to 1 by 1); starting a dash with a zero-length piece along the earlier segment when the cut lands on the segment start made two samples worse. Cap lengths use `Math.sqrt` of the exact sum of squares: `Math.hypot` rounds differently in V8 and JavaScriptCore and flipped an exact tie (sample 154).

  Round 6 also swept the compat sheets under mirrored maps (`compat-playback-mirror-probe`: `compat-playback-m<x|y|xy>-<map>-<pen>`, 60 sheets of the 36 mixed shapes under a negative viewport x extent, y extent or both, maps 1:1, 10:7, 3:4 and 3:2 by 1:1 and five pens, and `compat-rects-m*` / `compat-ellipses-m*`, 72 sweep sheets of 315 shapes at every fractional phase; the viewport origin sits at the far edge) and their paths (`compat-playback-shapes-probe`: 6,912 Arc, Chord, Pie, Ellipse, RoundRect and Rectangle records, 48 of each kind per map, 24 maps, four pens, half of them clockwise, each played alone inside a path bracket). Before, the 60 sheets differed in 399,930 pixels and the 72 sweeps in 150,507; now 410 and 0 (`src/emf-compat-playback-mirrored.fixture.test.ts`, `src/emf-compat-playback-shapes.fixture.test.ts`). The rules, each found by scoring the alternatives over the whole capture:
  - **The record.** GDI writes the box in device order (`l > r` under a mirrored x, `t > b` under a mirrored y), and the recorder inverts the arc direction once when the map's determinant is negative: a call under the default `AD_COUNTERCLOCKWISE` stores `SetArcDirection(AD_CLOCKWISE)` (the first record of a y-mirrored sheet). The converter read the box's own signs, so a reversed box undid the mirroring and a mirrored map drew the long way round the ellipse (3,200 to 3,500 differing pixels per Chord or Pie kind on one sheet); the map decides.
  - **Arc, Chord, Pie, Ellipse.** The shape is the logical one mapped point by point, with the roundings of the mirrored axes flipped. A mirrored y runs it the other way on screen and its vertical roundings follow the opposite of the recorded direction. A mirrored x takes the whole quadrants from the ellipse of the mirrored box (the horizontal controls round down instead of up) and draws the circle on the same centre with the horizontal radius `floor(w / 2)` instead of `ceil(w / 2)`: scored over 144 arcs under a mirrored x, the eight choices of centre (`l` or `r` plus or minus `ceil` or `floor` of half the width) and radius give 144 exact for `l + ceil(w / 2)` with `floor(w / 2)` (the same circle as `r - floor(w / 2)`) and 109 to 111 for the other six, the pointwise mirror of the unmirrored arc among them. With these, 864 of 864 shapes of each kind are exact in every mirroring and map under the cosmetic, plain and inside-frame pens.
  - **Rectangle.** The path is the logical one mapped (864 of 864 exact, start vertex and direction included), and a drawn Rectangle with a cosmetic or dotted pen takes every edge to `ceil(device coordinate)` in any orientation (the converter skipped a box with a negative extent: 150,507 differing pixels over the sweeps). The dot pattern of a drawn outline starts in device space at the right-top corner heading left, or at the right-bottom corner heading left under the recorded `AD_CLOCKWISE`, for every mirroring (the eight start-and-direction choices scored over the 12 dotted sweeps: only this one is exact for all four mirrorings).
  - **RoundRect.** The horizontal controls round down under a mirrored x (143 of 143 corners under x and 140 of 140 under x-and-y, against up in 142 of 142 unmirrored and 139 of 139 under y), the vertical ones follow the recorded direction XOR the y mirroring (552 of 553 left-edge measurements over the four mirroring), and the path is the unmirrored construct mirrored in x afterwards. The dotted RoundRects of the playback sheets (four maps) differed in 1,011, 786 and 980 pixels under x, y and x-and-y and now in 7, 0 and 6. The corner half extent is `round(hb * c / w)` (round 7, G3, see the geosq9 entry below): the mirrored RoundRects of the cosmetic and plain pens are exact under every map (144 of 216 under x and x-and-y, the other 72 being the inside-frame pen that was not mirrored until round 8; 216 of 216 unmirrored and under y), against 109 and 103 of 216 under x and x-and-y and 196 and 187 unmirrored and under y before.
  - **Wide pen under a mirrored x (round 7, 410 to 55 pixels).** The wide plain pen (3 units) under the anisotropic 3:2 by 1:1 map that mirrors x put a diagonal edge pixel of polygons, lines, ellipses and arcs on the other side (169 and 166 pixels on the x and x-and-y sheets; the unmirrored sheet is exact), and the geometric round pen 12 more on each. `compat-widemirror-probe` (`CompatPlaybackProbe.WideMirror`: 300 random lines, horizontal and vertical lines, triangles, quadrilaterals, ellipses and zero-length lines per sheet, plain pens of 3 and 4 units, the map and its x mirror, 28 sheets) isolated it. An unequal-scale pen is an elliptical nib (2 x 3 pixels for 3 units); a horizontal line shows the nib's ends: with the map unmirrored they are square, mirrored the end pixels are pointed on exactly the ends whose fractional device x puts the nib's corner outside the diamond (0.67 at the device-left end and 0.33 at the right end, 3 units; independent of the line's direction, 118 square, 52 + 32 + 23 pointed of 225 lines). That is the polygon `penPolygonMatrix` builds for a matrix that flips x (the diamond (+-16, 0), (0, +-24) FIX where the unmirrored map gives the flattened ellipse with (+-11, +-17)): the mirrored sheets (3 units) fall from 455 (lines), 536 (ellipses) and 637 (triangles) differing pixels to 3, 0 and 2, and `compat-playback-mx-r32x11-wide3` and `-mxy-r32x11-wide3` from 169 and 166 to 2, `-geo5` from 12 to 0 (`src/emf-compat-widemirror.fixture.test.ts`; unmirrored and y-mirrored maps keep the ellipse). The dots (zero-length lines; 98 pixels on each 3-unit `dot` sheet, mirrored and not, 196 for 4 units mirrored) and the 3 to 14 line-end pixels of the line sheets were one rule, found with `compat-dot-grid-probe` (`CompatPlaybackProbe.DotGrid`, `compat-dot-grid.json.gz`): native `WidenPath` of a `MoveTo`/`LineTo` to the same point at every sub-pixel position (half-FIX steps in x, 0.75 and 1 FIX in y) under the 2 by 3 and 2.67 by 4 pixel nibs, both signs of the x extent, plus one whole-pixel dot under fifteen other pens and maps (5,653 outlines). The nib is whole at every position except a dot whose rounded device position is a whole pixel on both axes (the sheets' `lx % 3 = 0` columns at whole rows: a third of the dots), where every nib vertex but the pen's first and its opposite (the top and bottom of an ellipse, or the diamond's) sits one FIX nearer the centre along each axis it is off the centre: (+-16, 0) is (+-15, 0), (+-11, +-17) is (+-10, +-16), the digital pens of 2 to 5 pixels and the flattened circles of 8 and 12 pixels alike (`Outliner.dot`). A line from a whole pixel keeps its whole nib (the 8 captured lines). Every `line`, `hline`, `vline` and `dot` sheet is now exact (28 sheets: 25 exact, the three polygon sheets keep 1, 2 and 2 pixels); no other native capture changed. Left over from the mirrored `compat-playback` sheets (5 pixels after round 7, G3: `mx-r34-geo5` and `my-r34-geo5`; the RoundRect corner under the non-dyadic maps was closed, see `geosq9` below; all closed in round 8, see the R8 entry after the G entries). (An inside-frame pen under a mirrored x or x-and-y was not mirrored for a RoundRect, 0 of 72 against 72 unmirrored and under y; closed in round 8.) The null pen's quarter-pixel growth of a whole-pixel box is part of native `GetPath` in a path bracket as well as of the drawn shape, but the converter applies it to the drawn shape only (pen 0 of the capture is excluded from the path test).

  Rectangular inside-frame native sweeps now cover 0.5–10 px widths and are exact; fractional transformed cases remain open.


- **Round 7, workstream G (non-text vector residuals).**
  - **SourceCopy gradients (`gpx-rec-compositing` 475 and `gpx-rec-compositing-aa` 645 differing pixels, both now 0).** The images were one level off only in translucent pixels (the translucent gradient cells; the half-covered edge rows and ellipse edges). Two native rules, each isolated on its own. (1) The straight channel GDI+ stores for a premultiplied ramp pixel `p` under alpha `a` is `floor(p * floor(255 * 65536 / a) / 65536)`, not the nearest rounding the sampler used: `gradient-unpremultiply-probe` (`GradientUnpremultiplyProbe.cs`, `gradient-unpremultiply.json.gz`) filled 700 random two-colour ramps (alphas 1 to 254) in SourceCopy onto a transparent 32bpp ARGB bitmap and read them back: the formula reproduces 49,000 of 49,000 pixels (a reciprocal of 15 to 24 bits fits equally; nearest rounding 6,269, the exact floor 46,564, which fails wherever `p * 255 / a` is a whole number: 27 * 255 / 81 = 85 is stored as 84). The rule applies to SourceCopy only: the same change on the SourceOver gradients broke `gpx-lin-alpha` (0 to 2,721 pixels), so `linearRampSampler` takes it as a flag. (2) A partly covered antialiased edge pixel of a SourceCopy fill scales the premultiplied colour, not the straight one: `p' = round(p * k / 32)`, `a' = round(a * k / 32)`, stored `unpremultiply(p', a')`. 62 of 62 half-covered gradient pixels and 121 of 121 edge pixels of the 128-alpha ellipse are exact (keeping the brush colour matched 10 of the 121); the gradient hands its own premultiplied pixels to `sourceCopyPixels`, a solid brush premultiplies `round(c * a / 255)`.
  - **`gpx-lin-sigma` (2 pixels, open).** Both pixels have a ramp coordinate one 16.16 unit below a table-fraction boundary (`u / 256` = 17126.996 and 19432.996); a global +1 on `u00` (`round` of -103837.78 taken as -103837) removes them without disturbing the other 15,998, but changing how `u00` rounds (truncating `v + 0.5`, ceil, for `u00` alone or all three coefficients) loses 6 to 17 of the 663 exact comparisons (`gpx-lin-gamma`, `gpx-lin-preset5-large`, `gpx-pen-lingrad`, `grad-linear-a30-*`, `grad-linear-skew-blend-*`: the `u00` of those are -88833.37, -425159.85, -241833.80, -775652.79 and -825268.13, all needing the nearest rounding), and evaluating the inverse brush matrix in float32 (three operation orders) moves `u00` by 0.07 to 0.16 the wrong way where +0.28 is needed. It is a sub-unit offset of the ramp origin that this rule set does not produce; left as it is.
  - **Per-vertex path gradients (`grad-path-triangle-*`, open; a candidate left out).** The 54 pixels of each tiled fixture and 13 of the clamped one are one level, on exact half-level ties (57.5 theoretical, 57.49998 computed). Scoring edge arithmetic against the 320 independent captures (`path-gradient-vertices.json.gz`, 175,640 painted pixels, 98.33% exact) found that forming the edge colour as `ca (1 - t) + cb t` in float32 rounded toward minus infinity (`down32`, as for the copies of a uniform surround) lifts every group (rgb, shape, quad, pentagon, rand, randa, randf, rotated and scaled sets) to 99.23% (2,905 to 1,345 wrong pixels; about 60 other arrangements of float32 operations, double and fixed-point forms peak at 99.25%, a fully float32 accumulating span). But it trades against the named fixtures: `grad-path-triangle-clamp` 13 to 17 and the four tiled ones 54 to 70, 74 or 70, because every remaining tie of `grad-path-triangle` is a tie that native rounds UP (cL = 105, cR = 115, 12 pixels, i = 3 and 9: 107.5 and 112.5, native 108 and 113, which no floored 16.16 step or float32 span produces) while the dataset's remaining ties go both ways (589 up, 514 down of the 1,618 wrong channels). The 5,383 exact-rational ties of the 320 captures round up 2,190 times and down 3,193, with no feature (edge denominators, parity, span length) separating them. The change is therefore not shipped; the rule that decides the up ties is the open piece. `grad-path-focus-ellipse-*` and `-triangle-*` (2 to 35 pixels, one level, symmetric pixel pairs on a copy's edge) are the documented slanted-edge ties and were not touched.
  - **Round 8, workstream R5 (EMF+ gradients; nothing shipped, three isolating experiments).**
    - **Per-vertex path gradients: the span stage isolated (`PathGradientSpanProbe.cs`, `path-gradient-span.json.gz`, `src/emf-plus-path-gradient-span.fixture.test.ts`).** 108 fan triangles whose two end colours of every scanline are exact in any arithmetic (colours in multiples of 64, edges 256 and 512 rows tall, so `t = k / steps` is a binary fraction and no model of the edges is in the way): 60 random colour triples and start fractions and 48 "flat" fans (centre 64, p0 192, p1 128) whose end colours are equal on every row. 2,974,721 pixels of 55,188 spans, none unpainted. (1) **Not a stepped delta.** Every one of the 2,631,233 pixels that is not an exact half-level tie of `cL + (cR - cL) j / n` has the nearest level of it (0 differ); the 16.16 span of the converter differs on 33 of them. (2) **Ties are a function of the span length.** A flat span (one colour, no delta) at exactly x.5 is entirely x + 1 when its length is a power of two (208 of 208), x + 1 at the first pixel and x at every other one when it is odd (1,516 of 1,516) and either of the two when it is even (71 and 1,181; no other pattern in 2,976 spans). Nothing that starts at the left colour and adds, or scales the difference, can do that. (3) **Candidate.** Weighting the two ends separately in single precision with every operation rounded toward minus infinity reproduces it: `l * down((n - j) / n) + r * down(j / n)` (the weights sum to less than one unless `n` is a power of two), with the edges as `a * w0 + b * w1`, `w1 = down(k / steps)`, `w0 = down(1 - w1)`. Flat spans: 70,612 of 1,444,812 pixels wrong against 241,536 for the converter's 16.16 step; other spans 10,511 of 1,529,909 against 4,105 (the ties of an exact step that native rounds up are a loss for it). On the 320 per-vertex captures it lifts 171,194 to 172,759 of 174,084 pixels exact (98.34% to 99.24%; every group improves, rgb to 99.8%, shape 94.2% to 98.4% and the random polygons 98.8% to 99.3%) and the five `grad-path-triangle-*` fixtures from 13, 54, 54, 54, 54 to 12, 50, 50, 52, 52 differing pixels (`report.ts` with the candidate in place of the module). **It is not shipped:** of the 120 per-vertex alpha controls (`path-gradient-color-bounds.json`) 14 exceed their ceilings (7 controls, #69, 71, 135, 136, 137, 141 and 143, that were exact are left with 2 or 3 differing pixels; the other 7 exceed theirs by one to four pixels), while 44 others tighten and the 240-control sum falls from 784 to 721 differing pixels. The candidate is kept test-only (`src/__fixtures__/path-gradient-vertices-candidate.ts`, pinned in the span test). Other arrangements measured the same way (captures exact / fixtures / alpha-control ceilings exceeded): a single precision step added pixel by pixel from the left colour, 99.33% / 17, 70, 70, 74, 74 / 13; the same with the weights themselves accumulated (`w1 += down(1/n)`, `w0 -= down(1/n)`), 99.37% / 16, 66, 66, 68, 68 / 17; the per-pixel `l + (r - l) t`, 99.08% / 11, 46, 46, 48, 48 / 14; the converter's 16.16 edges with the weighted span, 98.83% / 12, 52, 57, 50, 54 / 5; the 16.16 edges and span with the single precision step, 98.50% / 13, 54, 54, 54, 54 / 4. None keeps every control, and the fixtures and the independent captures disagree between them (the fixtures are 66 near-tie pixels, the captures 5,261), so there is a missing piece. What it is not: the destination format (the captures regenerated on a straight-alpha `Format32bppArgb` surface with SourceOver, `generate-path-gradient-span.ps1 -Argb`, are byte for byte the committed ones in all 273 opaque captures; only the 60 translucent ones differ, as stored), the premultiplication of translucent colours (nine single precision and double forms: 13 to 23 alpha ceilings exceeded for each; three integer-rounded forms: 120), the edges walked from the top or bottom vertex instead of in the order of the fan (734 to 1,897 against 562 wrong near-tie pixels), `c / 255` normalised colours (every normalised form scores below the levels). What would decide it: the interior ties of the spans that are not flat (14,326 in the synthetic set). Those whose 16.16 step is exact (`n` divides the delta in 1/65536 units) native rounds up 8,181 times against 524 (94%), which the weighted form, whose weights fall short, rounds down; those of an inexact step go both ways (rising: 1,264 down, 1,119 up; falling: 2,429 up, 809 down), which a floored step rounds down. A single formula for the flat spans (equal ends weighted separately) and for these (the delta added, exact when the step is) is the missing piece; it is not one of the forms above.
    - **Linear gradient origin (`LinearRampOriginProbe.cs`, `linear-ramp-origin.json.gz`, `src/emf-plus-linear-ramp-origin.fixture.test.ts`).** The experiment prescribed for `gpx-lin-sigma` (a blend table question) turned out to be an origin question, and the table is not in it: the brush rectangle moved in 2^-16 pixel steps (2,049 steps, three scanlines, four brushes) reads the ramp origin `u00` to one 16.16 unit at every step, because a pixel flips colour where its coordinate crosses a multiple of 256. The converter's rounded origin is native's at 97% of the steps of the 60 degree sigma brush (66 of 2,049 need one more unit, 109 pixels of 983,520 differ), 99% of the plain 60 degree one (26 steps, 59 pixels), 99% of a 30 degree one (19, 21) and all 2,049 of the sigma shape at 0 degrees. The correction is always +1, `du` and `dv` are right (a unit on `u00` repairs every pixel of the step), the fractional part of the unrounded `u00` is anywhere in 0.05 to 0.95 where it is needed (the 66 sigma steps need an offset of at least 0.83 of a unit and the ones that need none allow less than 0.0005, so no constant), the share of steps that need it follows `N / w * 65536` (3.2%, 1.3%, 0.9%), and the 0 degree brush, whose matrix has no translation to round, never needs it. Evaluating the inverse matrix in single precision (three orders) or the closed form from the angle matches fewer steps (1,838 of 2,049 for the best single precision form against 1,983 in double). So the table is exact and the open item is a sub-unit offset of the rotated brush's translation; `gpx-lin-sigma` (2 pixels) and `gpx-pen-lingrad` (13) stay, as they were, and the converter keeps the nearest rounding.
    - **Focus ramp on slanted edges: not run.** The copy edges of an isotropic focus keep the round 6 and 7 rules; the per-pixel weighted result above says nothing about them (the chain there is a ray and a quotient, already float32 rounded down), and the data that would decide the DDA question for them is the integer-vertex sweep named at the end of item (a) of the path-gradient section above, not captured this round.
  - **Round 9, workstream W5 (EMF+ gradients; the span and the edges of a per-vertex fan isolated, shipped, with one accepted trade: alpha control #137 is 1 pixel off).**
    - **Sources first.** Microsoft documents the brush's meaning only (blend positions: 0 is the boundary, 1 the centre, `PathGradientBrush::SetInterpolationColors`, https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/gdipluspath/nf-gdipluspath-pathgradientbrush-setinterpolationcolors); no Microsoft page found by search describes the rasteriser, its precision or its rounding, and a search for Microsoft patents on it found none. Re-implementations read as behavioural reference only: Wine's `dlls/gdiplus/brush.c` (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/wine-mirror/wine/master/dlls/gdiplus/brush.c) only stores a path gradient (colours as 32-bit ARGB, blend positions and factors as single precision `REAL`; the filling is in a file that was not read), and a gowemf pull request (https://github.com/mgilbir/gowemf/pull/35) notes that the boundary-to-centre parameter is affine inside each fan triangle. Neither says fixed or floating point; everything below is measured here, none of it is quoted from a source.
    - **The span (`PathGradientSpanProbe.cs` `RunGrid` / `RunGridColors`, `path-gradient-flat-grid.json.gz` and `-neg`, `src/emf-plus-path-gradient-centre.fixture.test.ts`).** The round 8 "flat" fans tied the span length `n` to the colour of the row (one `n` per row), so the interior ties could not be told apart. Here the right vertex is swept over whole and half pixels so that `n` and the exact half-level colour `v` (centre 64 or 192, rows 52 + 4 k, `v = 64.5 + k` or `191.5 - k`) are independent: 600 fans per sign, 477 of them used (sweeps wider than 250 columns have x-major edges), 667,306 pixels each. A rising flat span (`v > 64`) rounds up at `j >= 1` exactly when the odd part `m` of `n` divides `2 (v - 64)` (659,630 of 659,630 pixels; the first pixel is always up), which is the condition that `(v - 64) / n` is a binary fraction, and the single precision form `C + qa (n - j) + qb j` with `qa = qb = (v - 64) / n` rounded toward zero, each product and the sum rounded toward zero and `C + g` rounded down, reproduces every one of them (the weighted ends of round 8 cannot, in any of the rounding arrangements tried: the rows of an even `n` that are entirely up leave 270 of the 6,228 distinct (v, n, j) cases of the old "const" fans wrong). So a span is measured from the fan's centre colour, not from its left end. Falling flat spans (centre 192) are up in 630,406 of 659,630 pixels and down in 29,224; the toward-zero form is up in all of them, so those 29,224 (4.4%) are not explained (they sit at small `j` for large offsets and at most `j` for small `n`, and no combination of the weight forms, rounding modes and orders tried reproduces them).
    - **The edges (`RunEdges`, `RunEdgesX`, `RunEdgesMid`; `path-gradient-edge-stage.json.gz`, `-x`, `-mid`).** A vertical or x-major left edge of `S = 2 |b - a|` steps makes every odd row a half-level tie, and the first pixel of a span is that edge's colour. All three kinds are again measured from the centre colour `C`, in single precision with every product and sum rounded down: an edge from the centre `C + (P - C) down(k / S)` (rising edges round down, falling ones up), an edge that ends at the centre `C + (P - C) down(1 - down(k / S))`, an edge between two vertices `(C + (a - C) down(1 - t)) + (b - C) t`. 49,464 rows (vertical), 7,336 (x-major) and 12,584 (between vertices, vertical and x-major) are reproduced with no row wrong; the `k / S` that is rounded down at 24 bits is the only reading found (a 16.16 integer form is wrong on 3,500 / 392 / 2,049 rows, a nearest fraction on 9,476 / 1,212 / 2,215). A probe of this kind cannot see a one-ulp deficit on an edge colour whose exact value is a whole level (it rounds to the same level), which is why the right edges below are fitted.
    - **What the model gives (`vertexGradientColorAt` in `src/emf-plus-path-gradient-vertices.ts`).** Measured stages as above, plus three settings fitted to the controls and captures rather than isolated: the premultiplied vertex colours floored to 1/65536, the right edge's sum rounded to nearest with a toward-zero product for an edge that starts at the centre and the weight `down((S - k) / S)` for one that ends there, and both edge colours floored to 1/65536 before the span. The 320 per-vertex captures rise from 171,194 to 172,969 of 174,084 pixels exact (98.34% to 99.36%; every group of the fixture test improves, `rand` 98.8% to 99.4%, `randr` 96.6% to 97.7%, `shape` 94.2% to 98.4%), `report.ts` takes `grad-path-triangle-clamp` / `-tile` / `-flipx` / `-flipy` / `-flipxy` from 13 / 54 / 54 / 54 / 54 to 4 / 16 / 16 / 18 / 18 differing pixels (max 1 level) and `gpx-text-pathgrad` from 67 to 30 (the other 904 comparisons are unchanged, 803 of 910 exact before and after), and the 240 alpha controls fall from 784 to 613 differing pixels in total, 55 ceilings tightening. **Shipped on the owner's decision (16 October 2026), accepting that** control #137 (one of the 80 with a focus of (0.75, 0.25), which native ignores) goes from exact to one differing pixel (its ceiling in `path-gradient-color-bounds.json` is raised from 0 to 1, every other improved ceiling is tightened, `gpx-text-pathgrad` text bounds 6.28% to 6.02% and 0.70% to 0.10%), (38, 45), channel red: an exact evaluation puts the pixel 3.1e-5 above the half level and the model 7.6e-6 above it, native rounds it down. Keeping the quantisation stages that fix controls #3 and #27 and trying the others (premultiplied colour floor 14 to 20 bits, edge floor 15 to 18, step floor, result floor, span order and the right-edge variants: several thousand settings) never reached zero controls over their ceilings; the nearest, one control over by one pixel, is the model above. A 16.16 integer reading of the whole pipeline (edges as integers, floored shifts, span by integer division) is exact on fewer captures (98.80%) and was dropped.
    - **Linear gradient origin (`gpx-lin-sigma`, `gpx-pen-lingrad`; same 8,196 steps of `linear-ramp-origin.json.gz`).** The origin `u00` the converter rounds from a double precision inverse is one unit short on 111 of the 8,196 steps. Building the inverse of the composite `Scale(w / N, 1) Translate(rect.x, 0) Transform` in single precision (products and sums rounded down, division nearest) and taking the translation of the inverse as the origin is wrong on 27 of them (23 for the best per-stage choice: 9, 6, 2 and 6 for the sigma, plain 60 degree, 0 degree and 30 degree brushes), and puts two steps of the 0 degree brush, which the converter already has exact, one unit off, so the converter keeps the nearest rounding. The residue is therefore a rounding of the translation that depends on the order of the matrix products, not a constant offset.
    - **Left open, in order of size.** (1) Falling spans and the mixed-sign ones of the fan (the 29,224 pixels above; 1,802 tie pixels of the synthetic spans with a negative offset). (2) Control #137 and the right edges' fitted settings: an isolating probe would need a right edge whose exact value is a whole level and whose result reaches a tie in the span (spans of two to four pixels), read at more than one level of premultiplied alpha. (3) The focus ellipse / triangle residuals (`grad-path-focus-ellipse-*` 8 to 35, `grad-path-focus-triangle-*` 2 to 15) and `gpx-pen-lingrad` (13) were not touched: they are the copy-edge DDA of an isotropic focus on slanted edges and the origin above.
  - **`compat-playback-r34-geosq9` (14 pixels, closed in round 7, G3).** Native `WidenPath` of the sheet's RoundRect against the converter's outline showed the widener exact (feeding native's own unwidened `GetPath` of the four wide RoundRects, `compat-playback-r34-geosq9-roundrects.json.gz`, to `widenPath` gives all four outlines vertex for vertex) and the converter's path one FIX off at a corner end point (`hy` 85 native, 86 ours). **The corner rule** (`compat-roundrect-sweep-probe`, `CompatPlaybackProbe.RoundRectSweep`, `compat-roundrect-sweep.json.gz`, 107,520 native paths over seven maps that scale x, y or both, and `compat-roundrect-modes-probe`, `CompatPlaybackProbe.RoundRectModes`, `compat-roundrect-modes.json.gz`, 67,200 more over three graphics modes): GDI does not halve the corner's device extent. The half extent of a corner is `round(hb * c / w)`: the half of the rounded device box extent `H` (`ceil(H / 2)` horizontally and for a clockwise shape vertically; `floor(H / 2)` for the vertical extent of a counter-clockwise shape and the horizontal extent under a mirrored x) times the call's corner extent `c` over its box extent `w` as a **single precision** ratio, rounded half up, the corner clamped to the box. It is exact for all 71,680 one-axis records (every one of the four corners) and all 67,200 mode records, including 32 exact-tie records of the dyadic 21/16 map that only the float32 ratio breaks the right way (`31.5` falls to 31). That is why more than a hundred closed forms of the corner extent `E` topped out at 93%. The isolating experiments: the half extent does not depend on the box's position (sweeping the left edge by single logical units gives the same half extent for equal `H`; `H` takes two values per extent depending on the sub-pixel phase and the half extent follows `H`, not the phase); it is the same function in `GM_COMPATIBLE` playback under a map, in `GM_ADVANCED` under the same map and in `GM_ADVANCED` under a float world scale on an identity map (identical paths for 768 of 768 shapes in the first probe and for 5,600 of 5,600 at each of four scales in the committed one), so it is neither a compat-only effect, nor a 28.4 conversion at the map stage, nor a separate record-to-reference-device rounding (the playback frame ratio is 1 in the sheets); and the other axis' scale never matters. The converter takes it in `roundRectCorners` (a `ratio`), set by the RoundRect handler on the drawn box for an axis-aligned, non-inside-frame, non-grown shape. Result: `compat-playback-r34-geosq9` 14 to 0 and every unmirrored `compat-playback` sheet pixel-exact (24 to 0 pixels), the mirrored sheets 55 to 5, the shapes test 216 of 216 RoundRects exact with no mirror and under y and 144 of 216 under x and x-and-y (the rest inside-frame), 675 to 699 of 898 exact RGB comparisons in `report.ts` with no capture made worse. The earlier candidate that passed the exact device corner (`round(E / 2)`) had traded `r107-geo5`, `r34-cosmetic` and `r34-dash` for the fixed sheets; this rule loses none.

- **Round 8, workstream R8 (GDI geometry leftovers; 777 to 783 of 898 exact RGB comparisons in `report.ts`, nothing made worse).**
  - **Segments parallel to a nib edge under a mirrored x (`compat-widemirror-poly3-p3-m` 2, `-poly3-p4-m` 1, `-poly4-p4-m` 2 pixels, now 0).** `sheet-paths.ps1 -Lines` now also captures Polygon and Polyline records. 17 of the 300 polygons of `poly3-p3-m` (and 188 of 4,000 random ones) differ from native `WidenPath` in their vertex sets, mostly in fill-neutral loops; every one that differs in fill contains a segment whose device vector is exactly parallel to an edge of the nib (all 34 fill-differing 3 unit triangles of 4,000; none without one), so `compat-poly-widen-probe` (`CompatPlaybackProbe.PolyWiden`, `compat-poly-widen.json.gz`: 400 random and edge-parallel triangles, quadrilaterals, three-point polylines and plain two-point lines for each of 6 kinds and the 3 and 4 unit pens under the 3:2 by 1:1 map mirrored in x, 4,800 native `GetPath`/`WidenPath` pairs) isolated the tie. A segment parallel to an edge touches two pen vertices and `drawVertices` picks the one further along the segment's direction normalised to point rightwards (downwards when steeper). Under a map that flips orientation the other end is native's choice, for steep and shallow segments alike: with that flip, all 4,800 outlines fill the pixels native's does (1,770 differed without it; the 4 unit pen's edge-parallel lines 400 of 400, the 3 unit pen's 143 of 400, the random polygons 1 or 2 of 400). The widemirror sheets are 28 of 28 pixel-exact (`drawVertices`, orientation-flipping `matrix`).
  - **Rectangle stroked by a wide pen under a mirrored map (`compat-playback-mx-r34-geo5` 2 and `-my-r34-geo5` 3 pixels, now 0).** The 5 pixels are the missing round-join corner pixels of Rectangle records (`SheetPaths` of the sheets' 44 records, widened outline against the converter's, one pixel set per record: only the Rectangle records differ, by exactly the sheet's pixels). The widener is exact on the path native reports (`GetPath` of a Rectangle under a mirrored x runs left to right from the bottom edge: the logical Rectangle mapped point by point), and its outline depends on the path's orientation at a round join (the four rotations of the forward path give native's outline, the reversed path leaves two vertices extra), but the converter handed a wide pen the device-positive box, the orientation of a cosmetic outline (the dotted start-and-direction rule). A non-cosmetic pen now strokes the mapped box (`handleRectangle`).
  - **Inside-frame RoundRect under a mirrored x (`emf-compat-playback-shapes`, 72 of 216 under x and 72 under x-and-y, now 216 of 216 in every mirroring).** Native `GetPath` (`compat-playback-shapes.json.gz`, 144 inside-frame RoundRects under the two maps) against the converter, vertex by vertex: the control points were one FIX off in all 144 (the horizontal controls round down under a mirrored x for every pen, but the inside-frame branch took `ceil(KAPPA * hx)`), and the 24 odd-width boxes (12 of 72 for each) still differed in the corner extent: the width made even upwards, `w + w % 2`, is made even downwards, `w - w % 2`, when x is mirrored (`hx = round(c * (w - w % 2) / w / 2)` against `w + w % 2`).
  - **`wmf-embedded-emf` (16 pixels, not a hatch edge, now 0).** The 16 pixels are the four corner pixel pairs of the `RoundRect(60, 40, 180, 110, 20, 20)` outline, not the hatch fill (the hatch pixels all agree). The reference PNG is the EMF's calls drawn straight onto a bitmap, but the WMF carries the recorded EMF and Windows plays that: native `PlayEnhMetaFile` of the recovered EMF (`wmf-embedded-emf.recovered.emf`, byte for byte the embedded one) differs from the direct drawing in exactly those 16 pixels (the recorded RoundRect corner of a playback is not the direct one, as for the `compat-playback` sheets) and the converter matches the playback in all 27,960 compared pixels. `wmf-embedded-emf.playback.png` is committed with a `playback-references.json` entry (`emf`: the staged recording, new in `generate.ps1 playback-references`); `gdi-parity.fixture.test.ts` drops the 0.0006 bound for exact, `gdi-rotated-placement.fixture.test.ts` pins the 16-pixel direct-versus-playback gap.

- `PS_INSIDEFRAME` geometry follows `GetPath` captures (`GM_ADVANCED` for EMF, `GM_COMPATIBLE` for WMF, five and six scales, 1,200 boxes per shape):
  - The pen width along each axis, `wx` and `wy`, is its logical width times that axis' scale in whole FIX of a device pixel (not the isotropic width: 1,200 of 1,200 boxes against 664 of 1,200). A pen under 1.5 pixels along both axes (24 FIX) is cosmetic and leaves the box alone; a box narrower than the pen along either axis is drawn unchanged (EMF).
  - EMF Rectangle: the left edge moves in by `ceil(wx / 2)`, the right by `floor(wx / 2)`, the top and bottom by `ceil(wy / 2)` each. Every other box shape: `floor` on the left and `ceil` on the right (an odd width puts these edges on half a FIX; the Ellipse path is then the box [left, right] with its first half (points 0 to 5 and 12) one FIX to the right, exact for 127 of 127 captured odd-width Ellipses; the RoundRect's first corners follow the same shift except for the rounding of its four side points, correct in about two cases of three) and `ceil(wy / 2)` on the top and bottom. WMF (compatible mode, whole-pixel boxes): Rectangle left `ceil(wx / 2)` and right `floor(wx / 2)` of the pixel box, other shapes `floor` both sides, top and bottom `ceil` (WMF 1,200 of 1,200 boxes).
  - An EMF Arc, Chord or Pie measures its radials' angles on the box as given and carries them onto the framed box about its centre (the same rule as WMF; measuring them on the framed box leaves 0.7% to 1.2% of the pixels off). A RoundRect corner is scaled onto the framed box as `floor(min(corner, box) * framed / box)` from the unrounded corner and box extents (about 95% of the corner extents on `GM_ADVANCED` captures, 308 of 600 whole paths on the WMF scaled captures against 43 with the earlier pen-width model; the rest are a FIX off).
  - New capture group `emf-insideframe` (`emf-insideframe-{rect,ellipse,roundrect,arc,chord,pie,polygon}`: pens of 1 to 16 px, GM_ADVANCED, the Polygon as control; `emf-insideframe-scaled`: the shapes under a 1.37 world scale). The unscaled sheets are pixel-exact; the scaled one keeps 80 of 170,962 pixels (0.096% with the box rule alone).

### Round 6: the rotated GDI pen

Captures (`RotatedPenVectorProbe.cs`, mode `rotated-pen-vector-probe`): `rotated-pen-sweep.json.gz` (a 100-unit segment at every whole logical degree under the identity, the 30 and 45 degree rotations and three mirrors, widths 8 and 16, flat, round and square caps: 12,960 outlines), `rotated-pen-nibs.json.gz` (round-capped, round-joined 40-unit segments in four directions, widths 5 to 40, under 14 rotations and scales and 10 general matrices: 864 nibs), tests `src/gdi-rotated-pen-nibs.fixture.test.ts`. The round caps of a long segment show the pen's polygon vertex by vertex, which the zero-length path of round 5's `nib-angle-sweep.json.gz` does not (that outline is a dot: it differs from the long segment's polygon, e.g. side vertices (0, -96) and (0, 96) where the polygon has (2, -96) and (-2, 96) at 1 degree and width 12).

- **The nib is an Ellipse box.** The pen is the path of an Ellipse under the same matrix (`ellipseBeziersBox`, the rotated and skewed boxes of the compat Ellipse work) whose box is centred on the pen position and whose half edges are the images of the logical half width along the two logical axes, `(m0, m1) * w / 2` and `(m2, m3) * w / 2` in FIX; the first half of the path is flattened and the second half is its negation. Each component of a half edge is rounded to nearest with the tie moved down to a quarter: `sign(v) * floor(|v| + 0.75)`. Evidence: 864 of 864 captured nibs exact, under rotations of 10 to 300 degrees at 0.75x, 1x, 1.5x and 2x, shears, unequal scales, a rotation with unequal scales and three mirrors (the three mirrors flip the second half edge: 108 mirrored nibs, 6,480 mirrored sweep outlines and 1,440 mirrored miter corners are exact); the bias is sharp (0.20 reproduces 433 and 0.30 reproduces 448 of 476 nibs of widths 7 to 40, 0.25 all of them; components at fractions 0.2499 and 0.2509 fall either side). Before: 30 of 476 (mapping the logical circle's Bezier points to FIX and rounding them, which also left every other construction tried at 44 to 56 percent of the vertices: mapped identity polygon, exact Bezier midpoints, `floor`/`ceil`/`trunc` of either, a matrix quantised to 6 to 20 bits).
- **The perpendicular keeps the identity pen's interpolation, with two changes under a matrix.** (1) The half-unit bias follows the sign of the interpolated coordinate itself, opposite in x and y (it was the direction's sign; a nib can lie on the other side of the direction's normal under a shear or an unequal scale). (2) The further half unit of an odd pen edge is added for a support vertex in the first, explicitly flattened half of the nib and subtracted in the second (an identity pen has only vertex 0 in its first half there, which is what the old "first edge" test was). Evidence: 6,432 of 6,432 flat-cap corners of `rotated-pen-vectors.json.gz` exact (5,310 before; 5,608 with the nib alone), grouping all 12,864 coordinates by (axis, coordinate sign, odd edge, half of the nib, direction reversed) leaves a single bias that fits 12,862 of them (two coordinates of one group fit none), which is how the two rules were found.
- **Square caps** extend by the device vector over the logical vector cut to whole units times half the width (the rule under a scale, `cutToLogicalUnits`, which playback already sets for undashed matrix pens): 1,440 of 1,440 square-capped rotated sweep outlines exact (1,407 with the extension computed from the real, uncut vector, whose errors sit at the rounding ties, about 0.03 of a FIX; no additive bias fits).
- Results: the sweeps are 12,960 of 12,960 exact (flat, round and square caps, six matrices; before, the two rotations were 1,086, 0 and 1,062 of 1,440 for flat, round and square); `general-matrix-pen.json.gz` 504 of 504 outlines pixel-identical for the seven non-trivial matrices (matrices 0, 1, 5 and 7 had 620, 292, 554 and 416 differing pixels); bevel and miter joins under matrices 288 of 288 outlines (270 with 324 differing pixels with the nib alone; 221 and 2,055 before round 6).
- **Rotated arcs and the extension's ties.** `scaled-cap-sweep.json.gz` and `scaled-line-caps.json.gz` under the 30 degree rotation: 358 of 358 swept arcs and 323 of 323 segments vertex for vertex (356 arcs before the tie rule; 94 with the exact vector). The two arcs left had a start tangent whose square-cap extension was a tie (-81.5 and -82.5 FIX); native rounds a tie away from zero once the device vector is cut to whole logical units, as it does for the non-matrix extension (`squareExtension`).
