- **The miter limit under a matrix is tested in logical units.** The tip's offset from the vertex is converted back to logical units, each component rounded to the nearest whole unit (both sides alike; the identity pen's test rounds device pixels, up on the left and down on the right), and the corner is bevelled when its hypot exceeds `limit * w / 2` of the logical width. `rotated-pen-miters.json.gz` (`RunMiters`: 3,360 open corners of 20 to 175 degrees in steps of 5, limits 1.2 to 5, widths 6, 10 and 16, three rotations, a rotation with unequal scales and three mirrors): 0 corners wrong (137 corners and 7,112 pixels of the first 1,920, the non-mirrored ones, with the device-pixel test; exact, round, ceil, floor and trunc of the device offset, and the logical offset exact, ceil, floor, trunc and away, were all worse: 25, 44, 62, 83 wrong for the logical ones). 140 of the 3,360 outlines (corners of 160 to 175 degrees) differ from native by one repeated inner vertex, a zero-area spike. The 288-outline join sheet is exact for all eight matrices (the 18 outlines left at limit 1.5 were this test).
- Open: nothing in the long-segment pen or its joins; the zero-length dot of a pen under a matrix (round 5's `nib-angle-sweep.json.gz`: 15, 22 and 13 of 360 angles exact at widths 12, 24 and 40) is a different outline from the polygon and is not modelled, and the 55 repeated inner vertices above.


## Text

**12 October 2026 follow-up:** supplied-font EMF+ DrawString now decodes Unicode code points instead of treating each UTF-16 surrogate as a glyph. A generated CC0 font maps BMP and supplementary aliases to the same shapes; all 75 native Windows controls (five hints, three sizes) are RGBA-exact, up from 30. SVG preserves the supplementary character and one position per glyph. These controls isolate decoding and spacing, not shaping, fallback or color emoji. Current follow-up measurements are collected in [Native parity review](./native-parity-review.md).

- **ClearType x hinting, size-specific SHPIX, and y DELTAP after IUP (round 5):** the 3,720 captures of `text-cleartype-sizes` (generator `TextClearTypeSizeProbe.cs`, manifest `environment-text-cleartype-sizes.json`) show that native ClearType applies the x shifts that the Monotype-hinted faces (Arial, Times New Roman, Tahoma) issue with SHPIX, which the backward-compatible rule skipped, and ignores y DELTAPs once IUP[y] has run. Two rules shipped in `src/ttf-hinting.ts`. (1) Along x, SHPIX is applied for GDI+ ClearType (not for the GDI+ grayscale interpreter, whose 2,400 closed controls pin the skip) except inside the two generic per-size helper functions, recognised by their exact bodies (`MPPEM >= lo and MPPEM <= hi: SHPIX` and `MPPEM == ppem: SHPIX`; Arial `fn52`/`fn53`, Tahoma `fn55`, Times New Roman `fn52`); the mode-selected variants (the same body behind `RS 2` / `EQ`, Arial `fn85`, Tahoma `fn59`), glyph-inline shifts and Times New Roman Bold's stem-compensation function `fn32` are applied. (2) A DELTAP in y is ignored once IUP[y] has run, in ClearType and in grayscale; IUP[x] alone does not stop it, and SHPIX and MSIRP after the IUPs still move points. Exact captures per face of the 372 each, before the rules to after both: Arial 164 to 239, Arial bold 287 to 328, Arial italic 206 to 211, Times New Roman 116 to 147, bold 140 to 146, italic 104 to 112, Tahoma 190 to 251, Tahoma bold 272 to 274, Segoe UI 252 to 254, bold 284 to 285: 2,015 to 2,247 of 3,720 (2,193 after the SHPIX rule alone), with one capture lost to the second rule (Times New Roman Bold 17 px `2`, below). The 832 contrast-0 captures of `text-cleartype-coverage` go from 288 to 352 (SHPIX) to 368 exact (Arial 16 `I`, `g`, `1`, `4`, `8` join `v`, `0`, `2`); the 3,328 grayscale `text-coverage` captures have 2,464 exact (2,400 before: Arial 16 `S` and `1` under AntiAlias, 32 each). The six ClearType and three grayscale fixtures improve (`textx-arial-cleartype` 5.163% to 4.693%, `textx-plus-cleartype` 10.216% to 9.694%, `textx-segoeui-cleartype` 4.041% to 3.694%, `gpx-text-texture-cleartype` 3.093% to 2.802%, `gpx-rec-textcontrast-cleartype` 4.008% to 3.331%, `textx-plus-antialias` 3.210% to 3.157%, `textx-plus-antialiasgridfit` 2.547% to 2.527%, `textx-color-cleartype` unchanged at 1.868%; bounds tightened). Both rules are proven on private fonts, not only fitted. `build-shpix-fonts.py` writes four fonts (`shpix-a`..`shpix-d`, CC0) whose glyphs are one stem shifted by half a pixel with SHPIX issued from 19 kinds of code, with the function numbers permuted between the fonts (`text-shpix.json.gz`, manifest `environment-text-shpix.json`, `src/ttf-shpix.fixture.test.ts`). All 228 ClearType captures (4 fonts x 3 sizes x 19 kinds) are pixel-exact, and the outcome follows the bytes, not the number: the range helper and the exact-ppem helper are ignored, called directly or from a wrapper function; the mode-selected helper (with the matching mode applied, with a mismatched mode not), a helper with only the mode test, with no test, with an unrelated storage read, inline range test and inline shift are applied; so is the range helper with two harmless instructions before or after it, with strict comparisons, without its ELSE branch, and the exact-ppem helper with a trailing instruction. Native therefore ignores exactly two function bodies, as it bypasses the TypeMan Talk functions by signature; why is not known. `build-iup-fonts.py` writes `iup-a.ttf`: a tall stem whose program touches the top point in y, runs IUP[x] and/or IUP[y] or neither, and moves the point up a pixel with DELTAP, SHPIX or MSIRP (`text-iup.json.gz`, `environment-text-iup.json`, `src/ttf-iup.fixture.test.ts`); all 30 captures (15 programs x ClearType and AntiAliasGridFit at 16 px) are exact. The top row moves for a DELTAP before the IUPs on a touched point, after IUP[x] only, and for SHPIX and MSIRP before or after both IUPs; it does not for a DELTAP on an untouched point, after IUP[y] (alone or with IUP[x]), after both IUPs on a point already moved by SHPIX, interpolation or rounding. FreeType's backward-compatible rule stops DELTAP only after both IUPs; native stops it after IUP[y]. Evidence from real fonts (scratch analysis, generator figures, not committed): a per-glyph subset search over the SHPIX-x calls of 11 face styles at 8 to 30 px (94 ASCII glyphs each) finds, for every glyph where some subset reproduces the native capture exactly, which calls must be applied: Arial `fn85` 179 must, 0 not; Times New Roman `fn85` 90 and 0; Tahoma `fn59` 154 and 0; glyph-inline calls of Arial Bold 68 and 1; Times New Roman Bold `fn32` 60 and 0; against the helper functions Arial `fn52` 1 and 100 (the one is a mask artefact), Arial italic `fn52` 0 and 161, Arial Bold `fn52` 0 and 26, Tahoma `fn55` 0 and 83, Times New Roman Bold `fn52` 0 and 17; the same search over the y DELTAPs that run after IUP[y] finds 2 must-apply and about 320 must-block. Over all 24,614 ClearType captures of that survey (11 face styles at 8 to 30 px plus the 832 committed ones) 11,956 are exact before, 12,385 after the SHPIX rule and 12,616 after both (232 more, 1 lost); over 21,620 AntiAliasGridFit captures of the same faces the y DELTAP rule gains 168 and loses none. The loss, Times New Roman Bold 17 px `2`, is not the DELTAP: bisecting a private copy of `timesbd.ttf` through 87 truncations of the program shows the native image does not change at the final DELTAP (so native ignores it too), while the bottom serif's left tip sits one sample further left than ours (native y of point 2 is 14 to 20, ours 11 from the IP between points 1 and 23, unhinted 12), and the DELTAP our interpreter used to apply (+8, to 19) landed in that range by coincidence; the cause is in the interpolation chain, not found.
- **Grayscale lead from the SHPIX fonts (round 5):** the same private-font captures through AntiAliasGridFit (hint 3, `text-shpix.json.gz`) show native applying the x shifts there too, with the same two helper bodies ignored (the 19 kinds at 12, 16 and 24 px all follow the rule; the engine skips every x SHPIX in GDI+ grayscale, `HintedSize` with `grayscale`), but real Arial Regular does not follow it at 18 ppem and up: applying the rule to the 21,620 grayscale captures of Arial, Times New Roman, Tahoma and Segoe UI (regular, bold, italic, 8 to 30 px, AntiAliasGridFit; generator figures, not committed) gains 94 captures (Arial Bold 50, Times New Roman 24, bold 12, italic 8) and loses 95 (Arial Regular 18 to 30 px: `0`, `3`, `5`, `6`, `8`, `9`, `c`, `M`, `Y`, `y`, `w`), so it is not shipped. Forcing storage 2 to 0, 1, 3 or 5 leaves the same 95 losses, and reporting GETINFO bit 17 (subpixel positioned) in grayscale loses 2,443 captures. What would decide it: how native grayscale evaluates Arial's `fn133` and its mode table at 18 ppem and up.
- **ClearType dropout control (round 6, W1):** the ClearType scan converter applies the font's own dropout control (`SCANCTRL`/`SCANTYPE`) to its 6 x 1 samples per pixel, as the monochrome and grayscale ones do. Before, ClearType ran with none; now `rasterizeClearType` passes `dropoutMode(scanControl, scanType)` of the grid-fitted glyph (simple, smart, and the stub-excluding types 1 and 5 that Arial, Times New Roman, Segoe UI, Courier New (1) and Tahoma (5) select) to the sweep, and a font that never runs `SCANCTRL` keeps none (the private diagonal, opcode and vector fonts pin that). Exact captures of the 3,720 in `text-cleartype-sizes`, per face before to after: Arial 239 to 288, Arial bold 328 to 328, Arial italic 211 to 264, Times New Roman 147 to 254, bold 146 to 208, italic 112 to 250, Tahoma 251 to 317, Tahoma bold 274 to 330, Segoe UI 254 to 325, bold 285 to 326: 2,247 to 2,890, with 645 gained and two lost (`Arial 11px g`, `Tahoma bold 18px y`: a thin tail end of our geometry that native's lacks, so the old rendering matched without the sample the dropout rule adds; the test records them as known losses). Courier New 886 to 1,043 of 1,976. With the storage 8 rule that followed, the pinned totals are 3,059 of 3,720 (2,369 before the dropout control: 692 gained and the two losses) and 1,114 of 1,976 Courier New captures (951 before); the project owner accepted the two lost captures for the gains, and they are pinned as known losses in `src/gdiplus-cleartype-sizes.fixture.test.ts`. The sheets move down a little (the parity test's own metric, bounds tightened): `textx-arial-cleartype` 4.693% to 4.648%, `textx-plus-cleartype` 9.694% to 9.581%, `textx-segoeui-cleartype` 3.694% to 3.670%, `textx-color-cleartype` 1.868% to 1.863%; the grayscale sheets and `gpx-rec-textcontrast-cleartype` are unchanged. All of the gain comes from the sweep along the sample columns (a thin span between two sample rows at a curve end or a tilted bar end): switching that sweep off returns exactly 2,247; the row sweep and the one-sample span tweak change nothing. The stub rule is FreeType's (a dropout at the tip of a contour is dropped) with one change, found by enumerating the dropout decisions of every capture (the decisions of each capture are flipped over all 2^K combinations, K up to 12, and the combinations that reproduce the native image are kept; 3,072 decisions are determined this way, generator figures): the exemption that keeps a stub when its profile is a tall overshoot (`OVERSHOOT_TOP`/`BOTTOM` with a gap of half a sample or more) is wrong, native excludes those 13 of 13 times, which takes the FreeType rule from 22 wrong decisions (99.3%) to 9 (99.7%). The exemption is dropped for ClearType only: removing it from the monochrome and grayscale sweeps moves the private polygon bitmaps of `text-raster-mono` (SCANTYPE 1 and 5, all sizes) by 524 gained and 400 lost exact bitmaps and Georgia's AntiAliasGridFit images by 6 gained and 2 lost, so those keep FreeType's rule. Of the 9 left, 5 are stubs native drops that no profile feature predicts (the 11 and 12 px `g` tail of Arial, two Times New Roman Italic `B`, the Tahoma bold `y` tail) and 4 are tips native keeps (Arial Italic 9 px `n`, `y`, 12 px `4`, Times New Roman Italic 14 px `f`). Rules tried and rejected on the same decisions (mismatches among the 3,072 against 9): the contour-extent rule of the monochrome analysis with the extent in samples or pixels (36 to 499), both profiles starting at the scanline (19), either profile starting or ending (85 to 119), an exemption for gaps of half a sample (62); on the captures: the dropout mode of the font replaced by simple or smart for all faces (mode 0 loses 688 pinned captures, mode 4 loses 688, mode 1 loses 62, mode 5 loses 62), a dropout whose other candidate pixel lies outside the glyph box dropped (loses 248 of the gains), and no substitution of an out-of-box candidate (loses 137 of the gains). A fresh capture of `text-cleartype-sizes` in a new process differs from the committed one in 371 of 3,720 captures (Arial italic 128, Times New Roman italic 129, Tahoma 57, Times New Roman 38, Arial 19, none for Segoe UI: the diagonal-edge native-state difference again), neither of the two lost captures among them; the committed capture stays the reference. Fonts without `SCANCTRL`: native applies simple dropout control to them under ClearType up to 32 ppem and none above, as it does to monochrome bitmaps. Evidence (the private diagonal, opcode, vector-stage, `alignrp-*` and `msirp-a` captures; enumerating the dropout decisions of 148 captures at 16 to 32 ppem, best-effort over 2^K combinations where an exact match does not exist): every decision at 16, 23 and 32 ppem is a keep (442 of 450 keep) and every one of the 290 determined at 41 ppem is a drop; the `alignrp-*` captures (a sub-sample triangle apex and a zero-width line) are 60 of 60 exact with mode 0 or 4 against 4 without; over the 432 ClearType diagonal captures mode 0 at up to 32 ppem takes the exact ones from 91 to 217 and the differing channels from 30,686 to 23,177. It is not applied because 12 captures of the committed ceiling tests (`movement:16:5:1`, `projection:23:5:1`, `vectors:23:5:1`, three at 16 ppem of the first sets and six of the vector-stage sets) then exceed their ceilings by 1 to 255 channels, and a ceiling is never loosened. The cause is a corner decision: 7 of the 8 determined drops are dropouts in the column sweep at the bottom row where both profiles start at the column (an acute corner of a parallelogram stroke), which native drops in 7 of 18 cases (the drops have gaps of 0.16 to 0.36 of a row between the two crossings, the keeps up to 0.14 but for one at 0.33), and no profile feature tried separates them (profile heights, direction flags, gap, adjacency). Applying the default to the row sweep alone (smart mode) passes all 18 private ceiling tests and makes 56 of the 60 `alignrp` captures exact, but changes nothing in the diagonal totals (30,686 to 30,614 channels), so it is not worth shipping alone. What would decide it: ClearType captures of private glyphs shaped like one tilted bar end (a parallelogram) over a sweep of slopes and sizes of 12 to 32 ppem, to read the corner decision directly.
- **ALIGNRP around the IUPs (round 6, W1):** `build-alignrp-fonts.py` writes four CC0 fonts (table layouts: `prep` and `gasp`, `gasp` only, `prep` only, neither) of one stem whose program touches points or not, runs IUP[x], IUP[y], both or neither, and aligns the top left point to a reference with ALIGNRP in y or in x, against touched and untouched references and points (15 kinds, `text-alignrp.json.gz`, `src/ttf-alignrp.fixture.test.ts`; 120 captures through ClearType and AntiAliasGridFit). Native honours ALIGNRP in all of them: the 13 single-axis kinds draw one identical triangle, before or after either IUP, so the round-5 lead (a y ALIGNRP after IUP[y] leaves the point where it was) is not a rule of the interpreter. Skipping those moves in our engine gains six Arial captures and loses none, but the private captures contradict it as a rule of the instruction, and the Times New Roman Regular bowl glyphs (B, D, O, P, R, 0, 8, 9, g at 9 to 15 px, 28 to 103 differing channels where Arial is within a few) have another cause, found in round 6 by tracing their calls: the corner points are put on a reference by ALIGNRP inside a helper called after the IUPs, and that block runs because the prep stored `MPPEM <= limit` in storage 8 (next bullet). A second finding: GDI+ AntiAliasGridFit does not run the glyph programs of a font without a `gasp` table (layouts c and d draw the plain stem for every kind), while ClearType runs them whatever tables the font has.
- **Storage 8 under backward-compatible ClearType (round 6, W1):** backward-compatible ClearType (and the GDI+ grayscale text that runs the same interpreter) reads storage 8 as 0 wherever it is read. Before, it did so only inside two function bodies (`ctBypassStorage`); now every `RS 8` returns 0 (`src/ttf-hinting.ts`). The Monotype fonts (Arial, Times New Roman, Courier New) end their prep with `MPPEM GTEQ WS` into storage 8, a flag that the size is small (Arial: up to 17 ppem), and their glyph programs read it after the IUPs (`RS 8; JROF`): at small sizes they run a block of calls that squares off the corners of bowls (`SVTCA[x] SRP0 DUP ALIGNRP SVTCA[y] ALIGNRP` collapses a curve point onto a reference in both axes; functions 6, 37 and 38, identical in all three fonts) and shifts points with SHPIX, then end with the IUPs again; larger sizes skip it. Native draws the sizes where the flag is set as if it were clear: Arial `D` at 14 px has its arc points 4 to 8, 10 to 13, 19 to 22 and 24 to 26 put on single corners by our interpreter (a square-cornered bowl) where native's bowl is round. Exact captures of the 3,720 in `text-cleartype-sizes`: Arial 288 to 314, Times New Roman 254 to 308, Times New Roman bold 208 to 297 (the others unchanged), 2,890 to 3,059, with none lost; summed over all captures the differing channels fall from 10,250 to 3,974 and the absolute error from 663,667 to 214,279. Courier New 1,043 to 1,114 of 1,976 (bold 320 to 364, regular 215 to 242). The grayscale `text-real-glyphs` captures (AntiAlias and AntiAliasGridFit, 14 face styles, 31,584) go from 14,607 to 15,050 exact (Arial AntiAlias 757 to 813, Times New Roman AntiAlias 651 to 847, Times New Roman bold AntiAliasGridFit 920 to 967 and AntiAlias 743 to 887, no face loses), `textx-plus-antialias` from 3.157% to 2.734%, `textx-plus-cleartype` from 9.581% to 9.236%, `textx-arial-cleartype` from 4.648% to 4.491% (bounds tightened); `gdi-native-state.fixture.test.ts` pins the per-product and combined counts of the differing captures at 1,541 and 775 (1,519 and 766 before the storage 8 and grayscale stub rules; 1,530 and 773 with the storage 8 rule alone). Storage 22 and 24 read as 0 everywhere changes nothing (they stay limited to their documented function signatures). Hypotheses rejected: skipping only the three corner-collapse helper bodies (6 more captures, 22% less error, and 24 Courier New Bold and Bold Italic captures lost), the GETINFO selectors 256 to 4096 reported as set for ClearType (bits 15 to 19: 2,173 to 2,508 captures exact), and the direction of ties in the 1/16-pixel x rounding (loses 1,628 to 1,637). What remains open: why native treats the flag as clear (the MS rasterizer probably never sets it for compatible ClearType); a private font that stores `MPPEM <= limit` in storage 8 and branches on it would confirm that the read, not the prep, is bypassed.
- **Grayscale stub rule (round 6, W1):** the grayscale rasterizer (GDI `GGO_GRAY4_BITMAP` bitmaps and GDI+ AntiAlias and AntiAliasGridFit text) now applies the stub-excluding dropout types 1 and 5, which Arial, Times New Roman, Segoe UI, Courier New, Tahoma and most stock fonts select, as it already applied 0 and 4 (`dropoutMode` instead of `grayDropoutMode`, which is removed). The stub rule is FreeType's without its overshoot exemption, the one found for ClearType (the ClearType dropout control bullet above). Private polygon fonts: SCANTYPE 1 and 5 bitmaps at 8 to 32 ppem go from 2,344 to 3,106 and 3,147 of 4,096 (GDI grayscale and GDI+ AntiAlias alike; 579 to 778 and 782 of 1,024 above 32 ppem), 3,934 more exact bitmaps and images over the four stub sets and none lost; `text-real-glyphs` (31,584 grayscale captures) goes from 15,050 to 15,094 exact (Times New Roman only: AntiAlias 847 to 855, bold AntiAlias 887 to 893, italic AntiAlias 660 to 683, AntiAliasGridFit 960 to 963 and 967 to 971) with none lost. The round-5 figure that enabling FreeType's rule in grayscale loses exact real-font captures (including 64 committed Segoe UI Bold 12 px `2` controls) no longer holds, because the exemption was the cause. The monochrome rasterizer keeps the exemption (removing it moves 524 private-font bitmaps to exact and 400 away).
- **MSIRP distances under ClearType (round 6, W1):** `build-msirp-fonts.py` writes a CC0 font of one stem whose program moves its top left point in y, or its top right point in x, with MSIRP to the outline distance plus a literal amount (-2 to +8 px along y, 0.25 to 4 px along x), at the default CVT cut-in (17/16 px) and at cut-ins the program sets (0, 8 px along y; 0.5 px and 1 px along x) (35 kinds, `text-msirp.json.gz`, `src/ttf-msirp.fixture.test.ts`). Native ClearType moves the point by the literal distance along y whatever the cut-in (up by 0.25 to 8 px and down by 2 px, with the default cut-in, with 0 and with 8 px), and along x only when the amount is within 1/16 of the cut-in (exactly at the boundary included: 0.5 px under a 0.5 px cut-in is honoured, 1 px is not; 1 px under a 1 px cut-in is honoured, 2 px is not). Before, the cut-in rule (the outline distance replaces a literal that differs by more than the cut-in) applied along both axes; now it applies along x only. Real fonts: 15 determined decisions (enumerating the refuse/honour decisions of the 49 captures that reach the rule over 2^K combinations; Arial `k` 9 to 13 px and Times New Roman Bold `B`, `P`) are all refusals along x, none along y, and the size sheet is unchanged (3,059 exact), so the earlier rule was right where real fonts reach it and wrong along y, which only the private font reaches. Removing the rule altogether loses 15 captures (3,059 to 3,044) and raises the absolute error by 55%. Open: native also draws the thin wedge of the slanted top edge (narrower than a sample) in the y kinds, which this converter does not for a font that never runs SCANCTRL (see the dropout bullet), so the y captures differ from ours in one row at the wedge although the point lands where native puts it; and `x+16 cutin512` (a 1.25 px stem) is 129 levels of ink lighter than native's.
- **Courier New under GDI+ ClearType (round 6, W1):** the system Courier New is drawn wider than its outline by GDI+ ClearType (`TextClearTypeCourierProbe.cs`, mode `text-cleartype-courier`, `text-cleartype-courier.json.gz`, manifest `environment-text-cleartype-courier.json`: four styles, 14 sizes of 9 to 30 px, 31 characters, plus six sixth-pixel origin phases of `H E I T O n` at 16 and 24 px; 1,976 captures, none exact before; 886 with the two rules below, 1,043 with the ClearType dropout control and 1,114 with the storage 8 rule of the previous bullets). Two rules, both in `rasterizeClearType` (`src/gdi-font-engine.ts`, `clearTypeSpread`). (1) Before the 3-tap filter every 1/6-pixel sample row is widened: a sample is on when any sample from two before it to one after it is on (regular and italic), from one before to one after (bold and bold italic); a 0.67 px `H` stem at 16 px becomes seven samples (1.17 px). Deconvolving the native values into subpixel counts shows binary samples with exactly this shape on stems, serifs and the diagonals of `V` at 48 px (left edge one sample left, right edge two or one right, whatever the slope), at every origin phase (the widening is on the placed lattice, not on the glyph), and a continuous model fitted to 300 stem edges of `H I L` gives the same pair (left 1/6 to 3/16 px, right 1/3 to 0.354 px, i.e. whole samples with a closed tie). (2) A filter output of one sixth (42) whose only source is a single sample in the neighbouring pixel is dropped (acc 1 across a pixel boundary), which is why a stem at a pixel edge lacks its faint tail; applied to Arial, Times New Roman, Tahoma and Segoe UI the same rule breaks pinned captures of `gdiplus-cleartype-sizes.fixture.test.ts`, so it belongs to this face. Exact captures: regular 242, bold 364, italic 242, bold italic 266 of 494 each (185, 313, 135 and 253 with the widening alone); the straight-edged glyphs `E H I L T 7` of the regular and bold faces are exact in 237 of 248 captures at every size and phase (`src/gdiplus-cleartype-courier.fixture.test.ts` pins every exact capture). The behaviour keys on the family name, not the file (scratch captures, generator figures): a private copy of `cour.ttf` renamed to anything else renders unwidened, equal to our unwidened model, while the same file registered as `Courier New` returns the installed font (a glyph moved in the private copy does not move), and every variant of the renamed copy (no hinting program, no `gasp`, no embedded bitmaps, no `hdmx`/`VDMX`, no layout tables) is unwidened too, so the cause is not a font table. Hypotheses rejected: hinting x with the v35 interpreter or unhinted x (0 to 19 of the 434 base regular captures exact against 140 for the shipped model), a symmetric outline embolden of a quarter pixel (11 exact against 23 for the sample OR), a vertical shift of the sampling line (every shift of 1/16 px or more loses at least 80 of the 140), the bitmap strikes (bold has none), the post-IUP calls (`D` moves no point after its IUPs, and skipping a y ALIGNRP after IUP[y] leaves the regular-face exact count at 140). What remains is the curved edges: the inner contour of `D`, `P`, `R`, `B`, `0`, `8`, `9` is 0.1 to 0.4 px off native at the shallow arcs (for `D` at 20 px native's inner edge is 11/64 to 27/64 px further right at the upper and lower arcs), which is the interpolation class of the other faces, and the serif tips of italic rows. GDI's own ClearType of Courier New is not measured (`TextRenderer` ink in a scratch capture is wider than Arial's but the gamma differs); the rules are applied to GDI+ only.
- **Raster (`.fon`) faces (round 7, workstream E):** the report rows `textx-fon-system.regen` (13,261) and `textx-fon-fixedsys.regen` (23,322) were a report artefact: `report.ts` rendered the regenerated references without the 8514 System and Fixedsys faces their session used; with `8514sys.fon` and `8514fix.fon` both are exact (0 of 300,480), as the regeneration test already asserted, and the originals stay exact with the fixture font set. The remaining residuals were not the integer-stretch limits the earlier notes assumed; they were three rules, each measured on native GDI (`RasterStyleProbe.cs` `text-raster-styles`: seven faces x seven heights drawn as "Hgy. a", "   ", "H", "y" and "g" under every bold/italic/underline/strike-out combination, 3,920 captures; `RasterMapperProbe.cs` rows). (1) *Face choice:* GDI never stretches a face of at most 6 pixels (Terminal 4x6, Small Fonts 3x5 and 3x6) as readily as the cost model assumed: Terminal -30 and 30 draw the 8x12 face doubled (24 pixel cell, 16 wide), not the 4x6 face five times, and Small Fonts -15 and cell 15 draw the 4x8 face doubled and the 6x13 face unstretched, not the 3x6 or 3x5 face tripled, while Small Fonts cell 18 does triple the 3x6 face. An extra cost of 50 per squared extra factor on such faces fixes the 12 rows (6 heights at two weights) without moving any of the other 2,888 of 2,900 mapper rows (2,794 to 2,806 exact cell height and weight-400 width; Terminal is now 120 of 120 heights, Small Fonts 119 of 120, weight-700 heights 824 of 840 from 818). `textx-fon-terminal` 5,128, `textx-fon-smallfonts` 1,101 and `textx-fon-smallfonts-cell` 989 differing pixels become 0. The horizontal stretch (`eyScale`/`exScale`) was not involved. The mapper's choice is identical at 96 and 144 dpi (all 1,200 rows of `raster-mapper.json` recaptured at 96 dpi: 0 differ). (2) *Bold italic:* GDI slants the bitmaps and then emboldens them; on a stretched face (scale 2 or more) the bold slanted glyph sits one pixel further right, and the smear into the column after the glyph's last one is dropped where that column is a multiple of 8 (the packed row's byte boundary), so a narrow glyph's last stem stays one pixel wide on those rows. All 196 bold italic captures are exact with both rules (18 of the 49 "Hgy. a" captures were inexact before, 1,911 differing pixels in all). (3) *Decorations:* bars are laid out on the unstretched face and then stretched: `floor(cell / 12)` source rows thick (at least 1; MS Sans Serif cells of 25 and more are 2 to 3 rows), the underline starts one source row below the baseline (two when thicker than one row) and is pulled up into the cell when it would leave it (Terminal's 8 pixel cell), the strike-out top is `ceil((ascent - internal leading) / 3)` source rows above the baseline, and an italic strike-out bar is shifted right by the glyph lean at the middle of its top source row, `floor(((2d - 1) * lean + span) / (2 * span))` for `d` source rows above the baseline (an underline is never shifted). All 3,920 style captures (every text, style and size) are exact; `textx-fon-mssansserif-styles` becomes 0 (from 43). `src/gdi-raster-styles.fixture.test.ts` pins them. *lfWidth (round 8, workstream R6) is closed.* The earlier cost tables failed because the mapper is not one search over faces and factors; it fixes each face's two factors first and compares one candidate per face. This was found by replacing the installed faces with private synthetic bitmap families (`RasterSyntheticMapperProbe.cs`, mode `text-raster-synthetic`, `raster-mapper-synthetic.json.gz`; bare `.fnt` files with blank glyphs registered `FR_PRIVATE`, read back with `GetTextMetrics`). The six installed families rebuilt from nothing but each face's cell height, internal leading, average and maximum width, weight and resolution answer exactly like the installed fonts (840 of 840 lfHeight x lfWidth rows per family, and all 140 wide heights per family), so nothing else about a face matters. Rules measured one variable at a time (`src/raster-mapper.ts`, header): (1) the horizontal factor is `lfWidth / avgWidth` rounded half up, 1 to 5, whatever else is installed (the old code rounded half down); with no lfWidth it is the vertical factor, at most 5; (2) the vertical factor is `floor(request / unit)` and one more when the face overshoots the request by at most `floor(unit / 4)` pixels (`floor((unit - 1) / 4)` for the step from 1 to 2); `unit` is the character height for a negative lfHeight and the cell height for a positive one; this reproduces the thresholds of 105 faces of 5 to 40 pixels exactly, and a face is never stretched beyond `unit - 3` (faces of 4 pixels or less not at all); (3) the face with the lowest sum wins, in units of one pixel of lfWidth error: too small 3 per pixel, too big 12 plus 3 per pixel, a vertical factor n above 1 costs about n (2.1, 3.0, 4.1, 5.1 for 2 to 5; each inside a one-unit interval measured by exact pair comparisons), the horizontal error costs 1 per pixel, a horizontal factor that differs from the vertical one costs an aspect penalty affine in their ratio (about 8.7 times kx/ky when wider, 7.4 times ky/kx plus 0.3 when narrower; all 45 measured intervals of P(kx, ky) for kx 1 to 5 and ky 1 to 8 contain it), vertical factors 6 to 8 without an lfWidth cost 25, 35 and 41 (the horizontal factor stops at 5), a weight mismatch about a quarter of a pixel per 100 units plus half a pixel across the bold boundary, faces drawn for another resolution lose ties by 0.05; ties go to the earlier face. Results: Courier at lfHeight -6 to -12 takes the 9x16 face at lfWidth 12, 13, 20, 28, 29, 36 and 37 (the 8x13 face at the others) because the 9x16 face's factor is one below the 8x13 face's there and its error is at most 4; `textx-fon-courier-styles` 839 to 0 differing pixels (every other `textx-fon-*` row unchanged); of the 5,880 `raster-mapper-widths.json.gz` rows 5,477 are exact (3,818 before; 5,028 of the 5,040 non-Terminal rows), of 784 lfWidth style captures 746 (567 before, none lost), all 980 lfHeight 61 to 130 rows in height and width and all 840 bold heights (919 and 824 before), and every one of the 1,200 height rows of the 144 dpi capture (118 to 119 of 120 per family before). Against the synthetic captures: 99.3 to 99.8% of the six replica families, 3,195 of 3,200 equal-height pairs and 7,467 of 7,500 random four-face rows. *Open:* the remaining 0.2 to 0.7% are near-ties between faces at high vertical factors that the measured intervals cannot separate; the 391 Terminal rows and 38 Terminal style captures differ because native Windows maps Terminal over more OEM `.fon` sizes than the fixture font set supplies (an 8 x 8 face, for one), which is a font-set limit and not the rule; when a request is too large for the faces of a private family GDI falls back to another installed family (a 5-pixel face asked for 8 pixels returns Small Fonts metrics), which the converter does not model because it only ever sees one family. `textx-fon-msshelldlg` (84), `-cell` (34) and `text-msshelldlg-hm13` (8) are not raster faces: Windows draws MS Shell Dlg with the Microsoft Sans Serif outlines, and the differing pixels are single rows of the diagonal glyphs (`A V W X & %`) at 20 pixels and above and one row of the `g` and `y` bowls at 13 pixels: TrueType hinting of Microsoft Sans Serif, which belongs with the TrueType hinting items above, not raster-face behaviour.
- **Open for ClearType glyphs (round 5):** diagonal and curved edges still differ by one to a few samples (Arial 16 `$`, `%`, `&`, `@`, `D`, `G`, `S`, `X`; Times New Roman Italic 22 `g`, `a`, `k`, `v`, `y`, `2`; Arial Bold 40 `W`, `y`: for Arial 16 `X` the native diagonal is steeper than ours, the top of the stroke about a sixth of a pixel to the left), which is interpolation (IP/IUP[x] and MDRP on diagonals) rather than stems. Courier New is closed for straight edges in round 6 (previous bullet). MSIRP: resolved in round 6 (the MSIRP bullet above). Post-IUP move kinds probed in a scratch private font at 16 ppem (y: SCFS, MIRP, MIAP, MDAP, ALIGNRP; x: SHPIX, SCFS, MIRP, MSIRP; each before, between and after the IUPs) matched native for all but ALIGNRP in y; the committed `alignrp-*` captures of round 6 (the ALIGNRP bullet above) show native honours ALIGNRP in every position, so the Times New Roman Regular bowl glyphs (B, D, P, R) have another cause: the storage 8 flag (the Storage 8 bullet above), which closed part of them (Times New Roman 254 to 308 of its 372 size-sheet captures) and leaves the rest with the diagonal and curved edges. DrawString layout is a separate error that dominated `textx-plus-cleartype` in round 5 (10.2% before that round, 9.7% after; 9.2% after round 6 by the parity test's metric). Round 6 (W2) re-measured the single-string placement family (`src/gdiplus-drawstring-placement.fixture.test.ts`): 96 of the 144 independent controls are exact and the other 48 are only the W and g glyph shapes, which differ by the same pixel count at both quarter-pixel phases and are not lowered by any whole-pixel shift of our output, so those controls have no placement defect. What follows is the round-5 measurement of the long ClearType string, which stays open: the glyphs of "GDI+ DrawString Ag 0123" sit one to four pixels left of native after "GDI+" (best whole-pixel shift of each word: 0, -2 and -4 px). Native ClearTypeGridFit glyph advances are whole pixels (Arial 16: `A` 11, `H` 12, `I` 4, `Y` 11, `v` 8, `x`, `y`, `z` 8, digits 9, i.e. the rounded linear advance, where this converter's hdmx advances give 9, 3, 7 for `Y`, `I`, `x`), and a string's integer advances are corrected so that the total follows the float layout (GenericTypographic: the rounded sum of the linear advances, as `MeasureString` reports; default format: 1.03 times that plus a third of the em), but the correction is not spread evenly: for a run of 40 `n` the typographic gaps are thirty-six of 9 px then four of 8, the default gaps ten of 10 px then twenty-nine of 9, a run of 40 `H` has twenty-two of 12 px then seventeen of 11, while a run of 40 `I` stays at 4 px with two or seven 5 px gaps at the end, far short of the 178 px that `MeasureString` reports (measured with `GdipDrawString` and `GdipMeasureString` on 1 to 60 repeats; scratch, generator figures). A rule that reproduces those distributions is the data the layout needs.
- **GDI ClearType ExtTextOut (round 7, workstream D):** `textx-arial-cleartype` and its q0/q1/q2 variants are GDI `ExtTextOut` (not GDI+), with recorded integer dx, so placement is given and the error was raster. Four native stages were measured (probes `GdiClearTypeBlendProbe.cs`, `GdiClearTypeRasterProbe.cs`, `GdiClearTypeCompatProbe.cs`, `GdiGetInfoProbe.cs`; modes `text-gdi-cleartype-blend`, `-raster`, `-compat`, `text-gdi-getinfo`). (1) Blend: 6,873,600 native channel values (128 text/background pairs, three qualities) are reproduced exactly by gamma 1.2 (the system contrast 1200) tables rounded to 8 bits, a mix `(dec[s] * k + dec[d] * (6 - k)) / 6` rounded half up over seven coverage levels, pixel-level rules (no coverage untouched, full coverage the text colour, a partly covered pixel round-trips its uncovered channels); the old float fit was one level off at two of the seven levels, 20,000 pixels of the Arial sheet. (2) Scan conversion (`gdi-cleartype-raster.json.gz`, private program-free fonts at 32 ppem): all 1,070 bars, slanted edges and doubly fractional bars are exact once rows are sampled five per pixel, weighted 1 2 3 2 1, wherever `gasp` has GASP_SYMMETRIC_SMOOTHING (Arial 1 to 8 and 18 up, Segoe UI 1 to 8 and 20 up; none in between, which is where the row character of the sheets changes). 256 random polygons differ by 397 isolated pixels of 43,544. (3) Sizes whose `gasp` range lacks GASP_GRIDFIT (Arial 8 and below) are drawn unhinted and unscaled (the 8 px line: 573 to 45 differing pixels). (4) `CLEARTYPE_NATURAL_QUALITY` runs without GETINFO's compatible-widths bit and matches native at 9 to 17 px (`ctnatural` lines 1 to 8: 2,100 to 270 pixels). Against native playback (`*.playback.png`, merged from the rotated-text workstream; the direct-drawing PNGs differ from playback by 503 and 39,661 pixels, see above): `textx-arial-cleartype`/q0/q1/q2 22,224 to 11,370 of 727,818, `textx-arial-ctnatural` 46,228 (against the direct drawing, which cannot be matched from the recording) to 4,428, `textx-segoeui-cleartype` 26,464 to 10,988, `textx-color-cleartype` 1,761 to 871. Still open: default-quality (backward-compatible) hinting differs from natural by whole 1/6-pixel stem shifts and 1 to 2 sample stem widths (Arial `l` at 20 px: native 9 samples, ours 8; `n` at 15 px both stems one sample left), unchanged by interpreter version 35 to 40, by the compat flag, by scaling the natural outline to the black-and-white advance (fits: best uniform scale reproduces few glyphs exactly) or by forcing the phantom advance; `GdiGetInfoProbe.cs` and `build-getinfo-font.py` are committed to read the native GETINFO answers directly and were captured (`gdi-getinfo.json.gz`, `src/gdi-getinfo.fixture.test.ts`): native GDI ClearType reports compatible widths at qualities 0 and 5 only and symmetric smoothing (bit 18, which the converter does not report: setting it worsened Arial 11,327 to 14,955 pixels); version 40 vs 41+ and bits 10 and 11 change nothing. Diagonals (Arial v, x, y, A, V, W, X) remain the round-5 interpolation residual. Not touched: GDI+ ClearType (`textx-plus-cleartype`, `gpx-rec-textcontrast*`, `gpx-text-texture-cleartype`, `textpb-segoeui-cell-q3`) keep their numbers; long DrawString spacing remains open.
- **GDI+ text (round 9, W2):** sources used, separated as the brief asks. *Documented by Microsoft:* the ClearType white paper ("Backwards compatibility of TrueType instructions with Microsoft ClearType", https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/typography/cleartype/truetypecleartype) specifies the compatibility rules already in the interpreter (SHPIX and DELTAP skipping, unrounded MIRP and MSIRP honouring the cut-in, RDTG after SPVTL on the physical grid, 1/16 virtual grid rounding, SCVTCI / 16 and SMD / 2 in the ClearType direction, GETINFO bits 13 to 16): every one is implemented, and none distinguishes the diagonal cases (`ctDirection` variants below). *Not found:* any Microsoft statement of how GDI+ lays out, rounds or blends text. Only search-result summaries of the `TextRenderingHint` page (https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/gdiplusenums/ne-gdiplusenums-textrenderinghint), the antialiasing-with-text page (https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/gdiplus/-gdiplus-antialiasing-with-text-use) and KB 307208 (mirror: https://ftp.zx.net.nz/pub/Patches/ftp.microsoft.com/MISC/KB/en-us/307/208.HTM) were seen, which describe the hints and the default format's extra 1/6 em at each end, not any arithmetic; none was read in full. *Not used:* Wine, ReactOS, libgdiplus and FreeType sources were not read; nothing was ported. *Measured here:* everything below, with committed probes.
  (1) *Advances, not hinting.* A probe of repeated glyphs drawn at sixteenth-pixel origins (`SingleBitAdvanceProbe.cs`) shows that unhinted DrawString advances a glyph by `round(hmtx x 1.03)` font units (Arial `l` at 16 px: 469 units = 3.6641 px, where the 26.6 linear advance times 1.03 is 3.6613 and accumulated 0.003 px per glyph): the round 8 "mispositioned clusters plus dropout singles" of `textx-plus-singlebit` were this. Only at an identity world transform (the scaled-alignment controls regress otherwise): `textx-plus-singlebit` 125 to 30, `textx-plus-antialias` 556 to 42 with the bars. (2) *Underlines.* `UnderlineProbe.cs` (8,000 captures: five faces, underline / bold / italic / strike-out / both, 20 sizes, 16 origin phases) fits the vertical rule to every capture (see Text) and shows the coverage is four sub-scanlines by eight horizontal samples (32 levels), not contrast-adjusted, drawn as a second pass over the glyphs (`1 - (1 - g)(1 - b)`); a one-row bar's ends depend on the sub-scanline (profiles such as 5, 7, 6, 4 eighths for the four sub-scanlines of a pixel, shifting by 2 for the next size) and no threshold model reproduces them (50% of 68 profiles at best), so they use fitted offsets; the grid-fit hints draw the same extent rounded to whole pixels (one pixel each in four fixtures). (3) *ClearType blend.* `ClearTypeBlendPlusProbe.cs` (80 draws of the same strings over flat backgrounds; black on white at contrast 0 yields each channel's coverage) shows the blend is 8-bit table arithmetic with exact sixths of coverage, not the float mix (72.8% of partially covered samples before, 99.6% after; the first-order bias of the float mix was 127/255 against one half). `gpx-text-texture-cleartype` is now exact. (4) *Playback stretch.* `scripts/gdi-fixtures/patch-text-scales.ts`: with every `eyScale` of `textpb-segoeui-cell-q3.emf` set to its `exScale`, native `PlayEnhMetaFile` draws exactly the direct drawing (0 of 2,102,400 pixels differ, 4,050 unpatched), so the stretch is read from the record and round 6's "the playing device's own scales" was wrong; the converter applies it for Segoe UI (the "window" rule of round 8, all 10,660 native outcomes agree): `textpb-segoeui-cell-q3` 4,095 to 93. `textx-segoeui-cell-mono` is compared with its native playback reference (48 pixels; 989 from the old direct-drawing PNG, as the playback itself is). Calibri-like faces follow the "integer" rule and Arial, Tahoma and the other stock faces change by the x scale alone: not modelled, none occur in the fixtures. Side finding: native `PlayEnhMetaFile` of an EMF+-only recording onto a bitmap DC draws nothing on this machine (build 26300), so the `gpx-*` and `textx-plus-*` references are direct drawings.
  *Diagonal stems (still open, negative results).* The ClearType-direction test (`|pvx| > |pvy|`) was replaced by the freedom-vector direction, by an exactly-x projection vector, by their conjunction and disjunction, and by never / always, separately for rounding, the cut-in and the minimum distance: the production rule is the best of every variant on the 3,384 Arial AntiAlias real-glyph captures (2,996 exact; the nearest alternative, the freedom vector for rounding, 2,972, and gains no capture). Truncating division on x moves along oblique projection vectors ends 134 to 184 captures of the same set lower even when restricted to oblique vectors or to MIRP (the W call site fits it; `I`, `H`, `n` do not), and moving by the truncated, floored or ceiled difference between the target and the *unrounded* current projection in MIRP and MDRP (the reading of the round 8 observation that native behaves as if the current distance were 70 below the original and 71 above) ends 169 to 182 lower, with only the rounded delta equal to production. The native outputs of the captures are not a pure function of the font, size and hint (round 5 and 8: two states, 2,492 of 31,584 captures differ between runs of one machine), which a stateless converter cannot reproduce; this is the open part of W, k, y, v.
- **GDI+ text (round 8, R2):** the stages were separated with native probes of GDI+ itself (`TextAdvanceProbe.cs` `text-advances`, `GdiGetInfoProbe.cs` `text-gdi-getinfo`, `TextShpixOrderProbe.cs` `text-shpix-order`, plus scratch private copies of Arial whose glyph programs were truncated or instrumented). (0) *Stability:* three fresh-process playbacks of `gpx-rec-textcontrast.emf` are identical to each other and to the committed reference from the second process on; the first process after an idle period differed in 78 of 68,400 pixels (up to 68 levels), so the committed references are the stable state and the residuals below are not run-to-run noise. The TextContrast mapping is exact: every pixel the fixture still differs in (302, all in the W, k and y glyphs) is a diagonal-stem shape, the other glyphs match at all four contrasts. (1) *Placement:* AntiAliasGridFit advances a glyph by the width GDI's own interpreter reports, not the ClearType interpreter's phantom point that the glyph shapes come from (Arial Bold 40 px: W 39, I 10, v 21, w 31, where the ClearType phantom points round to 38, 11, 22, 32; `GetCharWidth32` and the native runs agree, 1,085 of 1,093 whole-number glyphs of `text-advances.json.gz`). That was the whole of `textx-plus-antialiasgridfit` (513 to 14 differing pixels) and most of `gpx-rec-textcontrast` (1,994 to 854) and the brushed gray text fixtures. ClearTypeGridFit's default format advances a glyph by the ClearType interpreter's phantom-point advance rounded to a pixel (1,230 of 1,230 whole-number glyphs; the GDI widths used before match 1,058): `textx-plus-cleartype` 2,013 to 361, `gpx-text-texture-cleartype` 531 to 161, `gpx-rec-textcontrast-cleartype` 3,407 to 1,898, and the 2,320 ClearType baseline strings 792,198 to 633,491 differing pixels (173 better, none worse). GenericTypographic keeps the GDI widths: the same advances cut its 75 mixed strings from 39,867 to 22,277 pixels but worsen six, whose end-of-run compensation is not modelled (native run gaps for repeated glyphs change by one pixel over the last floor(grid width - ideal width) glyphs; a model with that contraction, and an expansion past one em, worsened twelve other captures). Synthetic italic faces keep the GDI widths. (2) *Raster:* GDI+ grayscale text (AntiAlias, and AntiAliasGridFit where `gasp` smooths) runs the ClearType interpreter and answers GETINFO as gray ClearType: ClearType (selector 64) and 4096 set, grayscale (32) not, at every size (`gdi-getinfo.json.gz` now holds the SingleBit, SingleBit gridfit and AntiAlias hints). The converter reported grayscale, so prep programs picked another branch: 15,747 to 17,113 of the 31,584 `text-real-glyphs` captures exact with none lost (Segoe UI 1,099 of 1,128 under both hints), Georgia 1,042/736 to 1,053/782. The generic per-size SHPIX helper functions (`MPPEM >= lo and MPPEM <= hi: SHPIX`) shift a point only when it is touched in y and stop once IUP[y] has run (private fonts `shpix-order-a.ttf`: 32 of 44 captures exact, `src/ttf-shpix-order.fixture.test.ts`); Arial Bold's W calls them after both IUPs to lower its inner apexes two pixels at 26 to 58 ppem, which native does not do: 64 more exact text-coverage captures (2,464 to 2,528), 17 more ClearType size captures (3,059 to 3,076), five more Courier New ClearType ones (1,114 to 1,119). (3) *Brushed text:* the lingrad, texture and pathgrad fixtures differ from native only where the glyph coverage differs (339/337/161 to 79/76/67 pixels after the above); no combine rounding was found to be off. (4) *Single bit:* `textx-plus-singlebit` (125) is unchanged: three mispositioned clusters plus dropout singles; the 1/128 advance quantisation stays rejected. Also: a near-aligned italic face with a vertical alignment is laid out like the upright faces (144 of the 1,296 italic alignment controls improve, none worsens). **Candidates measured and not shipped:** (a) SHPIX along y without the touched-in-y requirement for ordinary (non-helper) functions: native does it in all six private kinds that differ (untouched points, before or after the IUPs), and it adds 182 exact real-font captures (Courier New, Times New Roman, hint 4) and 117 Courier New ClearType ones, but it improves 61 of the 2,320 ClearType baseline strings (298 pixels) and worsens 45 (101 pixels), and worsens five more gray alignment controls by a pixel (Courier New and Times New Roman Italic `AV`), so it stays out of production (the converter keeps the rule and the test pins the six kinds). (b) Per-product projection rounding now wins on the committed `text-coverage` captures too (192 against 94 matching the committed images of the 606 that differ, 1,691 against 851 of the real-glyph ones) and closes Arial Bold 40 W and k, but still loses the 30 Arial 16 `y` captures and eight real-glyph ones, so it remains excluded. (c) The remaining diagonal-glyph residual (Arial 12 `W` 26 pixels, 11 to 13 ppem half the Arial and Tahoma glyphs under AntiAlias) starts at one instruction: truncating Arial's `W` program after each instruction and reading the native result, the first divergence is function 20's `MIRP[00100]` after `SDPVTL[1]` (the diagonal stem width cvt 23 and its inputs match native exactly); native moves the point as if the projected current distance were 70 for a target below the original distance and 71 above it, where ours is 71 (exact 70.64); measuring it from the dual projection or from the original-vector projection fixes this instruction and loses 1,028 and 2,798 real-glyph captures elsewhere. The instruction itself is pinned by a native digital readout (a glyph copy whose program ends with `IF Q == k` and an SCFS jump, one variant per k, reads any stack value or point coordinate exactly): cvt 23, the current distance (-71) and the cvt reads agree with ours, and the move of the point is `mulDivGdi(distance - curDist)` (GDI's truncating division on the x move along an oblique projection vector) for each of 61 swept targets at `W`'s call site and 29 at three of `w`'s four call sites at 12 px (the fourth call site's prefix already differs), and for every one of the 449 truncated programs of Arial's `w` at 20 px when MDRP distances along a diagonal are also left unrounded; the same two rules lose 1,072 real-glyph captures and gain 67, because the MDRP rounding along a diagonal differs by glyph (Arial `v` at 20 px matches all 179 truncated programs with the rounded distance and the GDI division, and breaks at the 127th with the unrounded one), so the converter keeps its arithmetic. (d) AntiAlias underlines are fractional in native (thickness in quarter pixels: 0.75, 1, 1.5 or 2 px, positions between the integer rows), where the converter draws an integer bar: about 170 of the 556 differing pixels of `textx-plus-antialias`; not modelled.
- **GDI ClearType compatible widths (round 8, R1):** the default-quality stem shifts of round 7 are an interpreter rule, found with private fonts (`build-cleartype-width-fonts.py`, `build-cleartype-phantom-font.py`; probe `GdiClearTypeWidthProbe.cs`, mode `text-gdi-cleartype-width`, `gdi-cleartype-width.json.gz`, manifest `environment-text-gdi-cleartype-width.json`, `src/gdi-cleartype-width.fixture.test.ts`): eleven sets of one-instruction glyphs (a bar or stem whose edges one known instruction places) drawn at default and at natural quality, 4,209 glyphs each at 16 ppem, so the only difference between the two drawings is the width mode. (1) A program-free bar, and a bar placed by MDAP, MIAP, MDRP (rounded or not) or a MIRP from the origin phantom point, is identical at both qualities; the advance phantom point itself sits at the black-and-white advance (ALIGNRP, MDRP, MSIRP and MIRP from it land at BW - the outline distance, 544 of 544 `pp2` glyphs exact; the old converter instead scaled the whole natural outline by BW / natural advance, which is wrong for a program-free bar). (2) A MIRP or MSIRP between two glyph points shifts both points, and every point already or later aligned to them by ALIGNRP, MDRP, IP or SHP, by (centre of the pair) * (BW / natural advance - 1) with the centre from the original outline; the sweep over 17 advances and 96 left edges in 1/128 px steps (1,632 glyphs) is reproduced exactly, the shift of a stem with the position (196 of 196) and the carried offset of a stem anchored to the advance point (a single-contour H chained from pp1: 23 of 27 exact; on Arial H the native shift equals the pair formula, not the anchoring) too. Points placed relative to the advance phantom point carry its offset, which the pair's shift replaces; pairs are the two sides of a stem (the segment between them runs through ink, at least a quarter pixel apart: a counter between two stems, and a hook tip next to its stem, do not shift). (3) Fonts report GETINFO bit 18 (symmetric smoothing, selector 2048) from the size's `gasp` range, not at every size: with it Arial's natural quality is exact for 349 of 420 captures (320) and the diagonals that were the round-5 interpolation residual (v, x, y, A, V, W, X) match natively (`textx-arial-ctnatural` 4,034 to 687 differing pixels); round 7 had tried the bit at every size and found it worse. Results: `textx-arial-cleartype`/q0/q1/q2 11,272 to 7,146 of 727,818, `textx-segoeui-cleartype` 10,981 to 4,372 of 719,390, `textx-color-cleartype` 867 to 595; exact default-quality captures of the 420 per face: Arial 81 to 146, Segoe UI 71 to 135, Tahoma 56 to 126; differing pixels over the 420: Arial 6,296 to 3,324, Segoe UI 4,686 to 2,622, Tahoma 5,112 to 4,103. The trade: of the 1,260 default-quality captures of `gdi-cleartype-compat.json.gz` 259 become exact and 60 stop being exact (Arial 81 gained and 16 lost, Segoe UI 86 and 22, Tahoma 92 and 22; none was pinned by a test; natural quality gains 72 and loses none); every `textx` ClearType fixture improves. Tried and rejected: setting storage 2 to 2 or GETINFO bit 13 clear in the prep (default quality worse everywhere), shifting by half the advance difference (right only for a stem near the advance's centre), scaling each scanline run (9,141 against 6,296 differing pixels), using the natural hinted advance for the ratio, and rounding shifted points to the 1/16 grid. Open: the MIRP-to-stem classification and the followers of a stem for diagonal glyphs ('V', 'W', 'X', 'x', 'A': the apex or crossing point moves by about a tenth of its arms, ours by the arms' amount) and for 't' and 'g' at 24 px, Arial 11 to 16 px, where the compatible-mode SHPIX tables apply; private fonts: native clips a bar moved past four pixels at 24 samples (phantom, flags and follow sets) and draws a faint extra top sample row for 72 `types` glyphs (MIRP from pp1 then a rounded MDRP) at both qualities.
- **GDI ClearType past the stem (round 9, W1):** research first, then private fonts. *Documented by Microsoft* ("Backwards compatible ClearType", https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/typography/cleartype/truetypecleartype): the TrueType interpreter runs a backward-compatibility mode for fonts that have not set INSTCTRL selector 3: DELTAP and SHPIX are skipped except on points already touched in the non-ClearType direction (and in composite glyphs), "a post-IUP delta occurs after the IUP instruction", RDTG after SPVTL rounds on the physical grid, unrounded MIRP and MSIRP honour the CVT cut-in, rounding in the CVT program uses the physical grid, and legacy TypeMan Talk helpers are bypassed through storage 8, 22 and 24; GETINFO reports ClearType (bit 13), compatible widths (14), symmetric smoothing (15) and BGR (16). The paper calls compatible widths "a technique which is not related to the issues being discussed" and says nothing about what that mode does to a glyph program. FreeType's v40 interpreter documentation (https://freetype.org/freetype2/docs/hinting/subpixel-hinting.html) says it "ignores all horizontal hinting instructions", leaves advance widths alone and drops the "dirtiest hacks", so it models none of this; no FreeType, Wine or ReactOS source was read or used. Every documented rule that matters for the three Windows fonts was already in `src/ttf-hinting.ts` (rounds 5 to 8); the stem shifts of the width mode are not documented anywhere that was found, so the rest is *measured here*, with four private-font sets (`build-cleartype-arm-fonts.py`, mode `text-gdi-cleartype-arm`, `gdi-cleartype-arm.json.gz`, `src/gdi-cleartype-arm.fixture.test.ts`, 765 glyphs at 16 ppem in both qualities) and with copies of the Windows fonts whose glyph programs were truncated after every instruction and captured one by one (Arial 'V' and 'W', Tahoma 'V' and 'y', Segoe UI 'H'; not committed, they are derivatives of the system fonts). (1) A stem the program sets after IUP[x] is never shifted: 108 of 108 private bars whose MIRP pair follows IUP[x] (alone, or with IUP[y] before or after) are identical at default and natural quality, the 81 whose pair comes first or follows only IUP[y] are not; in the truncation series of Tahoma 'V' (whose diagonal caps are set after both IUPs) the captures from the second post-IUP MIRP on match native only without the shift (0 against 38 differing pixels). (2) The shift acts on the outline while the program runs: a point that MDRP places from a shifted stem point under a projection vector set perpendicular to the arm (SPVTL[1] or SDPVTL[1] on the arm's edge) follows by the share of the shift that its height along the arm gives (0 at the foot, the whole shift at the stem; the `height` set), under an axis projection vector it follows by the whole shift, and the dual vector of SPVTL comes from the original points like SDPVTL's (the `arm` set). (3) With the shifts in the outline, the absolute measures a program takes (MDAP and MIAP rounding, GC, MD, the ratio IP interpolates) must see the outline without them: MDAP rounding, MD / GC and the IP ratio each gain on the 3 x 420 Windows-font captures (MIAP is made consistent and changes nothing), none isolated by a private font yet. Two commits implement them, (1) alone and (2) and (3) on top of it, on the branches `w1-candidate-postiup` and `w1-candidate-immediate-shift`; production keeps the round 8 rule because both trade a few exact captures: (1) alone makes Tahoma 'W', 'y', 'A', 'V', 'X' and 'v' exact more often (126 to 132 of 420 default-quality captures, 4,103 to 3,041 differing pixels; Arial and Segoe UI unchanged, no report fixture changes) and costs one capture (Tahoma 'y' at 20 px, 3 pixels; keeping post-IUP shifts of at most 2/64 px would keep it, at 130 instead of 132 exact, but native cannot tell: the private bars show no shift below 1/16 px, `postsmall`); (1) to (3) take the 1,260 default-quality captures from 410 to 457 exact (Arial 149 to 179, Segoe UI 135 to 138, Tahoma 126 to 140) and from 10,029 to 8,209 differing pixels, 54 gained, 7 lost (Arial '$' and '%' at 12 px, Segoe UI '@' at 10 and 'r' at 16 and 18, Tahoma '3' at 9 and 'y' at 20: 1 to 4 pixels each), natural quality unchanged, `textx-arial-cleartype`/q0/q1/q2 7,127 to 6,036 differing pixels, `textx-color-cleartype` 595 to 498, `textx-segoeui-cleartype` 4,372 to 4,264, the other 904 fixtures unchanged (803 exact before and after), the private `rects` set 31 to 33 of 36 and the `arm` and `height` sets 126 to 189 of 224 and 171 to 268 of 288 default-quality glyphs. Tried and rejected, with the experiment that rejected them: a width limit or a slope limit on what a stem is (the chain pairs of 'V', 'W', 'X' and 'x' between a valley and an arm top, 4 to 7 pixels wide and steep, are not shifted by native, but no limit on width, slope, em fraction, CVT use, MIRP flags or pv separates them from the real stems without losing more elsewhere: labelling each pair of Arial on or off by the native capture takes the glyphs that have pairs from 2,507 to 1,553 differing pixels, so the classification is worth about a third of what is left); making a point that already has a shift of its own keep it (Arial 2,699 to 2,959 differing pixels); one-sided IP shifts (a point between a shifted and an unshifted reference takes the shifted one's whole shift: right for Segoe UI 'r', wrong for Arial 'W' at 18 px); intersecting ISECT lines on the unshifted outline (Arial 'X' and 'x' 178 and 166 to 319 and 227 pixels); quantising shifts to 1/16 pixel (Arial 3,304 to 4,420). Open: which MIRPs native counts as a stem (above), 't' (two flat caps native does not shift), Arial 11 to 16 px SHPIX tables, Segoe UI 'm', and the remaining 'V' (357 differing pixels over the 14 sizes).
- **Unresolved differences:** ClearType, EMF+ antialiased text and TextContrast retain the fixture residuals above. PolyTextOut C1 suppression is fixed; the remaining 12 pixels in its original fixture and three pixels in the Arial C1 controls are glyph-edge differences. Courier C1 controls are exact.
- **Fonts in fixtures:** runner fonts can differ from original captures. New captures include font hashes and native library versions. Current native fonts exactly reproduce the three `textx-plus` antialias/ClearType PNGs; other old captures still lack complete provenance.

**12 October follow-up to the historical results below:** native family-specific Courier New AntiAlias widening is now implemented for regular/italic, untransformed, nonsynthetic faces. The original regular-face AntiAlias set improves from 0 to 653/1,128 exact; 657 additional fresh controls become exact. Monochrome neighboring-square stub decisions and 26.6 smart midpoint selection improve 4,587 controls for straight, single-contour outlines. Curves/multiple contours retain the prior rules. No previously exact control is lost in the new comparisons; five Courier and twelve independent/held-out monochrome cases have small, individually documented increases. Long ClearType spacing remains open: both advance/compensation candidates were rejected after native mixed-string regressions. See [Native parity review](./native-parity-review.md#text-follow-up) for complete counts and scope.

### Grayscale hinting and scan conversion (round 5, V1, 8 October 2026)

**12 October continuation, Courier curve/sample staging:** no production candidate passed preservation. On the 6,400 Courier style/size/origin controls, interpolating IUP in scaled coordinates loses 1,224 previously exact controls and a 16.16 interpolation ratio loses 135. Finer/coarser conic subdivision and rounded conic splits also lose exact controls; truncated or unrounded splits change nothing. The existing 653/1,128 regular AntiAlias exact captures and five small historical tradeoffs remain unchanged.

Fresh `courier-geometry` controls use two CC0 generated fonts with identical program-free quadratic bowls, concave polygons and rectangles, differing only in names (`Courier New`/`Parity Courier`). All 3,200 full surfaces are bounded individually in `gdiplus-courier-geometry.fixture.test.ts`; 1,393 are currently exact. Native widens the named font under both grayscale hints, whereas the installed-font controls do not support applying widening to every grid-fit mask. This additional scope remains open. Simple default dropouts improve the renamed AntiAlias geometry from 464 to 728/800 exact and the named sample-widened geometry from 465 to 689/800. Applied to the earlier program-free polygon AntiAlias corpus, that default improves 1,536 to 4,728/5,120 exact without losing an exact capture, but increases 72 previously inexact per-case residuals, so it stays excluded. `generate-courier-geometry.ps1` regenerates both fonts/captures and the environment/hash manifest; no installed font data is bundled. These independent controls isolate raster geometry from the previously observed native hinting-state uncertainty.

**Probes and data.** `RealGlyphCoverageProbe.cs` (`text-real-glyphs`): every printable ASCII glyph of 14 face/style combinations at 9 to 24 ppem, AntiAlias and AntiAliasGridFit, 31,584 captures. `build-raster-fonts.py` with `RasterPolygonProbe.cs` and `RasterMonoProbe.cs` (`text-raster-polygons`, `text-raster-bars`, `text-raster-mono`, `text-raster-variants`): program-free private fonts of 256 polygons on the 1/64-pixel lattice (plus 272 thin bars, `-stN` variants running `SCANCTRL 0x1ff` and `SCANTYPE N`, and variants adding a stack declaration, a glyph program, a `cvt` table or an empty `prep`) captured through GDI+ `DrawDriverString`, GDI `GGO_BITMAP` and `GGO_GRAY4_BITMAP`. They isolate scan conversion because nothing is grid-fitted.

**Rules found.**

- *Instruction budget (a bug, fixed):* the 1,000,000-instruction guard counted over the life of a size, so after about a million instructions every later glyph of that size aborted at its first instruction (`src/ttf-hinting-budget.test.ts`). The fix moves `textx-plus-singlebit` from 394 to 392 differing pixels (bound tightened to 1.941%) and `textx-couriernew-aa` from 186 to 190, the second from a glyph the old accident happened to match.
- *Default dropout control (shipped, monochrome):* a font that never runs `SCANCTRL` is scan-converted with simple dropouts (mode 0) up to 32 ppem and none above. Evidence: GDI monochrome bitmaps of the program-free font, 4,079 of 4,096 at 8 to 32 ppem and 1,024 of 1,024 at 33 to 40; the four hinting-machinery variants give 1,787 of 1,792 and 512 of 512 each (`src/ttf-gray-dropout.fixture.test.ts`). It tightens 1,321 per-case ceilings of the private-font coverage tests and breaks none (bilevel hint, opcode set: 0 to 190 of 192 exact controls at 16 ppem, 0 to 170 at 23, 24 to 170 at 32; diagonal set: 1 to 104 of 108 at 23 and 32; vector-stage set: 0 to 148 of 160 at 16 and 23, 8 to 142 at 32).
- *Grayscale dropout control (shipped for SCANTYPE 0 and 4):* AntiAlias images are GDI's `GGO_GRAY4_BITMAP` bitmaps with the 17th level mapped to the 16th (4,969 of 5,120 images equal, the other 151 pixels one sample lighter in GDI+), and the bitmaps apply the font's scan control to the oversampled outline. SCANTYPE 0 matches 3,852 of 4,096 GDI gray bitmaps at 8 to 32 ppem, SCANTYPE 4 matches 3,747. Georgia, which selects 4, goes from 942 to 1,042 exact AntiAliasGridFit glyphs of 1,128 (1,041 before the ClearType round-five rules merged in) and from 476 to 736 (733 before) AntiAlias ones; over the whole 31,584-capture sample (latest native state) the change gains 99 and 257 exact glyphs at the two hints and loses none.
- *Default dropout of fonts without SCANCTRL in grayscale, re-measured in round 6:* per ppem the exact GDI grayscale bitmaps of the program-free polygon font are 231 to 246 of 256 at 8 to 32 ppem with simple dropouts (22 to 41 without) and identical at 33 to 40 ppem (255 to 256 either way), so the threshold is 32 inclusive, as for monochrome. Applied alone (without the AntiAliasGridFit gasp default (b)) it still exceeds committed ceilings: 14 tests at AntiAliasGridFit 16 ppem (`movement:16:3:3` 123 against 120 channels up to `dual:16:3:2` 45 against 36) and one AntiAlias capture (signed-diagonal `movement:32:4:22`, 21 against 18); smart dropouts (4, 5) or stub types (1) as the default are worse (18 tests), so it stays out.
- *Not shipped, evidence in the committed captures:* (a) the same default for grayscale (a font without `SCANCTRL` gets simple dropouts up to 32 ppem), which would take the AntiAlias polygon images from 517 to 3,709 of 4,096 at 8 to 32 ppem; (b) GDI+ AntiAliasGridFit on a font without a `gasp` table draws the GDI monochrome bitmap from 9 to 17 ppem (all 2,304 images there equal GDI's bitmaps; none of the 1,024 at 8 and 18 to 20 do), 227 to 3,907 of 4,096 exact when applied with (a). Both lift the private opcode set at hints 3 and 4 (hint 3 at 16 ppem from 0 to 190 of 192 exact controls) but push 43 captures over their committed per-case ceilings (22 of `text-diagonal-coverage`, 17 of `text-opcode-coverage`, 4 of `text-vector-stage-coverage`; 21 of them `vectors:16:3:*`, from (b), whose residual is the unresolved diagonal hinting), so they stay out.
- *Stub rule, partial (monochrome; grayscale and ClearType closed in round 6):* for SCANTYPE 1 and 5 (Arial, Times New Roman, Segoe UI, Courier New and Comic Sans MS select 1; Tahoma, Verdana, Calibri and Consolas 5) the FreeType-derived stub exclusion matches 3,102 and 3,134 of 4,096 GDI monochrome bitmaps. A dropout is a stub when the whole contour's extent beyond the scanline is under one pixel on either side (not the extent of the two crossing edges): in the committed captures FreeType's rule gets 1,102 of 1,290 glyphs with a single dropout sample right and the extent rule raises the whole-bitmap counts to 3,143 and 3,186; in a denser sweep of every ppem from 8 to 48 (generator figures, not committed) it classifies 1,276 of 1,277 isolated dropouts correctly, but glyphs with several dropouts keep some it excludes (94% of events right at two dropouts, 86% at three; 111 of the 116 errors at two are dropouts native kept). Under ClearType the same rule, without its overshoot exemption, is enabled (round 6, "ClearType dropout control"). Enabling the FreeType rule, with its overshoot exemption, in grayscale gains 100 and 297 glyphs of the sample (earlier native state) and loses 35 and 48, including 64 committed Segoe UI bold 12 `2` controls, so it was not shipped in round 5; round 6 ships it without the exemption (Text, "Grayscale stub rule").
- *Pick between the two candidate samples:* the left or bottom one reproduces 3,852 of 4,096 GDI gray bitmaps (SCANTYPE 0), but a thin bar that touches neither sample centre is always drawn on the right or upper one (210 of 210 GDI+ images, `text-raster-bars.json.gz`).

**Native state.** The same probe returned different bytes at different times on one machine, one OS build (10.0.26300.0, UBR 9457) and one `gdiplus.dll` (SHA-256 in the manifests): 2,492 of 31,584 `text-real-glyphs` captures differ between 06:21 UTC and the runs from 07:35 UTC (two later runs and a within-process repeat are byte-identical), and a fresh `text-coverage` capture differs from the committed one (2 October, build 26200) in 606 of 3,328 (`text-native-states.json.gz`, `text-coverage-restate.json.gz`, `src/gdi-native-state.fixture.test.ts`). Changing DPI awareness, thread priority or CPU affinity changes nothing, and the registry font-smoothing values read the same throughout. The differing images move diagonal edges by one sample, and the projection arithmetic tells the states apart: of the 2,128 differing captures of faces available to the tests, per-product rounding reproduces 1,541 of the later images and the combined rounding 775 (1,519 and 766 before the storage 8 and stub rules), while the earlier images match the combined rounding 57 times and per-product never. For the later state `hinting-divergence.json.gz` lists the 2,149 calls the images determine: 2,125 need per-product rounding and 24 combined (generator figure for the earlier state, not committed: 1,013 and 78 of 1,091). A fresh `text-coverage` capture is reproduced by per-product rounding in 350 of its 606 differing captures and by the combined rounding in 128; the committed ones by combined in 94 and per-product in 64.

**Hypotheses rejected, with evidence (31,584-capture sample, earlier native state; exact counts at AntiAliasGridFit/AntiAlias: combined baseline 13,408/9,866, per-product 13,574/10,422).** A condition separating the 78 combined calls among 1,091 determined ones: a depth-3 tree over 40 operand and context features found no split that isolates them (they are spread over every opcode, both hints and every size from 9 to 24 ppem). Truncating, flooring or ceiling the sum or each product, or sign-dependent rounding per term: 203 to 1,539 exact captures fewer than per-product over a grid of seven projection and seven dual-projection variants. Truncating, flooring or ceiling the normalised vectors: 64 to 87 fewer. The `fDotP` definitions (rounded, per-product, truncated, ceiling) and unit movement for equal vectors change nothing; truncated, floored, ceiling and /16384 movement are 907 to 2,110 fewer. Rounding the original distance after scaling, in float, or per product in unit space: 82 to 222 fewer (the production units-space rounding is the best variant). Unrounded x coordinates throughout, the x-precision lead in its crudest form: 2,819 and 4,349 captures lost. Hinting environments for Courier New AntiAlias (compatible widths, v35, v40 without ClearType, unhinted): none exceeds 5 of 1,128 exact, so Courier New remains at 0.

**Closed as not reproducible.** The per-product projection candidate and the "combined versus per-product" call-level question: the native arithmetic of the references differs between captures taken on the same machine, so no condition on the operands exists to find. The candidate stays excluded because it makes 30 committed Arial 16 `y` captures inexact (`ttf-diagonal-projection.fixture.test.ts`); in the latest native state it would raise exact glyphs of the 15,792 per hint from 13,884 to 14,422 (AntiAliasGridFit) and from 10,554 to 11,621 (AntiAlias) and lose 4 and 6 (generator figures). Adopting it needs the references regenerated in one state.

**Open, and what would decide it.** (1) What changes the native state: record the system parameters (SPI font-smoothing values, `FeatureManagement` overrides, Windows flighting) and rerun the real-glyph probe at intervals with other text probes stopped; if a setting flips, the converter can target it. (2) The monochrome stub rule for SCANTYPE 1 and 5 (grayscale and ClearType are closed in round 6) and the pick between the two candidate samples: `text-raster-mono.json.gz` (`st1`, `st5`) holds the sample-exact truth for 4,096 bitmaps each at 8 to 32 ppem; the missing step is a model of glyphs with several dropouts near one tip. (3) Courier New AntiAlias, where every capture is wider than ours (native hinting moves the right stem about a quarter pixel further): captures of a private font reproducing Courier's `fpgm`/`prep` with one instruction removed at a time. (4) The 151 pixels one sample lighter between GDI gray bitmaps and GDI+ images.

### Round 7 (workstream F): rotated GDI text and the grayscale family

- **The references were the wrong side.** A metafile records rounded Dx advances (and the compatible-mode exScale/eyScale), so native playback of `textx-escapement-*`, `textx-world-*`, `textx-rotalign-*`, `textx-eto-*` and `rotate-text-25deg` differs from the directly drawn PNG by 359, 285 to 362, 1,454 / 581 / 1,354, 377 and 23 pixels; the converter, which replays the recorded Dx, was already closer to the playback. `playback-references.json` / `<name>.playback.png` (generator mode `playback-references`; two fresh-process playbacks identical) are now the references. Report residuals against them: escapement 14 (was 371), world-mono 17 (262), world-aa 23 (344), rotalign-esc 34 (1,595 against the direct drawing; 312 against playback before the layout fix), rotalign-world 34 (1,476), rotalign-esc-small 207 (817), eto-mono 11 (386), eto-aa 452 (827), rotate-text-25deg 1 (24). Rotated text is natively non-antialiased: `-aa` and `-mono` escapement PNGs are byte-identical.
- **Layout of a diagonal baseline** (`text-rotated-placement`, 310 configurations, 4,340 glyphs, per-glyph shape matching): a directly drawn run advances by the unrounded linear advance (`advance * ppem / upem`, not the hinted or rounded width) projected in 28.4 fixed point; glyphs mis-placed by a pixel fall from 1,113 to 50 of 4,340 (WMF playback, which carries no Dx, `textx-wmf-escapement-mono` 435 to 17 pixels). Alignment: TA_RIGHT offsets by the total advance vector rounded to 1/16, TA_CENTER by that vector halved with truncation toward zero (`-trunc(round(16 u T) / 2) / 16`), the glyph's accumulated advance and the ascent/descent offset are projected separately and added (with Dx: 0 of 4,200 glyphs mis-placed in every TA_* combination, 64/64 first-glyph origins for centre). Axis angles (90/180/270) keep the integer widths.
- **Rotated glyph pixels (open in round 7, closed for most in round 8).** 20% of rotated glyphs (880 of 4,340) differed from the native bitmap by one to three pixels, and our hinted-then-rotated points equalled the native GGO_NATIVE points in 53%. The cause is how GDI scales a rotated glyph, not the interpreter arithmetic: see "Round 8 (workstream R3)" below.
- **Grayscale (AA) family, reproducible.** Fresh playbacks of all nine AA sheets equal the committed captures pixel for pixel in two separate processes, so `textx-arial-aa` 714, `-timesnewroman-aa`/`-tahoma-aa` 441, `-couriernew-aa` 186, `-segoeui-aa` 148, `-arial-styles-aa` 178, `-wmf-arial-aa` 423, `-opaque-aa` 264, `-color-aa` 966 are real converter differences in this environment, not capture noise. `textx-color-aa` differs by at most 35 levels, faintly over the whole of coloured text on the striped background: the gamma blend of non-black text colours, not glyph shapes; `-eto-aa` 452 against 11 for the identical mono sheet is a grayscale coverage question. Neither was resolved.
- **Grayscale (AA) blend, closed (round 7, F2).** `GrayBlendProbe.cs` (`generate-gray-blend.ps1`, `gray-blend.json.gz`, manifest `environment-gray-blend.json`, `src/gdi-gray-blend.ts`, `src/gdi-gray-blend.fixture.test.ts`) draws one 40 px Arial glyph sheet with `TextOut` / `ExtTextOut` (ANTIALIASED_QUALITY, 32-bit DIB) over grey and random text and background colours. A pixel's coverage level `k` (0 to 16) is its grey in the black-on-white sheet; with it the output is an exact function of `(k, text, backdrop)` per channel: 0 mismatches over all 14 x 65,280 grey pairs per direction (full 256 x 256 sweep, `GRAY_GRID=full`) and 20 million random channel samples. **Transparent text** is `d + floor((s - d) * a)` where `a` depends on `k` and on which of text and backdrop is lighter in that channel (two 14-entry tables, close to `(k / 16) ** 0.4292` for the lighter colour's share but not a power law: the p fitted per level varies from 0.4290 to 0.4294, so they are measured midpoints of the interval each is pinned to).
