The old `0.43` gamma blend was exact only for black on white. **Text drawn with its background fill** (OPAQUE mode, or `ETO_OPAQUE`) goes through an 8-bit linear domain instead: `ENC[floor((k * DEC[s] + (16 - k) * DEC[d]) / 16)]` with `DEC[c] = round(255 * (c / 255) ** 2.3535)` (it collapses the darkest 18 levels, so even text of the background colour changes the pixel) and a measured 256-entry `ENC`; 0 mismatches over 14 x 65,536 grey pairs and all 93,600 random channel samples in each of the OPAQUE and `ETO_OPAQUE` modes. `ETO_OPAQUE` takes the opaque blend only when its rectangle contains the text cell and every glyph bitmap, measured by sweeping each rectangle edge across the cell (`left <=` the leftmost glyph bitmap, e.g. one pixel left of the origin for `A`; `right >=` the rightmost, including italic overhang; `top` and `bottom` the cell): a smaller rectangle is filled and the text blended transparent over it and over the surface beyond it. `ETO_CLIPPED` alone is transparent; OPAQUE mode clipped to a rectangle is opaque inside and leaves the pixels outside. Report (against the native playback references): `textx-color-aa` 966 to 28, `-eto-aa` 452 to 78, `-opaque-aa` 264 to 1; the rest of the AA family is glyph coverage and unchanged.
- **Grayscale glyph coverage, localised to hinting (round 7, F2).** `GrayGlyphProbe.cs` (`generate-gray-glyphs.ps1`, `gdi-gray-glyphs.json.gz`, `src/gdi-gray-glyphs.fixture.test.ts`) captures `GetGlyphOutline` `GGO_GRAY4_BITMAP` and `GGO_NATIVE` (hinted outline) for every printable ASCII glyph of five faces at 18, 24, 32, 48 and 72 px; the capture is byte-identical across processes. (1) `gasp`: `ExtTextOut` draws the font monochrome below its first `GASP_DOGRAY` range (Arial 9 to 17, Tahoma 9 to 16, Times New Roman 9 to 17, Courier New 9 to 36 px), which the converter already does; `GetGlyphOutline` ignores the table and returns gray there, so those sizes are not comparable and are skipped. (2) Where both are gray, 45 to 79 of 94 converter bitmaps are exact (Segoe UI 56 to 90) and the differences are one 1/16 sample on edges. (3) Rasterizing GDI's own `GGO_NATIVE` outline with the converter's grayscale rasterizer reproduces the native bitmap for 90 to 94 of 94 glyphs at 18 to 32 px (Arial 64 of 94 at 72 px; a scratch rasterizer taking exact quadratic crossings instead of subdividing was no better: 92, 75 and 67 of 94 at 18, 48 and 72 px against 91, 78 and 64), so scan conversion is not the main source; the hinted outline is: ours equals the native hinted outline in about 72 of 94 glyphs for Arial, Times New Roman, Tahoma and Courier New at every size (1 to 3 points of 1/64 pixel off on diagonal strokes: `MDRP`/`MIRP`/`SHPIX` after `SDPVTL`, then `IUP`, e.g. Arial 24 `X` points 3, 9, 13, 19 and `V` points 0, 9, 10). Scored against this oracle (2,914 glyphs, 84,008 points, 3,199 points off) and none improved it: truncating, flooring or ceiling the move `mulDiv`, the dot product, the vector normalisation (also an integer square root), the point scaling and the CVT scaling; `SPVTL` or `SDPVTL` from original instead of current points; `MDRP` distance from original instead of unscaled points. Segoe UI's `GGO_NATIVE` outlines are not the outlines its bitmaps come from (16 of 94 equal), so only its bitmaps are pinned. A hinted-outline oracle for the next attempt: `outline-diff` style comparisons of `font.outline(glyph)` against the parsed `GGO_NATIVE` points, with the interpreter traced to the first point that differs.
- **Diagonal hinting arithmetic, closed for GDI (round 7, T).** Private fonts whose glyphs run one instruction on controlled points (`generate-hint-sweep.ps1`, `src/ttf-hint-sweep.fixture.test.ts`; 61 kinds x 250 vectors x 4 sizes when built in full, 3.1 million points, all read through `GGO_NATIVE`, identical across processes) recovered the per-instruction rules: the projection and dual projection round each product alone; the move divisor is the per-product dot, and a dot below 1/16 keeps its sign (+-1.0); a move along x truncates after adding half the divisor (negative quotients round toward zero), a move along y rounds half away from zero, a freedom vector copied from the projection vector (or a dot of exactly 1.0) rounds to nearest on both axes, and with the projection vector along x the x move is the distance itself; IUP interpolates with the x-style division; an axis shortcut for the dot product must not skip the other component. The mono-text workstream found the same rules independently (glyph programs truncated after k instructions); they are `gdiArithmetic`. Against the 94-glyph `GGO_NATIVE` set the hinted outline is now identical to GDI's for 93 to 94 of 94 glyphs of Arial, Times New Roman, Tahoma and Courier New at 18 to 72 px (1,591 of 1,598 compared glyphs and 10 points off, from 1,238 and 1,666 off), and 140 of 3.1 million sweep points differ. `GetGlyphOutline` ignores `lfQuality` (identical bytes at quality 5), so ClearType and GDI+ hinting cannot be probed this way. Switching GDI (not GDI+) ClearType text to the new arithmetic moves `textx-arial-cleartype` 22,224 -> 22,200 px, `textx-color-cleartype` 1,761 -> 1,758 and `textx-segoeui-cleartype` 26,464 -> 26,462 with no exact capture lost (shipped); GDI+ gray and ClearType lose 2 to 53 px per fixture and stay on the old arithmetic (the native state of those images differs between captures, see Native state above); rotated glyph runs do not change at all (the fonts switch most hinting off). Left over: the 140 sweep points (ties of the half-divisor rule), random-line ISECT, first divergences in ALIGNRP and CALL.

### Round 8 (workstream R3): rotated GDI glyphs, metrics and advances

Private fonts whose points sit 468 em from the origin (`RotationMatrixProbe.cs`, `build-rotation-matrix-font.py`, `generate-rotation-matrix.ps1`; `GetGlyphOutline(GGO_NATIVE)` under a world rotation and under an escapement, 5-degree steps) show the matrix to a relative 1e-6, where an ordinary glyph pins it to 1e-3. Fixture residuals are against the native playback references; "before" is the round 7 state.

- **How GDI scales a rotated glyph.** `head.flags` bit 3 ("force ppem to integer values", set in every stock font here) is what matters, not the font's programs (a font with an empty `prep`, `fpgm` and glyph program behaves like one without): without the bit the outline is the exact rotation of the font-size outline. With it the glyph is hinted **unrotated at the whole-pixel ppem `round(ppem * max(|cos|, |sin|))`** and the hinted 26.6 points are then multiplied by the rotation divided by its major component (1 on the major diagonal; on the minor ones `A / B`, `A` and `B` being `min` and `max` of `|cos|`, `|sin|` times `32 * ppem` rounded, the 16.16 em scale of a 2048-unit em; `rotationMatrix` in `src/gdi-font-engine.ts`). The round 7 model hinted at the font ppem and scaled by the rounded ratio (`roundedRotationMatrix`, still used for matrices that are not rotations). Hinting at the rounded ppem with `gdiArithmetic` reproduces **every point of the straight-edged glyphs (T, E, 4, 7, 1) of Arial, Times New Roman and Tahoma** in `rotated-glyph-bitmaps-small.json.gz` (2,709 of 2,709; Tahoma 1,107 of 1,107, which the round 7 note could not explain: its instructions simply ran at the wrong size), and 1,252 of 1,272 compared points (98%) of `rotated-glyph-matrix.json.gz` (2,539 of 5,436, 47%, with the round 7 model; glyphs whose point counts differ are not compared). In the 62-glyph set of the placement probe (4 faces x 6 sizes x 14 angles with an escapement; Arial also with a world rotation) exact glyph bitmaps rise from 3,647 to 4,723 of 5,544 for Arial (differing pixels 6,593 -> 1,176 of 430,000 ink pixels), 3,630 to 4,697 for Times New Roman (6,918 -> 1,325), 1,554 to 4,087 for Tahoma (110,119 -> 2,927) and 3,265 to 4,580 for Courier New (8,788 -> 1,492); the Arial world rows go from 1,177 to 1,626 of 1,848 (1,139 -> 305 pixels).
- **Not exact: the minor ratio and the target at ties.** The minor ratio is `k / 65536` with an integer `k` that is not `round(65536 tan)`: it differs from the tangent by up to 0.2% (8.378 px against 8.393 px for the 20 px, 25 degree case), and `A / B` with `32 * ppem`, as a 16.16 number, reproduces 38,436 of 40,052 far points (96%) and 484 of 1,292 whole rows under a world rotation (`src/gdi-rotation-matrix.fixture.test.ts`; the unrounded quotient gives 38,356 and 456, floor and ceil mixes of `A` and `B` and a 2.14 quotient are worse). The whole-pixel target is rounded half up at 1/32 pixel (12 px at 45 degrees is hinted at 9, not 8: 8.4853 -> 272/32 = 8.5), which is right for 1,268 of 1,292 world sizes (plain rounding: 1,240); the 24 left are native major components a hair above the half (32 px at 40 degrees: 24.513 -> native 24, the model gives 25). An escapement's sine and cosine are less precise than a world matrix's (relative error up to 3e-4; its pairs reproduce 33,620 of the 40,052 points), so under `lfEscapement` the same model is a little worse. An angle table would be needed and was not built.
- **The pixel centre on an edge.** Rasterizing the native GGO_NATIVE outlines, a centre that lies exactly on an edge is in the bitmap when the contour runs downward there (y up) and out when it runs upward, in every sweep direction (12,995 tie centres of the straight-edged glyphs of the 45, 135, 225 and 315 degree sheets: the converter now disagrees at 529, and every pixel it sets on an upward edge that the native bitmap lacks is Tahoma's, whose curves and dropout still differ). The converter's sweep took both ends of a span; `directedTies` in `rasterizeMono` is now on for glyphs at a non-axis angle (not for the 90/180/270 degree glyphs, which keep the upright rule: the Arial world sheet at 180 degrees worsened when it was applied there). Rounding the implied on-curve points to the 26.6 grid (as upright glyphs do) is also right for rotated glyphs: 313 against 298 of 357 Arial glyphs. A GGO_BITMAP row is padded to a DWORD, which for glyphs wider than 32 pixels is more than four bytes; the first measurements of this round misread the -28 and -40 px glyphs because of it.
- **Metrics of a rotated font.** `GetTextMetrics` of a font with an escapement differs from the upright one, and TA_TOP / TA_BOTTOM place rotated text by it (`RotatedMetricsProbe.cs`, 5 faces x lfHeight -8..-60 x 8 angles; world rotation and escapement are identical): at a non-axis angle `floor(headYMax * ppem / upem + ppem / 64 + 0.964)` (and the same for the descent with `-headYMin`; all 265 sizes of five faces at 25 and 160 degrees, 262 of 265 at 45 and 300 degrees where the last digit of the pad moves by 0.005), at a quarter turn the `OS/2` win box with the same pad (`+ 0.969`; all 265 sizes at 90 and at 270 degrees), at a half turn `round(win * ppem / upem)` (all 265); the upright metrics (VDMX) apply only at 0 degrees. The round 7 formula `floor(yMax * ppem / upem + 1.27)` held up to 32 px only (157 of 265 sizes of five faces). `rotatedMetrics` in `RealizedFont`; `textx-rotalign-esc-small` goes from 207 to 13 differing pixels because its 90 degree line at 13 px was placed by the upright ascent.
- **Advances along a quarter-turn baseline.** The linear advance is taken to 26.6 and rounded half up (Times New Roman at 9 px: E 5.4976 -> 6, H 6.4995 -> 7), along a half turn rounded plainly (E -> 5, H -> 6): all 840 widths at 90, 180 and 270 degrees and (rounded the same way) the 3,360 non-axis ones of `rotated-placement*.json.gz`. Glyphs of the directly drawn rotated runs placed by a pixel fall from 50 to 32 of 4,200 and the Dx runs stay at 0; exact glyph shapes (a different measure from the earlier "raster") rise to 3,695 and 3,731 of 4,200.
- **Fixtures** (differing pixels, before -> after): `textx-rotalign-esc-small` 207 -> 13, `textx-rotalign-esc` 34 -> 15, `textx-rotalign-world` 34 -> 15, `textx-world-aa` 13 -> 3, `textx-world-mono` 12 -> 2, `textx-wmf-escapement-mono` 17 -> 10, `rotate-text-25deg` against the native playback 1 -> 0 (exact), `textx-escapement-aa` / `-mono` 14 -> 15 (single pixels; the ceiling of 0.01% still holds). Exact fixtures in the GDI report 777 -> 778 of 898. Test ceilings in `src/gdi-parity.fixture.test.ts` are tightened to the new figures.
- **Open.** Rotated curved glyphs still differ by single pixels in about 15% of the glyphs (Arial 4,723 of 5,544 exact): the implied points and the minor ratio above; Tahoma's curves and dropout (its prep selects the smart dropout types 4 and 5; switching dropout control off for such glyphs fixes the straight-edged ones in a 13 to 20 px set but costs 266 Tahoma glyphs in the 62-glyph set, so it was not shipped). The upright mono and grayscale residuals of the workstream list (`textx-*-aa`, `-mono`, `-cell-mono`, `textx-fon-msshelldlg`, `text-msshelldlg-hm13`, `emfrec-text-exttextouta`; 1 to 93 px each) are scattered single-pixel hinting and dropout decisions and were not touched. Grayscale at 72 px: rasterizing GDI's own outline gives 64 of 94 Arial glyphs; the differences are +-1 sample (1/16) on curves and straight diagonals ('^' loses one sample on every edge pixel), shifting the outline by 0.05 of a 26.6 unit (1/1280 pixel) in either axis loses 181 to 262 of 538 exact glyphs of seven face/size sets (so the samples on edges are nearly all ties and the current both-ends rule is right for most), and the direction rule that fixes rotated mono ties (`directedTies` on the 4x4 grid) loses 10 of 94 Arial glyphs at 24 px and 4 at 48 px, so the grayscale gap is not this tie rule.

### Round 9 (workstream W3): upright GDI monochrome and grayscale text, the escapement table

**Sources, kept apart.** *Documented by Microsoft:* the TrueType instruction set (https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/typography/opentype/spec/tt_instructions, SCANCTRL and SCANTYPE) lists six scan-conversion rules. Rule 1: a pixel whose centre is inside the outline is on. Rule 2: a pixel whose centre a contour passes exactly through is on. Rule 3 (SCANTYPE 0, simple): when a scan line between two adjacent pixel centres is crossed by an on-transition and an off-transition contour and neither pixel was turned on by rules 1 and 2, the left-most (horizontal line) or bottom-most (vertical line) pixel is turned on. Rule 4 (SCANTYPE 1): apply rule 3 only if the two contours continue to intersect other scan lines in both directions, which is judged on "the scanline segments that form a square with the intersected scan line segment"; "these could be different contours than the ones intersecting the dropout scan line segment". Rule 5 (SCANTYPE 4): turn on the pixel closer to the midpoint of the two contours. Rule 6 (SCANTYPE 5): rule 5 with rule 4's stub test. SCANCTRL bits 0 to 7 are a ppem threshold and bits 8 to 13 the conditions (ppem, rotated, stretched) that switch dropout control on or off. `GetGlyphOutline` documents GGO_GRAYn_BITMAP as n squared plus one levels (5, 17, 65), that is an n x n oversampling (https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/wingdi/nf-wingdi-getglyphoutlinew). ISECT (same page) puts a point at the intersection of two lines and, for parallel lines, at `(a0 + a1) / 4 + (b0 + b1) / 4` on each axis; no rounding is stated. *Not documented:* how lfEscapement becomes a matrix, how a pixel centre exactly on a horizontal edge is treated, and how the intersection of non-parallel lines is rounded. *Inferred from re-implementations (behaviour only, no code read or taken):* FreeType's black rasterizer, which this converter's sweep follows, decides a stub from its own profile structure (a tip that ends before the next scan line, with an "overshoot" exemption); Wine's `freetype.c` and ReactOS' font engine call FreeType's rasterizer and add no Microsoft scan converter, so they could not decide any of the questions below. *Measured here:* everything that follows.

**Where the 140 mismatching sheet glyphs came from.** `mono-sheet-glyphs.json.gz` holds the native bitmap and hinted outline of 6,740 glyphs. Comparing our hinted outline with the native one separates hinting from scan conversion: 123 of the 140 glyphs that differed had the native outline point for point (all of them scan conversion), 3 differed in the outline, 14 are italic or bold (not compared). Most of the 123 were one dropout pixel that FreeType's profile rule kept or dropped differently from the native scan converter: on curves (the profile rule had never been calibrated on them) and on glyphs of several contours (`i`, `j`, `%`, accents) whose contours lie within a pixel of each other.

**Stub rule by the specification squares (this branch only: it gives up 20 controls that were exact).** Reading rule 4 literally (a dropout is kept only if the unit square on each side of the scan segment is crossed at least once, by any contour, on its far scan segment or on the two segments that join it to the dropout's pixel centres; a contour that only touches a segment counts twice; curves flattened at 16 steps plus their extrema on the 26.6 grid) reproduces the native decision in 9,061 of 9,070 isolated decisions of the private polygon fonts (flipping one decision at a time in 62k native `GGO_BITMAP` masks and keeping the glyphs where exactly one flip changes the bitmap; the nine others that were inspected touch a pixel-centre line within 0.004 pixel: a curve apex, a vertex on the scan line). The same rule decides the grayscale oversampled outline, because GDI's gray bitmaps are the oversampled monochrome ones. Measured against the round 8 code:

| native data | exact before | exact with the rule | lost |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6,740 real-font mono sheet glyphs (`mono-sheet-glyphs`) | 6,600 (175 differing samples) | 6,718 (28) | 0 |
| 2,068 native gray sheet bitmaps (`gdi-gray-glyphs`, five faces, 18 to 72 px) | 1,845 | 1,857 | 1 (Segoe UI 48 px `&`) |
| 31,584 real GDI+ gray glyphs (`text-real-glyphs`) | 27,193 | 27,288 | 0 |
| 61,952 private mono controls (`mono-stubs*`, `mono-multiple*`, SCANTYPE 1/4/5) | 51,971 (14,391 differing samples) | 61,465 (743) | 18 |
| 56,320 private gray bitmaps and GDI+ images (`text-raster-*`) | 38,753 | 43,309 | 2 (one glyph: st5, 40 ppem, #99, a tip within a sample) |
| 3,720 GDI+ ClearType size-sheet captures | 3,076 | 3,079 | 0 |
| `textx-segoeui-cell-mono` / `-tahoma-cell-mono` / `-timesnewroman-cell-mono` (px) | 40 / 18 / 24 | 1 / 2 / 4 | |

The 18 and 2 losses are controls that the profile rule happened to get right where a contour passes within about 0.004 pixel of a pixel centre or square corner (the edge of a thin sliver at `(-0.875,-1.0)`, a curve apex at `-1.9988` against the centre line `-2`); varying the tie convention (touch counted once or twice, 1/64 pixel tolerance, rounding the flattened points to 26.6, FreeType's overshoot exemption, merging overlapping spans before the smart pick) lost between 22 and 6,500 previously exact controls for the same or fewer gains (61,465 exact is the best of them). The rule that ships in the converter's ClearType scan conversion (0 losses of 3,720 and 1,976) is this one. The branch `worktree-agent-a5c6aaaa56009cbeb` keeps the round 8 rule for monochrome and grayscale text because the committed controls forbid even one loss; this branch (`worktree-agent-a5c6aaaa56009cbeb-squares`, one commit on top of it) applies the rule to them too, with a finer arc split for GDI monochrome (`fineArcs`: GDI evaluates curved edges almost exactly; 1,024 native ovals 921 to 933 exact). Its evidence is `ttf-mono-squares.fixture.test.ts` (per-case ceilings from `compare-mono-squares.ts` against the frozen round 8 renderer in `mono-squares-bounds.json.gz`, which replaces `ttf-mono-stub` and `ttf-mono-multiple`), and the 18 controls it gives up are pinned there by name; the gray one is st5 at 40 ppem, #99. Fixtures: 803 to 810 of 910 exact in `report.ts`, none worse (`emfrec-text-exttextouta` 7 to 2, `textx-segoeui-cell-mono` 40 to 1, `textx-tahoma-cell-mono` 18 to 2, `textx-wmf-segoeui-mono` 17 to 1, `textpb-segoeui-cell-q3` against the direct drawing 45 to 0 px).

**A scan line exactly on a horizontal edge (shipped, gray).** A sample centre on a contour is on it (rule 2). The sweep took one side of two descending edges that a horizontal edge joins (FreeType merges the shared row), so a Segoe UI capital's bar, whose underside lies on a sample centre of the 4x grid (cap height 23 px, bar 20.625 to 23 px), lost a sample row that the native bitmap has. `closedTies` inks such a row with the union of the spans just above and just below it. Native 4x4 gray bitmaps of five faces at 18 to 72 px: 1,845 to 1,857 of 2,068 exact (Segoe UI 12 more, none lost); GDI+ AntiAlias and AntiAliasGridFit images of 31,584 real glyphs (nine faces): 27,193 to 27,204 (none lost); `textx-segoeui-aa` 93 to 64 px, `textx-tahoma-aa` 50 to 47, `textx-couriernew-aa` 25 to 24. Not for monochrome or ClearType: the private diagonal GDI+ SingleBitPerPixel and ClearType controls (`text-diagonal-coverage`, `text-signed-diagonal-coverage`) leave a top edge's row out, and the ClearType gain is only two captures.

**lfEscapement is a chord (shipped).** `EscapementAngleProbe.cs` reads `GetGlyphOutline(GGO_NATIVE)` of a private font whose points lie 30,000 pixels out at every tenth of a degree (3,600 angles; `escapement-angles.json.gz`). A world rotation is exact to the 16.16 matrix, an escapement is not: its length dips by up to 3.2e-4, with the dips repeating every 2.8125 degrees. The matrix is the linear interpolation of the exact sine and cosine at 128 equal steps of a turn, which reproduces all 3,600 angles to 2e-5 (exact trigonometry is off by 3.1e-4). `escapementSinCos` rotates the glyph outlines; the baseline direction of the layout stays exact (the table in the layout moved `textx-rotalign-esc` from 15 to 61 px). Under an escapement the model of "How GDI scales a rotated glyph" now reproduces 38,204 of 40,052 far points (33,620 with exact trigonometry) and 476 of 1,292 whole rows (452). The fixtures do not move (13, 15, 14 and 9 px on the escapement sheets); what is left there is the minor ratio and curve ties.

**ISECT, negative.** The 960 random-line controls of `freedom-isect` stay at 431 exact. Tried: the rational intersection rounded to nearest on every line (435; floor, truncation or ceiling 195 to 197), the parallel formula of the specification (matches the one pair that is parallel to 0.03 degrees; native does not use it for lines 0.5 to 2 degrees apart), a t quantised to 2^12 to 2^20 (425 to 436), directions normalised to 2.14 (431 to 439), FreeType's formula with the products scaled by 1 to 4,096 (45 to 442, best 16), the intersection of the font-unit points scaled afterwards (310). The error is a rounding-sized one for well-conditioned lines (492 of 508 within one unit between 45 and 90 degrees) and grows as 1 / sin(angle) (the native point is 275 units from the rational one for lines 2.2 degrees apart), which says that native keeps its input points at a precision this interpreter does not; not reproduced.

**Left open, and why.** (1) The 22 mono sheet glyphs that the candidate rule leaves: a curve whose crossing lies within about 0.002 pixel of a sample centre, which depends on the native arc subdivision (replacing the exact arcs by integer-midpoint subdivision to fixed depth or tolerance leaves at best 577 of the 1,024 ovals exact against 933); (2) gray pixels one sample off at 72 px (about 3 per glyph; the native outline rasterized with ours is exact in 64 to 75 of 94 glyphs): the same arc ties; (3) Segoe UI's gray bitmaps, whose `GGO_NATIVE` outline is not the outline the bitmap comes from; (4) the escapement layout and rotated minor ratio; (5) ISECT. None of these is a scan-conversion rule that a native probe on this machine could still separate from tie noise.

## Tooling

- **Regenerating existing groups:** running existing fixture groups on the Windows runner can change their PNGs, because the runner's OS, fonts and DPI can differ. Compare before replacing committed fixtures.

Every new generator run writes an `environment-<groups>.json` manifest associating reference hashes with Windows/native-library versions, screen DPI and installed font hashes. This makes subsequent environment changes detectable; it cannot recover the environment of earlier captures.
